Original cone and an exact full matrix formula\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For independent formal vectors\n(x_0,g_0,...,g_n), define x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. All distinct ordered\noriginal interpolation rows remain in the model:\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLet lambda_ij be their nonnegative multipliers. Values cancel in\n\n M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij*Q_ij               (2)\n\nexactly when out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) at every finite i. A valid upper\ncertificate requires PSD on the ENTIRE original formal space, as specified\nin fact1252. In the tested family lambda_(star,i)=0. Write\nnu_i=lambda_(i,star), and z_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. Once flow cancels values,\nthe complete quadratic, with every original gradient retained, is\n\n M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<g_i,x_0>\n      +sum_i nu_i[-<g_i,z_i>+||g_i||^2/2]\n      +sum_(i!=j finite)lambda_ij\n           [<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].                 (3)\n\nThus its full matrix is [[q,-nu^T/2],[-nu/2,C]], where C acts on\nall n+1 finite gradient coordinates. If h_n=0, its entries are\n\n C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(j>i)lambda_ij,\n 2C_ij=-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)lambda_iv\n          +h_i*(nu_j+sum_(v<=i)lambda_jv), i<j.             (4)\n\nThe sums in (4) are over finite labels; out_i includes nu_i. To verify\nthe diagonal, the squared gradient differences contribute half the incident\nmass, which is out_i-1_(i=n) by flow. A forward position term based at i\ncontributes -h_i*lambda_ij. For i<j, squared differences contribute\n-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji) to the cross coefficient. Position terms based at i\nwith destination after j contribute -h_j times their multipliers; those\nbased at j with destination at or before i, including the optimum,\ncontribute h_i times their multipliers. This proves (3)-(4) directly.\n\nZeros assigned below are restrictions on this candidate's coefficients,\nnot assertions that any original interpolation row is redundant.\n\n## 3. One asymmetric transport; exactly one affine repair\n\nAll quantities introduced in this section are local to the displayed join;\nin particular its gradient matrix C is distinct from the benchmark C_n.\nConsider a parent (H_j,d,H_j), where k=2^j-1, j>=1, and d=1+a*c^j.\nLet the child steps be u_0,...,u_(k-1), with\n\n p_i=sum_(v<i)u_v, 0<=i<=k, A=p_k>0,\n t=A+d, S=2A+d, D=1+2S, D_child=1+2A.\n\nLeft finite labels are L_l=l, 0<=l<=k; L_k is the physical bridge\nquery. Right finite labels are R_i=k+1+i, 0<=i<=k. The actual parent\nterminal is R_k=2k+1. Their chronological masses are p_l and t+p_i.\n\nTake ANY nonnegative reference finite array K_ij (i!=j, 0<=i,j<=k), K_ii=0,\nand nonnegative optimum attachment vector mu_i satisfying\n\n sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+mu_i=2*1_(i=k).                    (5)\n\nSumming (5) gives sum_i mu_i=2. No PSD assumption, objective bound,\nrank, or child oracle is used in the obstruction. Incoming child-terminal\ncoefficients in K may be present. In particular the ensuing argument is\nstronger than one limited to already certified children.\n\nSet\n\n theta=(3A+d)/(2S), s=1-theta=(A+d)/(2S).                 (6)\n\nThe right finite block is an EXACT copy and its optimum attachments have\nthe separate scale theta:\n\n lambda_(R_i,R_v)=K_iv, lambda_(R_i,star)=theta*mu_i.     (7)\n\nThis exact-copy condition and (6) are new candidate restrictions, not\nconclusions drawn from the floating-point fits.\n\nThe base rule is kappa=0. Its ONLY repair is\n\n                         kappa=s/(2d).                  (8)\n\nFor either choice, define the complete backward transport by\n\n lambda_(R_i,L_0)=mu_i*[s-kappa*d*(A-p_i)/A],\n lambda_(R_i,L_k)=mu_i*kappa*t*(A-p_i)/A,\n lambda_(R_i,L_l)=0 for 0<l<k.                           (9)\n\nRetain the forward direction, with arbitrary nonnegative multipliers\nlambda_(L_l,R_i) whose column totals are\n\n F_i=sum_l lambda_(L_l,R_i)=kappa*mu_i*(A-p_i).           (10)\n\nBoth directions are therefore explicitly present in the repaired rule.\nIf mu_i>0 and i<k, F_i>0; the repaired backward coefficient to L_0 is\nstrictly positive too. At the original terminal, p_k=A, so F_k=0,\nlambda_(R_k,L_k)=0 and lambda_(R_k,L_0)=s*mu_k.\n\nALL left finite rows lambda_(L_l,L_v), and ALL left optimum attachments\nnu_(L_l), may be chosen independently of any scalar child-copy law,\nwith arbitrary dependence on position, m and q. The obstruction grants\nevery nonnegative such choice for which full original flow holds. This\nincludes adding nonzero incoming edges at the bridge query L_k. There\nis no assumption that it remains an unqueried child terminal.\n\nHere is an explicit full nonnegative flow completion, showing that these\nprescribed rows are not merely a hypothetical feasible-set description.\nPut\n\n E=sum_i mu_i*(A-p_i), 0<=E<=2A.\n\nSend every forward F_i from L_k; set all left finite pairs to zero; set\n\n nu_(L_0)=2s-kappa*d*E/A, nu_(L_k)=kappa*d*E/A,\n nu_(L_l)=0 for 0<l<k.                                  (11)\n\nEvery optimum-outgoing coefficient is zero. Together (7),(9)-(11) specify\nevery original multiplier. The theorem below also covers all the more\ngeneral left corrections and forward distributions just described.\n\n### Exact signs and value cancellation\n\nThe inequalities 0<theta<1, s>0, d>0 and 0<=p_i<=A imply all the claimed\nsigns. In the repaired rule,\n\n s-kappa*d*(A-p_i)/A=s*(A+p_i)/(2A)>=s/2.\n\nIn (11), 0<=kappa*d*E/A<=s, so nu_(L_0)>=s>0 and nu_(L_k)>=0.\nThe base has nu_(L_0)=2s, nu_(L_k)=0. All other coefficients are\nnonnegative multiples of mu_i or entries of K, or are explicitly zero.\n\nThe backward outgoing total at R_i is\n\n B_i=mu_i*[s+kappa*(A-p_i)]=s*mu_i+F_i.                 (12)\n\nBy (5), the right value flow is\n(2*1_(i=k)-mu_i)+theta*mu_i+B_i-F_i=2*1_(i=k).\nAt L_0, the incoming backward sum equals the first quantity in (11).\nAt L_k, incoming backward minus outgoing forward is\nkappa*(t/A-1)*E=kappa*d*E/A, equal to its optimum attachment.\nAll other left flows vanish in the explicit completion. Thus every\nfinite f_i cancels in (2), including f_n; the total optimum incoming\nmass is 2. No interpolation row or value variable is silently omitted.\n\n### Why these coefficients were tested\n\nThe base routes the right terminal's backward correction to the actual\ninitial left sample. If an input child has exact Huber contact and mu_k>0,\nits terminal row equation is sum_v K_kv*(A-p_v)=A*mu_k.\nFor the parent, that row at exact Huber contact would require\nS*theta*mu_k=A*mu_k+S*s*mu_k. Solving gives (6). Thus theta is derived\nfrom this chosen terminal attachment in the original identity. It is\nnot a fitted numerical constant.\n\nThe base's right-node flow permits no forward mass. The sole repair\nallows such mass to increase linearly with the remaining right mass\nA-p_i and distributes backward mass between the original endpoints\nL_0,L_k. Formula (9) has the required mass (12) and first moment\nkappa*t*(A-p_i), which interpolates the two endpoint contact conditions.\nIts interior contact defect is calculated explicitly in Section 9.\nThe largest kappa allowed by the displayed L_0 sign is s/d; (8) uses\nhalf of this exact cap to leave that multiplier strictly positive.\nThis is one analytic repair, not a scan over coefficient choices.\n\nThe initial-point terminal destination is a substantive restriction of\nthe tested law. It need not match the numerical optimizers. In particular\nthis draft does not claim that all asymmetric recursions must use (6).\n\n## 4. The first full-space PSD obstruction\n\nEvaluate the entire original quadratic on the following original-coordinate\nlinear subspace, in one real dimension:\n\n g_(L_l)=0 for every 0<=l<=k,\n g_(R_i)=v for every 0<=i<=k,\n x_0=x.                                                   (14)\n\nThe terminal gradient remains present and equals v in this TEST subspace;\nno equality constraint has been imposed on the certificate. A PSD matrix\nmust remain nonnegative on every such subspace. The original substitutions\ngive x_(L_l)=x and x_(R_i)=x-p_i*v, including the terminal.\nThese formal test vectors are not asserted to satisfy interpolation or to\nbe an actual convex-function trajectory; they test the full dual matrix.\n\nEvery left finite quadratic and left optimum quadratic vanishes on (14),\nirrespective of how large or position-dependent its coefficient is. The\nright finite block contributes\n\n sum_(i,l)K_il*(p_i-p_l)*v^2\n       =[2A-sum_i mu_i*p_i]*v^2,                         (15)\n\nwhere (15) is just (5) multiplied by p_i and summed, not a use of\nchild PSD or an independent-gradient assumption about actual iterates.\nThe right optimum rows contribute\n-2theta*x*v+theta*sum_i mu_i*(p_i+1/2)*v^2.\nThe backward rows contribute sum_i B_i*(p_i+1/2)*v^2;\nthe forward rows contribute sum_i F_i*v^2/2. Inserting (12) proves\nthe exact whole-parent identity\n\n M(q)|_(14)=q*x^2-2theta*x*v+Gamma*v^2,\n Gamma=1+2A+kappa*sum_i mu_i*(A-p_i)*(1+p_i).             (16)\n\nIn particular (16) is linked explicitly to ALL original rows and to the\noriginal (n+2)-coordinate matrix (3)-(4). It is not an autonomous bridge\ninequality, a relaxed child's bound, or a virtual matrix of lower order.\n\nFor q>0, set v=1 and x=theta/q. This is an explicit full-space direction,\nwhose quadratic value is\n\n                         Gamma-theta^2/q.              (17)\n\nIf q<0, x alone already gives a negative direction. If q=0, the nonzero\ncoefficient -2theta*x*v in (16) does. Hence any PSD level in this law\nis positive and necessarily satisfies q>=theta^2/Gamma.\n\n## 6. Why an affine transport also loses exact Huber contact\n\nThis additional identity diagnoses the repair, without being a premise\nof Sections 5-8. Suppose the reference child really has Huber contact\nand mu_k>0. A Huber-tight full parent must also have contact. Indeed\ntake Hub(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and Hub(x)=|x|-1/2 otherwise, and its\nactual trajectory from x_0=D. Every x_i=D-P_i>=S+1>1, so all finite\ng_i=1 and f_i=x_i-1/2. Its terminal numerator 2f_n is D and its initial\nsquare is D^2;\nevery finite Q_ij and every Q_(i,star) is zero, and\nQ_(star,i)=1-x_i<0. Nonnegative multipliers and PSD at q=1/D force\noptimum-outgoing multipliers to vanish and M(D,1,...,1)=0. A PSD\nquadratic with zero value at a vector annihilates that vector: apply\nit to that vector plus an arbitrary scalar multiple of another vector.\n\nWriting P_i for original chronological mass, expansion of (3) gives\nthe finite-coordinate contact equations\n\n (2S-P_i)*nu_i=2h_i+sum_(v finite)lambda_iv*(P_i-P_v).  (27)\n\nMore explicitly, if e is the all-ones gradient vector, differentiating\n(3) at equal gradients gives\n2(Ce)_i=2h_i+(1+P_i)*nu_i+sum_v lambda_iv*(P_i-P_v).\nIn the position terms, full finite value flow makes the sum of the\nposition-derivative contributions 2h_i; the remaining derivative based\nat i is its displayed outgoing drift. The norm terms contribute nu_i.\nThe gradient part of M(D,e)=0 is 2Ce=D*nu, which yields (27).\n\nFor the right finite copy (7), the child version of (27) subtracts out.\nLet backward coefficients more generally have the form\nlambda_(R_i,L_l)=mu_i*(b_l+c_l*p_i), with every actual coefficient\nnonnegative. Permit arbitrary nonnegative forward coefficients, and\nrequire their terminal column to be zero. Put\nB0=sum b_l, B1=sum c_l, M0=sum p_l*b_l, M1=sum p_l*c_l.\nRight flow gives F_i/mu_i=B0+B1*p_i-s at every mu_i>0.\nThe right equations (27) require\n\n (t+p_i)*(B0+B1*p_i)-(M0+M1*p_i)\n       =theta*t-s*(2A-p_i).                            (28)\n\nIf there are at least three distinct positions with mu_i>0, equality\nof the two degree-at-most-two polynomials at those positions forces\nB1=0. At the terminal, zero forward inflow and mu_k>0 then give\nB0=s. Thus every F_i=0. At mu_i=0, flow and the backward formula\nalso force F_i=0. Nonnegativity makes EVERY forward coefficient zero.\n\nConsequently an affine backward source profile cannot retain genuine\nforward mass while simultaneously preserving this exact finite copy,\na single right attachment scale, and Huber contact. This is scoped to\nthe stated law; it does not follow from the approximate numerical fits.\n\nFor the explicit repair (9), the backward first moment is\nkappa*t*(A-p_i). At the endpoints p_i=0,A the intended right equation\nis matched. At an interior position its drift exceeds the required drift\nby exactly kappa*mu_i*p_i*(A-p_i). This provides the concrete interior\ncontact defect. The result of Sections 5-8 is stronger for the selected\ntheta,kappa: raising q within a small whole-word budget cannot repair\nthe full PSD failure, even if the contact requirement is abandoned.\n\n## 10. Circular envelope and every local physical step\n\nFor 0<beta<=1/4 set\n\n    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,\n    v(q)=(p(q),-q), K=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).\n\nThe disk of center(beta,beta), radius1+beta contains0 because\n2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. Its radial vector(p(q)-beta,-q-beta) supports\nK at v(q). The endpoints are(C_beta,0),(beta,-1), and the horizontal\nsegment from0 to(C_beta,0) is in K. Hence\n\n    0<=p<=C_beta, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta q,\n    (p,0) in K whenever(p,-q) in K.                              (3.1)\n\nFor compact convex C containing0 define\nE_C(r)=max_(g in C){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}. Completing the square gives\nthe unique maximizer Pi_C(r), the Euclidean projection. Its inequalities\nat r,s give ||Pi_C(r)-Pi_C(s)||^2<=<Pi_C(r)-Pi_C(s),r-s>.\nComparing maximization formulas at r,r+d bounds the increment after\nsubtracting <Pi_C(r),d> by ||d||^2. Thus E_C is differentiable, convex,\nnonnegative and1-smooth with gradient Pi_C. Its zero set is the polar\ncone: a positive support value gives a positive objective at a small\npositive multiple of the supporting vector. The cone of K is generated\nby its endpoints, so\n\n    E_(sigma K)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta U-V<=0.                  (3.2)\n\nDefine Hub_delta(t) to be0 for t<=0, t^2/2 for 0<=t<=delta, and\ndelta*t-delta^2/2 for t>=delta; its derivative is clip(t,0,delta).\n\nTake an ordinary block a_1,...,a_N in its physical order, alpha_j=a_j/2.\nConstruct r_j=(u_j,-y_j) backwards from r_N=(beta,-1). For alpha_j>0\nchoose the unique q_(j-1) in[0,1) satisfying\n\n    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)\n       =max{beta/(C_beta-beta),\n                  (y_j+(1-alpha_j)beta)/(u_j-(1-alpha_j)beta)},\n    r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha_j*v(q_(j-1)).                              (3.3)\n\nFor alpha_j=0 keep the state and its projection. The denominator is\npositive because u_j>=beta and alpha_j>0; the left ratio increases\nfrom beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity. We have u_j>=beta,y_j>=1 and\nq_(j-1)<=q_j: at an interior current point the ratio is\n(y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta), and subtracting alpha_j beta from its numerator\nwhile adding it to its denominator can only decrease it. At q_j=1\nthe upper ratio is infinite. At q_j=0 the normal-cone condition\ny_j<=beta(u_j-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta) keeps the next point capped.\n\nFor q_(j-1)>0, equation(3.3) places r_(j-1)-v(q_(j-1)) on its\noutward circular normal; y_(j-1)>=1>=q_(j-1) fixes the sign. For\nq_(j-1)=0 it gives y_(j-1)<=beta(u_(j-1)-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta),\nthe endpoint cone generated by the circular normal and(0,1).\nThe criterion <r-g,w-g><=0 for every w in K proves\n\n    Pi_K(r_j)=v(q_j),\n    u_0=beta+sum alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),\n    y_0=1+sum alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_0>=beta*y_0.                    (3.4)\n\nThis is a symbolic finite definition, not an executed recurrence.\nFor incoming ell>=0,D>0 put\n\n    M=u_0+y_0/beta, sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y_0,\n    A=ell+ell_out/beta,\n    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K)(X-A,Y-ell_out).                          (3.5)\n\nThe local states(A+sigma*u_j,ell_out-sigma*y_j) start at(ell+D,0)\nand follow exactly -(a_j/2)grad Phi. Inputs exceed ell and outputs\nare in[0,ell_out). The final gradient is sigma(beta,-1). A selected\nb>2 has T=b/2-1,D_out=sigma*T and exact post-checkpoint state\n\n    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*D_out,ell_out+D_out).                      (3.6)\n\nThe component is zero at this instant by(3.2); later motion may\nreactivate it. It is zero whenever X<=ell,Y>=0. Its output derivative\nvanishes for EVERY X when Y>=ell_out: replacing(p,-q) by(p,0) in\nits maximization increases the objective by q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2\nfor q>0. This exact isolation pays the active-coordinate induction.\n\n## 4. Static function, all global updates, smoothness and exact radius\n\nApply Section3 to every block in(1.1), obtaining u_i,y_i,M_i. Set\nell_1=0,D_1=1 and\n\n    T_i=b_(J+i-1)/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,\n    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,\n    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,\n    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)).\n\nFix, before GD, the function on R^(k+1)\n\n    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)),\n    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))\n                 +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)), x_0=e_1.     (4.1)\n\nThe map stacking all coordinate pairs and the last scalar counts every\ncoordinate at most twice; its squared operator norm is at most2. Each\nunscaled summand is convex1-smooth on its own coordinates. Thus the\nfactor1/2 makes F globally convex1-smooth. F>=0 and F(0)=0.\n\nAt ANY state define\n\n    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),\n    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.\n\nThe full physical update, retaining all earlier components, is\n\n    x_j^+=x_j-(h/2)(P_j-Q_(j-1)), 1<=j<=k+1. (4.4)\n\nAll original directed interpolation rows hold for this static function:\nthe1-smooth descent bound for V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, minimized at y,\ngives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.\nBoth orientations, minimizers and terminal points are included. Actual\nEuclidean Gram matrices are positive semidefinite by definition.\n\n## 5. Uniform local estimates when beta=1/k\n\nLet w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta). (5.1)\n\nWhen q_(j-1)=0 this identity follows from y_(j-1)=y_j; if both q are\nzero it is equality. Its numerator is the integral from q_(j-1) to q_j\nof p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t). Capped steps have p=C_beta.\nIt follows from(3.4) that\n\n    u_0=beta+C_beta*s/2-E_gap,\n    0<=E_gap<=4k^2(Y_k+beta),\n    |M-C_beta*s/2|<=20k^2 exp(2k)=:K_k. After subsequent checkpoints, retired coordinates\nmay have changed in both directions. We do NOT set all of them to\ntheir individual post-checkpoint values A_i-beta D_(i+1) at the root.\n\nThroughout the suffix Q_k=0, and the fixed retired function is\n\n    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) E_(sigma_i K)\n                       (x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))\n                         +1/2 Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k).    (7.1)\n\nThe last circular component is horizontal because its output is above\nell_(k+1). Its value is retained exactly, not dropped. The full retired\nupdate is\n\n    x_i(t+1)=x_i(t)-(h_t/2)[P_i(t)-Q_(i-1)(t)], 1<=i<=k,        (7.2)\n\nwhere P_k is the derivative of the Huber in(7.1), and V_m=2G_ret(x(N)).\nFor i<k define\n\n    r_i=x_i-A_i, z_i=beta(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1),\n    U_i=P_i-beta Q_i>=0.\n\nAlso r_k=x_k-A_k,U_k=P_k,Q_0=Q_k=0. U_i is a horizontal cone\ncoefficient, distinct from the original benchmark U_n^ZJ. Since\nbeta A_i=beta ell_i+ell_(i+1), the local envelope is exactly\n\n    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta Q)^2+Q^2]/2:\n            0<=Q<=sigma_i,\n            0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta Q}.                (7.3)\n\nIndeed, the horizontal sections of K run from p=beta q to p=p(q).\nThe lower segment joins0 to(beta,-1), the upper boundary is concave,\nand their convex hull contains every intervening point. Concavity also\nbounds the convex hull above. Thus(7.3) is an identity.\n\nWhen r_i<=0, maximizing first over U gives U_i=0, followed by\n\n    Q_i=clip(z_i/(1+beta^2),0,sigma_i),\n    Phi_i=Hub_((1+beta^2)sigma_i)(z_i)/(1+beta^2).               (7.4)\n\nFor general r_i, U_i can be positive. The exact residual updates are\n\n    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta Q_i-Q_(i-1)),\n    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)[(1+beta^2)Q_i-beta Q_(i-1)-beta Q_(i+1)\n                                               +beta U_i-U_(i+1)]. (7.5)\n\nThese include both neighboring contributions. Summing independent\ntwo-Huber tail laws would delete genuine terms of(7.5).\n\n## 8. Whole-hierarchy comparison with all input excursions charged\n\nPut kappa=1+beta^2,theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta), and for i<k define\n\n    qhat_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i), qhat_0=qhat_k=0,\n    barsigma_i=sigma_i for 1<=i<k, barsigma_0=barsigma_k=0,\n    e_i=beta*(barsigma_(i-1)+barsigma_(i+1)),\n    gamma_i=kappa*sigma_i-e_i.\n\nHere e_i,gamma_i are fixed cap combinations. From(6.3) and beta->0,\neventually gamma_i>0 and, uniformly for i<k,\n\n    d_m*e_i<=gamma_i.                                         (8.1)\n\nEvery exact maximizer in(7.3) satisfies\n\n    Q_i<=qhat_i,\n    kappa(qhat_i-Q_i)-beta U_i<=theta U_i.                     (8.2)\n\nFor the complete curved-regime check, the function\nA(q)=sigma_i*p(q/sigma_i)-beta q decreases continuously from\nC_beta*sigma_i to0. At fixed U its feasible Q interval is[0,qmax(U)],\nwhere A(qmax(U))=U and qmax(0)=sigma_i. The maximizer therefore has\nQ=clip((z-beta U)/kappa,0,qmax(U)), proving Q<=qhat. If the upper\ncap is inactive, kappa(qhat-Q)<=beta U. If active, Q=qmax(U).\nConcavity and endpoint values imply p(q)+q>=1+beta, hence\nU=A(Q)>=(1+beta)(sigma_i-Q). It follows that\nkappa(qhat-Q)-beta U<=kappa(sigma_i-Q)-beta U<=theta U.\nLower and upper cap boundaries are included.\n\nInsert(8.2) into(7.5). All neighbor differences that are dropped are\nNONPOSITIVE, since Q_j<=qhat_j. The resulting unconditional step bound is\n\n    z_i^+<=z_i-(h/2)[kappa qhat_i-beta qhat_(i-1)-beta qhat_(i+1)]\n                                  +(h/2)[theta U_i+U_(i+1)].  (8.3)\n\nDefine the nonnegative charge from the ACTUAL suffix trajectory\n\n    L_i=(1/2)sum_(t=0)^(N-1) h_t[theta U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)].     (8.4)\n\nFor any starting vector, the final residual obeys\n\n    z_i(N)<=max{kappa*sigma_i+(c-1)e_i/2,\n                              z_i(0)-gamma_i*S_(m-1)/2}+L_i.  (8.5)\n\nIf z_i(N)<=kappa*sigma_i this is immediate. Otherwise take its LAST\nentry into z_i>kappa*sigma_i. If it was above the threshold at all\nsamples, qhat_i=sigma_i throughout, and summing(8.3) with the neighbor\ncaps gives the second term plus L_i. Otherwise the last entry uses an\nactual step b_j,0<=j<m-1, whose preceding z_i is at most kappa*sigma_i.\nDropping only its nonpositive own term bounds the new residual by\nkappa*sigma_i+b_j e_i/2 plus that step's charge. Immediately after\nthis occurrence is a complete H_j within the remaining suffix: this\nright-subtree property follows inductively from the word definition.\nAt all samples after the last entry, qhat_i=sigma_i. The following\nH_j therefore loses at least gamma_i*S_j/2 apart from its charges;\nall further steps lose a nonnegative amount apart from their charges.\nThe uncharged bound is kappa*sigma_i+(b_j e_i-gamma_i S_j)/2.\nUsing(5.5), b_j<=d_m S_j+c-1, and(8.1) proves the first term.\nAdding at most L_i pays EVERY source, including the crossing and all\nlarge steps. This is an argument on the ordered hierarchy, not a\nmass-only replacement of its nonlinear dynamics.\n\nFor this policy's ACTUAL incoming vector the second term in(8.5) is\nnonpositive for all sufficiently large m. Let S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1),\nthe complete mass before the suffix. Global updates(4.2) and caps give\n\n    delta_(i1)-C_beta*sigma_i*S_pre/2<=x_i(0)\n                    <=delta_(i1)+sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/2,\n\nwhere sigma_0=0 and delta_(i1) is1 for i=1 and0 otherwise. Therefore\n\n    z_i(0)<=beta*delta_(i1)\n                   +(beta*sigma_(i-1)+C_beta*sigma_(i+1))*S_pre/2. (8.6)\n\nOnly the nonpositive -beta ell_i was dropped. Uniformly in i, the\ncoefficient divided by sigma_i tends to lambda-2,\nS_pre/S_(m-1)->lambda-1, and gamma_i/sigma_i->1. Their product\ntends to (lambda-2)(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1. For i=1 the extra ratio\nbeta/(sigma_1*S_(m-1)) tends to0 by(6.2) and J=m-k. This proves\nthe incoming budget. As kappa+(c-1)e_i/(2sigma_i)->1 uniformly,\n(8.5) yields eventually\n\n    (z_i(N))_+<=2sigma_i+L_i.                                 (8.7)\n\nThere is no assumption that earlier components were zero at the root.\nThe exact maximization formula also gives\n\n    Phi_i<=C_beta*sigma_i*(r_i)_++sigma_i*(z_i)_+, i<k,\n    Phi_k<=C_beta*sigma_k*(r_k)_+.\n\nDefine one remaining nonnegative quantity on the ACTUAL trajectory:\n\n    X_m=C_beta*sum_(i=1)^k sigma_i*(r_i(N))_+\n           +(1/2)sum_(t=0)^(N-1) h_t\n                         sum_(i=1)^(k-1) sigma_i\n                                      [theta U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)]. (8.8)\n\nSumming(8.7) and using(6.4) proves the unconditional comparison\n\n    0<=V_m<=4sigma_1^2+X_m,\n    B_(n_m)E_m<=T_m+o(1)+B_(n_m)X_m/R_m^2.                    (8.9)\n\nEvery old value is included. X_m has NOT been proved small. It is a\nsufficient interface, not an equivalent formula for V_m: accumulated\nnonnegative charges can overcount activity dissipated later. Proving\nB_(n_m)X_m/R_m^2->0 would imply the complete scoped obstruction\nB_(n_m)E_m->T_infty<1. No such estimate is asserted here.\n\n## 9. The conditional slab result and a counterexample to blanket invariance\n\nIf every suffix sample satisfied x_i(t)<=A_i for all i<=k, then\nU_i(t)=0 by(7.3)--(7.4), and every terminal(r_i)_+=0. Thus X_m=0\nand(8.9) proves a full asymptotic obstruction. This is CONDITIONAL,\nnot a property proved for the actual trajectory. It already concerns\nthe coupled edge system, with no independence assumption.\n\nThe entire slab {x_i<=A_i for 1<=i<=k} is NOT invariant for this\nsame static function and the available large steps. For all large m,\nconsider this suffix state in the original R^(k+1):\n\n    x_1=A_1, x_2=ell_2-kappa*sigma_1, x_3=ell_3,\n    x_4=...=x_k=0, x_(k+1)=ell_(k+1)+D_(k+1).\n\nAll retired inputs are at most their A_i. The first component has\nz_1=kappa*sigma_1 and(P_1,Q_1)=(beta*sigma_1,sigma_1).\nThe second has input<A_2 and output ell_3, so P_2=Q_2=0. Later\ncircular components vanish: the third input is its threshold ell_3\nand its output0; subsequent inputs are0 with nonnegative outputs.\nThe terminal scalar does not affect coordinate2. Therefore\n\n    x_2^+=ell_2-kappa*sigma_1+h*sigma_1/2>A_2\n       whenever h>2kappa+2ell_3/(beta*sigma_1).                 (9.1)\n\nThe unchanged suffix contains h=b_(m-2). Meanwhile\nell_3/(beta*sigma_1)=T_1*y_2/(beta*M_2)=O(k exp(2k)) by\n(5.2),(5.4),(5.5), whereas b_(m-2) grows like lambda^(m-2).\nThus(9.1) holds eventually. This uses the SAME function and a physical\nstep in the suffix; it is not a construction repair.\n\nThe falsifying state is NOT asserted to be the actual state at that\nstep. It refutes blanket slab invariance only. A trajectory-specific\nbarrier or a bound on the excursion flux could still hold. The example\nidentifies why a two-checkpoint absorption argument cannot be applied\nindependently to every pair.\n\n## 10. Exact energy accounting and the remaining interfaces\n\nAn exact identity also keeps the signed effects that the sufficient\npositive-source bound(8.8) may overcount. Let z_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0)\nin R^k, which minimizes G_ret, and define at each suffix sample\n\n    d_i=P_i-Q_(i-1),\n    N_grad=sum_i P_i^2+sum_(i<k)Q_i^2,\n    C_cross=sum_(i=2)^k P_i Q_(i-1),\n    slack=sum_(i<k)[-<(P_i,-Q_i),\n                        (z_*(i)-A_i,z_*(i+1)-ell_(i+1))>]\n                                     -P_k*(z_*(k)-A_k)>=0.\n\nThe slack sign follows from the polar-cone condition at a minimizer;\nit is not an assumed sign for individual coordinates. The maximization\nidentity gives <grad G_ret(x),x-z_*>=G_ret(x)+N_grad/4+slack/2.\nSince sum_i d_i^2=N_grad-2C_cross, expansion of(7.2) gives for EVERY\nphysical step, with no restriction on its size,\n\n    ||x-z_*||^2-||x^+-z_*||^2\n       =2h G_ret(x)+h(2-h)N_grad/4\n                         +h^2 C_cross/2+h*slack.              (10.1)\n\nC_cross and slack are nonnegative. The N_grad coefficient is negative\nwhen h>2 and MUST be paid. Dropping it would not be a descent proof.\n\nFor the exact terminal value, write\nD_E(v,u)=E(v)-E(u)-<grad E(u),v-u>>=0 for the Bregman remainder of\na differentiable convex E. For i<k take the fixed envelope E_(sigma_i K)\nat its two centered coordinates, and for i=k take Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)\nat r_k. Let B_i(t) denote the remainder from sample t to t+1. Direct\nfirst-order expansion of(7.1) gives the equality\n\n    V_m=2G_ret(x(0))\n              -(1/2)sum_t h_t sum_i d_i(t)^2\n                                      +sum_t sum_i B_i(t).    (10.2)\n\nAll overlaps enter through d_i, and all overshoots remain in the actual\nBregman terms. Neither sum has been compared sharply enough here.\n\nA sufficient retained contribution for a diagnostic lower witness at\nthe original benchmark is, for example,\n\n    liminf_m B_(n_m)*V_m/R_m^2>=1-T_infty.                     (10.3)\n\nIts exact V_m is specified by the actual static trajectory or by(10.2).\nA strict version would give a strict margin for THIS diagnostic family.\nA strict upper bound for the same term below1-T_infty would instead\nobstruct this policy. The explicit sufficient unpaid obstruction\ninequality supplied by the new comparison is\n\n    B_(n_m)*X_m/R_m^2 ->0.                                   (10.4)\n\nAttempt one: obtain(10.4) from slab invariance. Section9 refutes general\ninvariance; actual-trajectory invariance remains unproved. Attempt two:\nuse distance descent. Equation(10.1) exposes the unpaid h(2-h) term.\nIts favorable coupled cross term is exact, but no comparison paying\nall large-step negative terms from it has been established.\n\nNo snapshot, independent pair sum, or terminal-only product is offered\nas the result. Equations(1.3),(8.8)--(8.9),(10.2)--(10.4) state exactly\nwhat is paid and what is not. This round does NOT complete a retained\nterminal bound for the chosen policy.\n\nEven a successful(10.3) only treats H_(m,1/4) at horizons2^m-1.\nThe original all-word/all-horizon minimax question would still require\na lower bound for W_n(h) uniform over EVERY nonnegative word h of\nlength n, at the original B_n scale, or another valid route to the\noriginal target. No such extension follows from this one policy.\n\n## 11. Retrieval, resources, and handoff\n\nQuery-memory recalled the shifted-circle construction, complete\ntwo-checkpoint draft, scoped scalar/tree failures, and current guidance.\nFact searches found no accepted growing circular-prefix result.\nFact5fd396eba75651e7 concerns different spiral families and is not a\npremise. Only the original-ray conclusion of04219554e41d9750 is used;\nits statement and relevant proof were read.\n\nMatlas retrieval for overlapping projection envelopes and long-step\nretained Huber transport returned three non-applicable results:\n2011.14269 Lemma3.6 is a continuous-flow perturbation bound;\n2212.04459 Lemma3 is a proximal descent inequality;2012.15458 Lemma2.1\nconcerns a different Moreau composition. None was imported.\n\nThe exact primary source was downloaded and extracted:\nKaizhao Liu and Yuhan Ye, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient\nDescent: The Strongly Convex Case, arXiv:2609.26873v1,\nhttps://arxiv.org/html/2609.26873v1. Read the preceding-component\nupdates, FactA.1, and the circular construction and checkpoint proof.\nIts finite-condition-number contraction and relative-function\nnormalization do not apply here. The circular envelope was reconstructed\ndirectly without a quadratic term, with overlap smoothness and closest\nradius derived anew. Original tau1/4 minimax target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Silver certificate deformation: source reconstruction and three viability screens\n\nStatus: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT. No formal submission, accepted new\nfact, full-class bound for the perturbed word, or resolution of the original\nminimax target is claimed. This is max4's one allocated round after completed64,\nwith completed ceiling65 / attempted ceiling66. TASK.md and guidance\n8fd9094550caff70 govern; plan cbe64562a9a92f5d records the work. Latest\nguidance56f0c49630564f24 was read and explicitly leaves this assignment unchanged.\n\nThe outcome is three exact, narrowly scoped failed screens. The second screen\nallows arbitrary terminal-gradient coefficients; the third changes the two\nright-child weights independently and passes the first two levels. Neither\nsurvives the original-coordinate test at the third level. The source itself\ndoes give a sharp GD objective certificate for ORIGINAL Silver, but its missing\nextension is precisely a primitive/history inequality already present in the\nproject. No new theorem for the perturbed word follows merely from retrieval.\n\n## 1. Model, accepted inputs, and unchanged physical word\n\nUse fact1252b8823bb5a290's normalized convention: L=1,\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0, x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t, and\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.\n\nAll formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) remain independent when testing a\ncertificate. For a nonnegative physical word h, W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over the entire smooth-convex class\nand every finite dimension. Thus W=2R in the project's notation. Every ordered\nrow exists; unspecified multipliers below are zero, not deleted constraints.\n\nThe other accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11\n(primitive and objective identities, including their full-coordinate scope),\nand fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 (the original benchmark comparison only).\nNo unverified previous obstruction or numerical optimizer is a premise.\n\nFix one enclosing integer m and set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), d_j=a c^j, 0<=j<m,\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, n_m=2^m-1.\n\nEvery child uses this SAME epsilon, beta, c, a. In particular testing H_3\nbelow means the actual initial seven-step subword of H_m, not resetting m=3.\nThe terminal gradient is a formal sample, not another physical step.\n\n## 2. Complete source and hypothesis mapping\n\n### 2.1 Altschuler--Parrilo and the original full interpolation certificate\n\nSource: Jason M. Altschuler and Pablo A. Parrilo, *Acceleration by Stepsize\nHedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization*,\narXiv:2309.16530v1, Theorem1.1, Definition2.1, Theorem3.4, Lemmas3.5--3.7,\nand AppendixA. The exact downloaded HTML reports v1, dated28September2023.\nThe proof, including the corrections, signs, and AppendixA expansions, was read.\nURL: https://arxiv.org/html/2309.16530 .\n\nThe source's nonnegative residual is\n\n    P_ij=2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 = -2 Q_ji.\n\nConsequently both a transpose and a factor2 are required when transferring\nits multipliers. Its theorem is for a convex L-smooth function with a chosen\nminimizer, arbitrary initialization and dimension, ORIGINAL Silver word\nH^0_(j+1)=(H^0_j,1+lambda^(j-1),H^0_j), and n=2^j-1.\nWrite A=lambda^j, S=A-1, and\n\n    C_AP=(1+sqrt(4A^2-3))/2, r_AP=1/(2C_AP).\n\nThe source identity is\n\n    sum sigma_ij P_ij\n      =||x_0||^2-||x_n-C_AP g_n||^2-2C_AP f_n.       (2.1)\n\nIt gives W_n(H^0_j)<=1/C_AP, not r_AP. This is a direct GD objective\nbound, not a proximal bound. Its multiplier recursion glues the left copy\nwith weight1 and the right copy with weight lambda^2=1+2lambda, adds\nlambda times each actual right step in the two endpoint-to-right rows,\nand corrects the three endpoint/optimum incidences. The final optimum\nincidences are sigma_(star,i)=h_i for i<n, sigma_(star,n)=C_AP,\nsigma_(n,star)=S. Negative correction entries must be aggregated with their\ninherited entries; the final multipliers are nonnegative.\n\nHere is a direct reconstruction in the ORIGINAL Q convention, using only\nthe accepted primitive identity, which also checks the source's full array.\nFor any word h of sum S and terminal n define\n\n    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nOn the original Silver word define the finite multiplier array Lambda^0\nfrom the empty array as follows. For a join, p is the bridge index, z is the\nparent terminal, and j ranges over the nonterminal right-child queries.\nLet A=1+sum(child), d=A/lambda and let h_j denote its right steps. Embed\nboth complete child arrays and add\n\n    Lambda^0_parent = Lambda^0_left + lambda^2 Lambda^0_right\n       +2lambda [ sum_j h_j (Q_(j,p)+Q_(j,z))\n                                      +Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z) ].   (2.2)\n\nHere and below a sum of Q terms denotes its coefficient array. All four\nadded coefficient types are nonnegative. Fact04219554e41d9750, identity(5),\napplies because d(2A+d)=A^2. It yields the polynomial identity\n\n                    V_h + sum Lambda^0_ij Q_ij = 0.           (2.3)\n\nThe empty identity is0=0. The actual parent capacity is2A+d=lambda A;\ninduction proves A=lambda^j, so this is exactly the original physical Silver\nword at every level. It is an identity on all formal gradients, with zero\nPSD remainder, not a restriction to actual or coincident gradients.\n\nTo recover (2.1), take sigma_ij=Lambda^0_ji/2 on finite pairs and the\nthree optimum-incidence types displayed above. The finite part of the\nsource's Theta+Xi+Delta recursion is exactly (2.2) after this transpose and\nfactor2; its endpoint coefficients are 2lambda and 2lambda d in Q convention.\nThe remaining identity follows from\n\n    V_h-2 sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,star)\n      =||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2-2S f_n-S(S+1)||g_n||^2,             (2.4)\n    C_AP^2-C_AP=S(S+2).\n\nIndeed adding -2C_AP Q_(n,star)-2S Q_(star,n) to (2.4) gives the\nright-hand side of (2.1). This checks all coefficients without invoking\nthe source's computer-algebra script. In project normalization the full\nPSD identity is the square ||x_n-C_AP g_n||^2/C_AP, with finite multipliers\nLambda^0/C_AP, prefix-to-optimum weights2h_i/C_AP, terminal-to-optimum\nweight2, and optimum-to-terminal weight2S/C_AP.\n\nThe SAME finite baseline array has the sharper completion\n\n    ||x_0||^2-2D f_n+sum Lambda^0_ij Q_ij\n       +2 sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,star)+2A Q_(n,star)\n                         =||x_n-A g_n||^2,  D=2S+1=2A-1.    (2.5)\n\nFor an explicit expansion, substitute (2.3) and (2.4), then use\nA^2-A=S(S+1) and D=S+A. Thus every coefficient in (2.5) is nonnegative,\nand division by D gives a full original-space objective certificate:\n\n                         W_n(H^0_j)<=1/(2lambda^j-1).        (2.6)\n\nThe matching Huber function has slope t=1/D, value x^2/2 for |x|<=t,\nand t|x|-t^2/2 otherwise. Start at1, with minimizer0. Since every step is\nnonnegative and each prefix sum is at most S, every query lies in the\npositive affine piece: x_i=1-t sum_(k<i) h_k and x_n=(S+1)/D>=t.\nIts value satisfies2f(x_n)=1/D. This proves tightness of the baseline\ncompletion within this joint reconstruction. It supplies no perturbed-word\ncertificate. No separate unverified intermediate result is being adopted.\n\n### 2.2 Chikhi: product identity, joint potential, and proximal output\n\nSource: Salah R. Chikhi, *Last-Iterate Performance of Gradient Descent and\nRelaxed Proximal Point via s-Composability*, arXiv:2609.13765v1,\nDefinition1, Proposition1 with proof, Theorem1 with equality construction,\nLemma2 with proof, and Section5/Theorem4 with proof.\nURL: https://arxiv.org/html/2609.13765v1 .\n\nIts definition requires a positive schedule and BOTH\n\n    eta=1/(1+S)=product_(i<n)(h_i-1),\n    ||x_0-x_star||^2 >= ||x_n-x_star||^2\n                    +S(S+1)||g_n||^2+2S(f_n-f_star).          (2.7)\n\nThe source obtains Silver's s-composability from Grimmer et al., Lemma6.\nNo uninspected theorem of that paper is needed here: (2.3)--(2.4) reconstruct\nthe necessary baseline inequality from the accepted project identity.\nFor a varying schedule the history expression in Chikhi's Lemma2 is\n\n    sum h_i[2(f_i-f_n)+||g_i||^2+2<g_i,x_0-x_i>]\n           -||x_n-x_0||^2-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nExpanding x_0-x_i=sum_(t<i)h_t g_t cancels every off-diagonal product\nagainst the squared displacement, leaving exactly V_h. Adding the\nnonnegative source residuals sum h_i P_(star,i) means subtracting\n2sum h_i Q_(i,star), which gives (2.7) by (2.4). Thus an appeal to this\nhistory mechanism for H_m is precisely the already-unpaid primitive\ninequality; the new paper does not furnish it for a perturbed word.\n\nFor a proper closed convex F with an attained minimum and mu>0, the\nsource's relaxed proximal iteration is\n\n    z_i=prox_(mu F)(x_i), x_(i+1)=x_i+h_i(z_i-x_i),\n    z_n=prox_(mu F)(x_n).\n\nIts exact theorem is sup mu(F(z_n)-F_star)/||x_0-x_star||^2=1/[4(S+1)]\nfor a positive s-composable schedule. The equality example is F(x)=t|x|\nwith t=R/[2mu(S+1)], x_0=R, and z_n=R/2. For original Silver this is\n1/(4lambda^j). Its terminal point is z_n, not our x_n.\n\nThe normalized envelope f=mu F_mu satisfies g_n=x_n-z_n and\n\n                   f(x_n)-f_star=mu(F(z_n)-F_star)+||g_n||^2/2.\n\nDropping the last term would misidentify the objective. The source's joint\nterminal inequality instead gives, with t=||g_n||,\n\n    mu(F(z_n)-F_star)+(S+1)t^2<=R t,\n    f(x_n)-f_star <= R t-(S+1/2)t^2 <= R^2/[2(2S+1)].        (2.8)\n\nThis is the paid conversion, with W<=1/(2S+1). For an arbitrary smooth\nf, the same result follows directly from (2.7) and the original optimum\ninterpolation inequality; no assumption that a particular smooth f has\nalready been supplied as an envelope is necessary. Formula(2.5) gives its\nexplicit original-row certificate whenever (2.3) is available.\n\nThe perturbed H_m does not satisfy the definition's product identity.\nSet v=a/(c-2), so0<v<1. The physical sum identity, checked by substitution\nin A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, is\n\n    A_j=v c^j+(1-v)2^j.\n\nCounting each level's multiplicity, or inducting in the physical word, gives\n\n    product_(i<n_m)(h_i-1)=beta^(2^m-1)c^(-m),\n    A_m product_(i<n_m)(h_i-1)\n      =beta^(2^m-1)[v+(1-v)(2/c)^m] <1.                      (2.9)\n\nThis prevents using the named s-composability theorem as stated. It does\nNOT disprove the joint potential without the product identity.\n\n### 2.3 Wang--Ma--Yang--Zhou: the older metrics\n\nSource: Bofan Wang, Shiqian Ma, Junfeng Yang and Danqing Zhou,\n*Relaxed Proximal Point Algorithm: Tight Complexity Bounds and Acceleration\nwithout Momentum*, arXiv:2410.08890, Lemma5.1 and Theorems5.2--5.3 with the\ndisplayed proofs. URL: https://arxiv.org/html/2410.08890 .\n\nFor arbitrary convex L-smooth h and original Silver, Theorem5.2 states the\ntight GD objective bound (h(x_n)-h_star)/(L R^2)<=1/(4lambda^j-2), and\ngradient bound ||grad h(x_n)||/(L R)<=lambda^(-j). Its finite array in\nLemma5.1 is Lambda^0 transposed and divided by2: its residual is -Q_ji\nand its identity(34) has right-hand side V_h/2. The weighted optimum completion\nis reproduced explicitly in (2.5); no typography in the extracted proof\nis being used as an algebraic premise.\n\nTheorem5.3 applies this to an envelope and bounds\nF(z_n)-F_star+(mu/2)||grad F_mu(x_n)||^2 by R^2/[mu(4lambda^j-2)].\nThe discussion calls the proximal objective bound obtained by dropping\nthe residual term non-tight. That is the older gap addressed by Chikhi.\nIt is not a new sharper bound for the GD objective of a perturbed word.\n\n## 3. A paid terminal-coefficient relaxation\n\nFor a word of sum S define the generalized history form\n\n    V_(h,K)=2 sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-K||g_n||^2.\n\nSuppose a proposed nonnegative finite array has the exact full identity\nV_(h,K)+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=M(g), where M is a quadratic form in ALL\nformal gradients. This equation defines M; it does not presume PSD.\nFor K>=0 put\n\n    b_K=(1+sqrt(1+4K))/2, F_K=S+b_K.\n\nExpanding (2.4) with K in place of S(S+1) gives\n\n    ||x_0||^2-2F_K f_n+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij\n          +2sum h_i Q_(i,star)+2b_K Q_(n,star)\n                          =M(g)+||x_n-b_K g_n||^2.           (3.1)\n\nAll added coefficients are nonnegative. If M>=0, division by F_K would give\n\n    W_n(h)<=1/F_K=2/[2S+1+sqrt(1+4K)].\n\nRelative to Huber1/(2S+1), the loss is exactly\n\n    chi_K=(2S+1)/(S+b_K).                                   (3.2)\n\nIf only a radius-inflated joint inequality with constant r_rad were proved,\nthe corresponding upper would pay the additional factor r_rad. Neither a\nsmaller K nor a different radius coefficient is free. The screens below test\nthe actual M, not a presumed child oracle or an informal potential estimate.\n\n## 4. One full-coordinate coefficient expansion for the screens\n\nConsider (h,1+d,h), with child sum s, A=s+1, T=2A+d, parent sum T-1.\nLet p denote its bridge query and z its terminal. Let r_vec=sum_j h_j g_j\nover the nonterminal right child. Its two copies of V_(h,K) have factor1\nand kappa. Introduce the nonnegative correction array\n\n    E=u sum_j h_j Q_(j,p)+v_w sum_j h_j Q_(j,z)\n                                      +p_w Q_(z,p)+z_w Q_(p,z).\n\nThe symbol v_w is a multiplier weight, distinct from v=a/(c-2).\nFor a nonempty right child, all function values cancel in\nV_(parent,K_+)-V_left-kappa V_right+E iff\n\n    u+v_w=2(kappa-1),\n    u s+p_w-z_w=2(A+d).                                     (4.1)\n\nWhen the right child is empty, choosing these identities remains a valid\ndefinition of the continuation. Empty sums are zero; no formal endpoint is\nidentified with a different endpoint. The resulting original quadratic is\n\n    M_def_parent=M_def_left+kappa M_def_right+R,\n    R=(u-v_w)||r_vec||^2/2\n       +(u d-z_w)<r_vec,g_p>+(p_w-v_w)<r_vec,g_z>\n       +(p_w d-z_w)<g_p,g_z>+C_p||g_p||^2+C_z||g_z||^2,       (4.2)\n    C_p=K-d-d^2+(u s+p_w)/2-(d+1/2)z_w,\n    C_z=-K_++kappa K+v_w s/2+(p_w+z_w)/2.\n\nFor a check that this retains remote and mixed terms, the gradient parts of\nthe four sums in E, before multiplication by u,v_w,p_w,z_w, are\n\n    sum h_j Q_(j,p): d<r_vec,g_p>+||r_vec||^2/2\n                       +sum h_j(1-h_j)||g_j||^2/2+s||g_p||^2/2;\n    sum h_j Q_(j,z): -||r_vec||^2/2\n                       +sum h_j(1-h_j)||g_j||^2/2\n                       +s||g_z||^2/2-<r_vec,g_z>;\n    Q_(z,p): d<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_vec>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;\n    Q_(p,z): -(d+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2\n                                       -<g_p,r_vec>-<g_p,g_z>.\n\nTheir value parts immediately give (4.1). Adding the history diagonals gives\n(4.2). Thus this is an identity on the original space, with no assumed\ncoincidence of gradients and no quotient-space PSD test.\n\n## 5. The first actual levels already detect the deformation\n\nFor every enclosing m>=3, c<3, so0<d_j<A_j at every level. To see this,\nA_j>v c^j=d_j/(c-2) and c-2<1. Also\n\n    t_j=d_j/A_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v-1)(2/c)^j]\n\nis strictly increasing in j. Put t_star=1/lambda. At the first three levels,\n\n    d_0=a, A_0=1; d_1=beta, A_1=2+a;\n    d_2=beta c, A_2=4+a(2+c),\n    t_0<t_star, t_1<t_star, t_2>t_star. For the second,\n\n    lambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.\n\nFor the third, use lambda^3=5lambda+2 and expand only the displayed scalar:\n\n    c(lambda d_2-A_2)\n      =epsilon[(lambda+4)-(4lambda+2)epsilon\n                                      -(5lambda+2)epsilon^2] >0. No schedule recurrence or numerical\nevaluation was executed to obtain these textual identities.\n\n## 6. At original Silver, d=A/lambda and\nkappa=lambda^2, so this is exactly (2.2), not an unrelated seed.\nSubstitution in (4.2) leaves\n\n    R=Delta(||g_z||^2-||g_p||^2),\n    Delta=kappa A^2-T^2=kappa d^2-A^2,\n    Delta/A^2=(1+t)(t^2+2t-1)/(1-t), t=d/A. Both\nproofs use the actual ordered suffix, all preceding physical updates,\nand state-dependent signs. They do not require the accumulated positive\ncharge X_m to be small. The middle k-2 components remain unpaid.\n\n## 1. The unchanged word, function, and initialization\n\nFor integers m>=16 set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,\n    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,\n    d=c-2, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,\n    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1.\n\nAll children use the SAME enclosing m. Select precisely the first\noccurrences of levels J,...,m-1, at zero-based positions\n2^(J+i-1)-1, i=1,...,k. The word is\n\n    (H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1),...,b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).       (1.1)\n\nLet s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k, and s_(k+1)=S_(m-1).\nSelected step i is b_(J+i-1). No extra checkpoint, step deletion,\nreordering, state reset, or modification of the function is made.\n\nFor 0<=q<=1 define\n\n    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),\n    C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), v(q)=(p(q),-q),\n    K=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).\n\nFor a compact convex body C containing0 put\n\n    E_C(w)=max_(g in C){<g,w>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nThe one-sided Huber function Hub_delta is0 on(-infinity,0], t^2/2\non[0,delta], and delta*t-delta^2/2 on[delta,infinity).\n\nHere is the exact symbolic local definition, included to fix the SAME\nstatic function. For each ordinary block a_1,...,a_N in its physical\norder, put alpha_j=a_j/2. Starting from (u_N,-y_N)=(beta,-1), choose\nq_(j-1) in[0,1) backwards by\n\n    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)\n      =max{beta/(C_beta-beta),\n               (y_j+(1-alpha_j)*beta)/(u_j-(1-alpha_j)*beta)},\n    (u_(j-1),-y_(j-1))=(u_j,-y_j)+alpha_j*v(q_(j-1)).         (1.2)\n\nThe word has strictly positive steps. The ratio on the left increases\nfrom beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity, and the denominator on the right\nis positive. The formula therefore uniquely specifies a finite set of\nlocal data without executing a recurrence. Denote the starting local\ncoordinates for block i by u_i,y_i and put M_i=u_i+y_i/beta.\n\nSet ell_1=0,D_1=1 and recursively define static constants\n\n    T_i=b_(J+i-1)/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,\n    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,\n    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,\n    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)).              (1.3)\n\nFor sufficiently large m all T_i are positive. The fixed function and\ninitial point are\n\n    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)),\n    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))\n                  +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)),\n    x_0=e_1 in R^(k+1).                                    (1.4)\n\nThe physical update is x^+=x-h grad F(x). Multiplying F by an arbitrary\nadvertised L>0 and dividing the physical gradient step by L gives the\nsame trajectory and normalized objective.\n\n## 2. Geometry, chronology, and true radius, derived jointly\n\nCompleting the square shows grad E_C(w)=Pi_C(w), the Euclidean\nprojection onto C. Projection optimality at w and w' gives\n||Pi_C(w)-Pi_C(w')||^2<=<Pi_C(w)-Pi_C(w'),w-w'>. Comparing the two\nmaximization formulas gives differentiability with this gradient.\nThus E_C is convex and1-smooth. It is nonnegative, and is zero exactly\non the polar cone of C: a positive support value remains positive in\nthe envelope at a sufficiently small positive multiple of the\nsupporting vector.\n\nThe horizontal sections of K are\n\n    0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p<=p(q).                               (2.1)\n\nThe lower boundary is the segment joining0 to(beta,-1), and the\nconcave upper boundary is the displayed circular arc. The endpoints\nare(C_beta,0),(beta,-1). In particular (p,0) belongs to K whenever\n(p,-q) does. Its cone has those two extreme rays. Therefore\n\n    E_(sigma K)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U-V<=0.              (2.2)\n\nThe disk center(beta,beta), radius1+beta contains0. Its outward radial\nvector at(p(q),-q) supports K. In (1.2), u_j>=beta,y_j>=1 and the\nq's are nondecreasing in forward order. Indeed, before a backward\nstep the uncapped supporting ratio is(y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta); the\nratio defining its predecessor reduces the numerator by alpha_j*beta\nand increases the denominator by the same amount. At q=0 the capped\nnormal-cone inequality is y_j<=beta*(u_j-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta),\nwhich is preserved by further capped backward steps. Formula(1.2)\nputs the vector from the candidate projection to the new point on\nthe circular outward normal, or in the endpoint normal cone when\nq=0. Projection optimality gives\n\n    Pi_K(u_j,-y_j)=v(q_j),\n    u_i=beta+sum_j alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),\n    y_i=1+sum_j alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_i>=beta*y_i.              (2.3)\n\nThe local states(A_i+sigma_i*u_j,ell_(i+1)-sigma_i*y_j)\nstart at(ell_i+D_i,0), follow every step -(a_j/2)grad Phi_i,\nand have last gradient sigma_i*(beta,-1). The checkpoint produces\n\n    (x_i,x_(i+1))=(A_i-beta*D_(i+1),ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1)).     (2.4)\n\nAt that instant Phi_i is zero by(2.2), but it may reactivate later.\nThe construction does not reset it.\n\nFor EVERY state write\n\n    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),\n    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.\n\nThen every physical update, including all prehistory, is\n\n    x_i^+=x_i-(h/2)(P_i-Q_(i-1)), 1<=i<=k+1.              (2.5)\n\nIf x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1), replacing(p,-q) by(p,0) improves the\nprojection objective by q*(x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))+q^2/2 for q>0.\nConsequently Q_i=0 in that region. If x_i<=ell_i and the output\nis nonnegative, Phi_i is zero by(2.2). These two properties prove\ninductively that the designated active pair follows its local states:\nthe previous component contributes no derivative in its input\ncoordinate; the next component and all subsequent components vanish.\nEvery earlier retired coordinate still follows(2.5). This proves the\nchronology without substituting post-checkpoint snapshots for the\nactual later states.\n\nThe map stacking adjacent pairs and the last coordinate has squared\noperator norm at most2. The factor1/2 in(1.4) therefore makes F\nglobally convex1-smooth. Also F>=0 and F(0)=0. Its complete minimum\nset is\n\n    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;\n                                         x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.\n\nThe point x_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) is feasible. Since\n1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1 and\nu_1>=beta*y_1, A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). For a feasible point with\nx_1<0 the squared distance from e_1 is at least1. On[0,A_1] the\nlower bound (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2 decreases up to A_1 and is\nattained by x_*. Thus the true closest radius is\n\n    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2\n         =(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.            (2.6)\n\nConvex1-smooth interpolation, in both directions and including all\nsamples and minimizers, follows directly: apply the smooth descent\ninequality to F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, whose minimum occurs at y, to get\nF(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.\nAll trajectory Gram matrices are actual Euclidean Gram matrices.\n\n## 3. Uniform scales and the accepted benchmark input\n\nWe give the local estimates because earlier unverified drafts are not\npremises. In a backward block let w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta).\nAt an uncapped point u_j-beta=(p(q_j)-beta)*w_j. A cross product\nin(1.2) gives\n\n    w_(j-1)=w_j*(1+\n      [p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j)]\n                  /[beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1))]).             (3.1)\n\nAt a transition to q_(j-1)=0 the same identity follows from\ny_(j-1)=y_j, and if both q's are zero it is equality. Its numerator\nis the integral of p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t) between the two q's.\nAfter division by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1), this integrand is at most\nmax(1,-p'(t)). Multiplication of(3.1), w_N=1 and 1+z<=exp(z)\ntherefore yield\n\n    y_i<=2 exp(2k)=:Y_k,\n    integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.     (3.2)\n\nFor the integral, split at t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta and integrate\n-p' on the second interval. At an uncapped point,\nu_j<=beta+2k*(Y_k+beta). Such backward steps form one contiguous\nportion; their total u increment is at most2k*(Y_k+beta), and\ntheir p is at least beta. The remaining steps have p=C_beta.\nThus their total deficit from C_beta times half the block mass is\nat most4k^2*(Y_k+beta). Combining this with(2.3) and y_i/beta\ngives, uniformly in i,\n\n    |M_i-C_beta*s_i/2|<=K_k, K_k=20k^2 exp(2k).              (3.3)\n\nThe concave function p(q)+q/beta has smaller endpoint C_beta.\nConsequently (2.3) also gives the useful exact lower bound\n\n    M_i>=beta+1/beta+C_beta*s_i/2\n        >=C_beta*(1+s_i/2).                                (3.4)\n\nPut v_mass=a_word/d. One has0<v_mass<1, since c*(c-2)>1>eta_word.\nSolving S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j by substitution gives the exact identities\n\n    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,\n    b_j=d*S_j+c-1-d*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                         (3.5)\n\nThey imply uniformly for J<=j<=m-1 that S_j/(v_mass*c^j)->1,\nk*K_k/S_J->0, and\n\n    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),\n    S_(m-r)/lambda^(m-r)->exp(1/4) for fixed r>=1.           (3.6)\n\nFor these claims, the relative error in the first limit is bounded\nby a constant times c^(-J)+(2/c)^J. The logarithm of k*K_k is\nO(k+log k), whereas J log(lambda) is of order m. Finally use\n0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 with x=1/(4m), and k/m->0.\n\nThe first transfer ratio T_1/M_1 equals(d/C_beta)*(1+o(1)); all\nlater ones equal(c*d/C_beta)*(1+o(1)). Their relative errors are\nuniformly O(K_k/S_J+c^(-J)+(2/c)^J), and k times this bound\ntends to zero. Thus\n\n    D_(k+1)=(d/C_beta)*(c*d/C_beta)^(k-1)*(1+o(1))\n            ->(lambda-2)*exp(-2),\n    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C_beta*S_J).                        (3.7)\n\nHere c*d=1+O(1/m), and log(C_beta)/beta->2, so C_beta^k->exp(2).\nThe radius follows from(2.6),(3.2),(3.3). For every i<k,\n\n    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2               (3.8)\n\nuniformly. Eventually all these ratios lie in[1/3,1/2], so\nsum_i sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2.\n\nThe ORIGINAL all-split benchmark is C_0=B_0=1 and\n\n    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nOnly fact04219554e41d9750 is used for\n\n    0<Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1)<infinity.              (3.9)\n\nIts statement and proof Section7 were read. Its enclosure at b=1,j=0\nalso gives Z_1<5 from B_1=4. No seed assumption or unverified\nconstruction is imported from that fact.\n\nA growth estimate needed below is\n\n    (B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*sigma_1^2*(1+K_k+2^m)->0.              (3.10)\n\nIndeed B*sigma_1^2=O(lambda^(-m+2k)). Multiplication by K_k is\nstill exponentially decreasing in m because k=O(sqrt(m)); the term\n2^m gives O((2/lambda)^m*lambda^(2k)), which tends to zero since\nlambda>2. No numerical evaluation or recurrence replay is used.\n\n## 4. Actual retired dynamics and screen I: the accumulated charge\n\nIndex the actual final suffix H_(m-1) by t=0,...,N,\nN=2^(m-1)-1. Its input x(0) is the result of EVERY earlier step(2.5).\nThe last excess evolves as D_(k+1)-delta times half the mass already\nread. It remains at least delta, and Q_k=0 by the output-isolation\nproperty. The fixed function governing the first k coordinates is\n\n    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))\n                     +1/2 Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k).    (4.1)\n\nThe retired update is still(2.5), with Q_0=Q_k=0, and\nP_k=clip(x_k-A_k,0,C_beta*sigma_k). Write\n\n    r_i=x_i-A_i,\n    z_i=beta*(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1), i<k,\n    U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i for i<k, U_k=P_k,\n    kappa=1+beta^2, theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta).\n\nIn the cone coordinates P=U+beta*Q, the exact maximization formula is\n\n    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta*Q)^2+Q^2]/2:\n      0<=Q<=sigma_i,\n      0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta*Q}.                    (4.2)\n\nHence U_i>=0. If r_i<=0 then U_i=0 and\n\n    Q_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i),\n    Phi_i=Hub_(kappa*sigma_i)(z_i)/kappa.                    (4.3)\n\nThere are also the unconditional complementary sign facts\n\n    U_i>0 implies x_i>A_i>=ell_i and Q_(i-1)=0;\n    Q_i>0 implies x_(i+1)<ell_(i+1) and U_(i+1)=0.          (4.4)\n\nThe exact residual equations, including BOTH neighbors, are\n\n    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta*Q_i-Q_(i-1)),\n    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)[kappa*Q_i-beta*Q_(i-1)-beta*Q_(i+1)\n                                             +beta*U_i-U_(i+1)]. (4.5)\n\nThe retained value and the total normalized payoff are exactly\n\n    V_m=2G_ret(x(N))=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x(N)),\n    B_(n_m)*2F(x_(n_m))/R_m^2=T_m+B_(n_m)*V_m/R_m^2,\n    T_m=B_(n_m)*D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))*R_m^2]\n        ->T_infty=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda).                 (4.6)\n\nIn the sum, Phi_k means exactly the last horizontal envelope, equal\nto the original circular value at the actual state. The limit follows\nfrom(3.6)--(3.9) and (lambda-2)^2=lambda^(-2). In particular\nT_infty<5/64<1, using exp(17/4)>16 and2lambda>4.\n\nScreen I assesses the previous sufficient NONNEGATIVE charge\n\n    X_m=C_beta*sum_i sigma_i*(r_i(N))_+\n         +(1/2)sum_t h_t sum_(i<k) sigma_i\n                                     [theta*U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)]. (4.7)\n\nThe elementary cap estimate for its flux part is only\n\n    X_flux <= (C_beta*S_(m-1)/2)\n                   sum_(i<k) sigma_i*(theta*sigma_i+sigma_(i+1)).\n\nThe RIGHT SIDE after normalization has order lambda^(2k), by the\npositive limiting ratio sigma_2/sigma_1 and(3.6)--(3.9). It does not\nvanish or supply the required strict constant. This is a failure of\nthat upper comparison; it is NOT a lower bound on actual X_m.\n\nSigned coordinate budgets do not automatically improve it. Define\nH_i^U=(1/2)sum_t h_t U_i(t) and H_i^Q=(1/2)sum_t h_t Q_i(t),\nwith H_0^Q=H_k^Q=0. Then\n\n    x_i(0)-x_i(N)=H_i^U+beta*H_i^Q-H_(i-1)^Q.              (4.8)\n\nThe telescoping positive weights that eliminate every H_i^Q are\nproportional to beta^(i-1), since their adjacent ratio must be beta.\nThe bulk weights in(4.7) have adjacent ratios asymptotic to lambda-2,\nwith different harmless endpoint factors. Explicitly their coefficients\non H_i^U are theta*sigma_1, theta*sigma_i+sigma_(i-1) for\n2<=i<k, and sigma_(k-1) at i=k. They are not those telescoping\nweights. Converting between the two\ncan cost ((lambda-2)/beta)^O(k), and the signed endpoint displacement\nhas not itself been bounded on the required scale.\n\nThe crucial actual restriction is stronger than this budget for the\nfirst coordinate. At its own checkpoint x_1=A_1-beta*D_2. Thereafter\nx_1 never increases, since Q_0=0 and P_1>=0 in EVERY physical step.\nThus throughout the suffix\n\n    r_1<=-beta*D_2<0, U_1=0.                               (4.9)\n\nThis is a property of the initialized trajectory, not invariance of\nthe entire input slab. Even after dropping U_1 from the cap estimate,\nthe sigma_1*U_2 charge retains the same lambda^(2k) scale in that\nupper bound. The actual barrier will remove the U_2 charge from the FIRST\nterminal component by a last-entry argument. We have not proved\nthat X_m itself fails to vanish, nor supplied a lower bound showing\nthat it overcounts by a nonvanishing amount. Its status remains open.\nOnly the unnecessary use of all-time U_2 to bound Phi_1 is eliminated.\n\n## 5. Screen II: quantitative actual terminal bounds at both ends\n\nThis is the viable part of the round. A last entry uses an occurrence\nof b_j in H_(m-1). Immediately following that occurrence lies an entire\nH_j inside the remaining suffix. This property follows by induction\nfrom H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), and keeps the original order and enclosing\nm. It is used only on samples AFTER the last entry, so the claimed\nregion persists by the choice of the last entry, not by an assumed\ninvariance theorem.\n\n### 5.1 First circular component: the apparent source switches off\n\nBy(4.9), formula(4.3) applies to component1 at EVERY suffix sample.\nThe exact scalar residual update is\n\n    z_1^+=z_1-(h/2)(kappa*Q_1-P_2).                        (5.1)\n\nIt includes the full P_2=U_2+beta*Q_2. If z_1>kappa*sigma_1,\nthen Q_1=sigma_1 and\n\n    x_2=beta*x_1-z_1\n       <ell_2-beta^2*D_2-kappa*sigma_1<ell_2.\n\nTherefore r_2<0, U_2=0 and P_2=beta*Q_2<=beta*sigma_2.\nDefine gamma_1=kappa*sigma_1-beta*sigma_2. In this region every\nphysical step loses at least h*gamma_1/2 in z_1, with no restriction\non h. In contrast, an entry step from z_1<=kappa*sigma_1 can add\nat most h*C_beta*sigma_2/2. These are different, justified caps.\n\nEventually gamma_1>0 and\n\n    d*C_beta*sigma_2<=gamma_1,                              (5.2)\n\nbecause the ratio of the left side to sigma_1 tends to\n(lambda-2)^2<1, whereas gamma_1/sigma_1 tends to1.\n\nIf z_1(N)>kappa*sigma_1 and there was an entry during the suffix,\ntake its last occurrence, at b_j. All later samples remain above the\nthreshold. Pay the entry with C_beta*sigma_2 and the following H_j\nwith gamma_1. Every subsequent step has nonnegative loss. Thus\n\n    z_1(N)<=kappa*sigma_1\n                      +(b_j*C_beta*sigma_2-gamma_1*S_j)/2\n            <=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C_beta*sigma_2/2,         (5.3)\n\nwhere(3.5) and(5.2) pay every large bridge.\n\nThe case of no entry must use the ACTUAL full prehistory. Let\nS_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1) be the mass before the final suffix. From\n(2.5), x_1(0)<=1 and x_2(0)>=-C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/2.\nHence\n\n    z_1(0)<=beta+C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/2.                    (5.4)\n\nIf all samples from t=0 were above the threshold, summing(5.1)\nwould give z_1(N)<=z_1(0)-gamma_1*S_(m-1)/2. This is negative\neventually: S_pre/S_(m-1)->lambda-1 and\n\n    C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/(gamma_1*S_(m-1))\n                  ->(lambda-2)*(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1,\n    beta/(sigma_1*S_(m-1))->0.\n\nSo the no-entry case cannot leave a terminal positive residual above\nthe threshold. If the terminal residual is below the threshold, (5.3)\nalready bounds its positive part. Consequently, for every sufficiently\nlarge m, on the prescribed actual trajectory,\n\n    (z_1(N))_+<=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C_beta*sigma_2/2\n               <=2sigma_1,\n    0<=Phi_1(x(N))<=2sigma_1^2.                             (5.5)\n\nThis is a full terminal bound for that component. No U_2 integral,\nindependent two-Huber trajectory, or alternative state was substituted.\nBy(3.10), B_(n_m)*Phi_1(x(N))/R_m^2 tends to zero.\n\n### 5.2 Last circular component: the small cap mismatch is paid\n\nWrite L_k=C_beta*sigma_k for the horizontal gradient cap. The actual\ncheckpoint formula for the active kth coordinate gives\n\n    r_k(0)=-beta*D_(k+1)<0.                                (5.6)\n\nThis statement concerns only coordinate k at its OWN checkpoint; it\nmakes no assertion about the other retired coordinates. During its\nactive ordinary block and checkpoint Q_(k-1)=0 by output isolation,\nso preceding gradients do not alter this particular formula.\n\nAt any suffix sample with r_k>L_k, x_k>ell_k, whence Q_(k-1)=0\nand P_k=L_k. Thus r_k loses h*L_k/2 there. At an entry from\nr_k<=L_k its increase is at most h*sigma_(k-1)/2. If the terminal\nresidual exceeds L_k, (5.6) guarantees an entry. Its last occurrence\nat b_j and its following H_j give\n\n    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(1/2) max_(0<=j<=m-2)\n                                      [b_j*sigma_(k-1)-L_k*S_j]_+.\n                                                                  (5.7)\n\nNo assumption of distance descent is used. Put s=S_(m-2),\nT=b_(m-2)/2-1 and Delta_k=d-C_beta*T/M_k. The EXACT ratio\nsigma_k/sigma_(k-1)=T/M_k follows from(1.3). Thus(3.5) implies\n\n    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(sigma_(k-1)/2)\n                                      [c-1+(Delta_k)_+*s]. (5.8)\n\nTo bound the possible positive cap defect, expand it BEFORE estimating:\n\n    d*M_k-C_beta*T\n       =d*(M_k-C_beta*s/2)+C_beta*(3-c)/2\n                   +(C_beta*d/2)*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).\n\nBy(3.3),(3.4), s/M_k<=2/C_beta. Therefore\n\n    (Delta_k)_+*s\n       <=(2d/C_beta)*K_k+3+d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).            (5.9)\n\nDefine the explicit nonnegative error allowance\n\n    A_err,m=c-1+(2d/C_beta)*K_k+3\n                                  +d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).\n\nSince Hub_(L_k)(r)<=L_k*(r)_+, the last retained value satisfies\n\n    0<=Phi_k(x(N))\n       <=L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err,m.                 (5.10)\n\nAll constants c,d,C_beta remain bounded, and sigma_k,sigma_(k-1)\nare at most sigma_1 eventually. Formula(3.10) pays (5.10), including\nthe changed cap ratio and its 2^(m-2) mass correction. Thus\nB_(n_m)*Phi_k(x(N))/R_m^2 tends to zero uniformly along the frozen\ngrowing policy. The bound is quantitative and applies to every physical\nbridge; it does not require exact absorption of the final component.\n\n## 6. Screen III: why the same comparison does not close the middle\n\nThis screen was rejected as a complete uniform comparison. The exact\nfailure is stronger than merely saying that the middle is coupled.\n\nFix 2<=i<=k-1 and put L_i=C_beta*sigma_i. At a sample with r_i>L_i,\noutput isolation gives Q_(i-1)=0. Maximizing over P at fixed Q in\n(2.1), r_i>L_i forces the UPPER circular boundary, so\n\n    P_i=sigma_i*p(Q_i/sigma_i),\n    L_i-P_i=sigma_i*[sqrt(1+2beta)\n               -sqrt((1+beta)^2-(beta+Q_i/sigma_i)^2)].     (6.1)\n\nUnlike at i=k, this cap loss need not be zero. Concavity of p gives\n\n    0<=L_i-P_i<=(C_beta-beta)*Q_i.                          (6.2)\n\nAfter the last entry into r_i>L_i, let s_entry be its step index.\nAs long as that terminal excursion persists, its exact signed loss is\n\n    r_i(N)=r_i(s_entry+1)\n                   -(L_i/2) sum_(t>s_entry) h_t\n                   +D_i^cap(s_entry),\n    D_i^cap(s_entry)=(1/2)sum_(t>s_entry)h_t*(L_i-P_i(t)).   (6.3)\n\nThis is a tail-specific cap loss, not the all-time source X_m. The\nentry is bounded by L_i+b_j*sigma_(i-1)/2. Its following H_j pays\nthe full horizontal-cap part exactly as in(5.7). However D_i^cap\nhas not been paid. If Q_i>0, formula(4.4) does give U_(i+1)=0,\nbut the next coordinate then follows\n\n    x_(i+1)^+=x_(i+1)+(h/2)(Q_i-beta*Q_(i+1)).              (6.4)\n\nThus bounding the accumulated Q_i in(6.2) requires its actual\ndownstream negative excursions and the NEXT neighboring gradient.\nDeleting beta*Q_(i+1), or assuming a frozen output, is not legitimate.\nWhen Q_i=0, later horizontal motion of component i+1 can recreate a\nnegative output at a large bridge. No entry-and-persistence statement\nfor a fixed quadratic core or a fixed face has been proved here.\n\nThere are two explicit quantitative failures of a cap-only closure.\n\nFirst, even the always-full-cap idealization cannot pay the available\nincoming horizontal estimate for a middle coordinate. The full\nprehistory bound from(2.5) is\n\n    r_i(0)<=sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/2-A_i, i>=2.                 (6.5)\n\nDropping -A_i and comparing its leading coefficient to L_i*S_(m-1)/2\ngives the limiting ratio\n\n    sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/(C_beta*sigma_i*S_(m-1))\n                   ->(lambda-1)/(lambda-2)=2+sqrt(2)>1.    (6.6)\n\nThe bound in(6.5) is not the actual value, and does not show that an\nalways-positive horizontal excursion occurs. It shows why one cannot\ndiscard that branch from a last-entry proof without a stronger actual\nprehistory estimate. The endpoint proofs avoided this: the first used\nits permanent negative input, and the last used its own exact negative\nincoming residual. Neither argument applies automatically to a middle\ninput after all later checkpoints.\n\nKeeping the omitted A_i does not repair this particular comparison:\nA_i=sigma_(i-1)*y_(i-1)+sigma_i*y_i/beta, so(3.2),(3.8)\ngive A_i/(sigma_(i-1)*S_(m-1))->0 uniformly. Thus the positive\nleading allowance in(6.6) survives that exact static threshold.\n\nSecond, the best state-independent lower bound for P_i on r_i>L_i\nfrom this same circular geometry is its infimum beta*sigma_i. To see\nwhy a larger fixed lower bound is unavailable, fix r>L_i and q in(0,1).\nFor q sufficiently close to1 put\n\n    t=(r-sigma_i*p(q))/(p(q)-beta)>0,\n    w=sigma_i*q+t*(q+beta).\n\nAt the centered point(r,-w), the displacement from sigma_i*v(q) is\nt*(p(q)-beta,-q-beta), an outward circular normal. Hence the actual\nprojection for the SAME envelope is sigma_i*v(q). Letting q approach1\ngives P_i approaching beta*sigma_i while r remains fixed above L_i.\nOther coordinates can be chosen with the permanent x_1 bound intact.\nThese are alternative states of the unchanged function, NOT states\nproved reachable from initialization. Their purpose is only to test a\nuniform cap-floor lemma, not to refute trajectory-specific control.\n\nUsing that valid floor in a last-entry comparison leaves the positive\ncoefficient\n\n    d*sigma_(i-1)-beta*sigma_i.\n\nFor i=2 it is a fixed positive fraction of sigma_1 eventually, by\n(3.8) and beta->0. The entry at level m-2 in the maximum of the\ncomparison therefore leaves an allowance of order\nsigma_1*S_(m-2). Multiplying the horizontal-value allowance by\nC_beta*sigma_2 and the original benchmark gives order lambda^(2k),\nby(3.6)--(3.9). This comparison does not even give a bounded normalized\nupper estimate, let alone one below1-T_infty. This is the scale of a\nFAILED UPPER BOUND, not an attained terminal lower bound.\n\nThe cap-only method is therefore rejected without pretending that\n(6.3) is a closed recursion. A viable replacement must simultaneously\npay the actual incoming middle residuals and the ordered cap losses\nD_i^cap, or directly bound the complete actual middle value. Both\nneighbors must be retained in that estimate. No universal distance\ndescent or large-step Markov positivity was assumed: expansion of\ndistance would retain h*(2-h) times the gradient-square term, with\nits negative sign for h>2. The present endpoint proofs instead pay\neach large step through its following physical subtree.\n\n## 7. Exact new handoff and limits of the result\n\nLet\n\n    V_mid,m=sum_(i=2)^(k-1) Phi_i(x(N)),\n    epsilon_m=(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*\n       [2sigma_1^2+L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err,m].\n\nEvery quantity uses the same unchanged static function and its actual\nfull-prehistory trajectory. The jointly derived estimates prove, at\nthe UNVERIFIED DRAFT level,\n\n    0<=epsilon_m->0,\n    T_m+(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*V_mid,m\n       <=B_(n_m)*2F(x_(n_m))/R_m^2\n       <=T_m+(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*V_mid,m+epsilon_m.             (7.1)\n\nThis is a quantitative removal of two terminal contributions from\nthe comparison, not physical deletion of either component. They\nremain in the actual gradient throughout. One CANNOT repeat the\nendpoint argument by renaming component2 as component1: U_2 has not\nbeen shown zero, and its incoming Q_1 has not been removed. Similarly,\ncomponent k-1 continues to have its original moving output and Q_(k-1).\n\nThe remaining actual quantity is V_mid,m for k-2 growing middle\ncomponents. No bound below1-T_infty for its normalized limsup is\nestablished, and no normalized lower witness is established. The\ndefect D_i^cap and the incoming r_i(0) are precise unpaid mechanisms\nin the failed third comparison, not a claimed substitute theorem.\nWe have also NOT decided whether B_(n_m)*X_m/R_m^2 tends to zero.\n\nAccordingly the frozen policy remains OPEN. The original question\nover all words and all horizons remains OPEN even if this policy were\neventually decided. The assigned one round ends with the quantitative\nendpoint progress and the explicit middle comparison failure; it does\nnot automatically renew the allocation.\n\n## 8. Retrieval, resources, and persistence\n\nSkills used: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,\nsearch-math-results. Web search\nlocated the existing Silver and composition literature, but supplied\nno justified transfer to the perturbed word. No external theorem was\nimported, and no new technical literature branch was opened. In\nparticular a smaller effective smoothness was not assumed to make\nthis word a certified Silver schedule.\n\nThree comparisons were screened: (I) accumulated/signed flux;\n(II) state-dependent terminal last entries, fully developed at the\ntwo endpoints; (III) extension to middle components via cap loss,\nrejected as a complete cap-only uniform comparison.
