{"id": "74648afc6ca81e6c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T15:13:18.206748+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max68 JOINT UNVERIFIED: exact same-word Huber price for original optimum-outgoing rows is sum b_i(2S-p_i)<=D(zeta-1), D=1+2S. Full flow, every original matrix entry and signed finite-row correction are derived. Three paid policies fail: balanced optimum cycles and backward optimum triangles on the specified asymmetric base force qD>4/3 at every m>=1536, hence any PSD levels have liminf B_n q>28/27; balanced cycles on the source-calibrated unequal-weight finite array require zeta-1>2^m/(30000m). These are certificate-family failures, not W lower bounds or general-dual infeasibility.", "evidence": "# Paid optimum rows: exact budget and three scoped analytical screens\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No formal submission,\nnew fact, full-class objective bound, or original-target resolution is claimed.\nThis is worker max's single round after completed67, with ceiling68 and\nattempted ceiling69, under TASK.md and guidance66aa167542a58193.\n\nThe three explicit policies below have nonnegative original multipliers and\nexact value flow. Their new optimum-outgoing rows fit a genuine same-word\nHuber budget. Nevertheless each fails a full original-coordinate PSD test.\nFor the two asymmetric policies, every PSD level even in the larger classes\nspecified below must satisfy q_cert*(1+2 sum H_m)>4/3 for m>=1536. For the\nsource-based policy, bounded whole-word slack cannot repair the fixed finite\narray: the necessary relative slack exceeds 2^m/(30000m).\n\nThese are restrictions on certificate families, NOT lower bounds on W.\nNo unrestricted-dual infeasibility is asserted. General finite-row changes\nthat alter the displayed cross-block moments or inherited right-child\ncoefficients remain outside these screens.\n\n## 1. Fixed words, original objective, and accepted inputs\n\nFix an enclosing integer m. Every child throughout this document uses\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta_word=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta_word/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nThe physical word is h=H_m of length n=2^m-1. Put\n\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, 0<=i<=n.\n\nAll n updates are retained in order. The terminal gradient g_n is an original\ninterpolation variable, not an extra physical update. No child resets m.\n\nUse accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290's convention: L=1,\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0 and independent original formal vectors\n(x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For all distinct\noriginal indices, including star and terminal, define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThe accepted interpolation convention gives Q_ij<=0 on actual trajectories.\nA full certificate is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij,             (1)\n\nwith every lambda_ij>=0, every value coefficient zero, and M(q) PSD on ALL\noriginal formal vectors. It would imply W_n(h)<=q, with W=2R and the\noriginal specified-minimizer radius. Zeros below are multiplier choices,\nnot deleted original inequalities.\n\nOther accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750's original all-split benchmark,\nray limit, and full-coordinate primitive/objective identities, and\nfact5fd396eba75651e7's scalar comparison for these exact words:\n\n    B_n/D<237/250 for m>=1536,\n    B_n/D -> Z_1/(2 exp(1/4)).                              (2)\n\nHere U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Only the scalar part of fact5fd is used. Its special\nwitness-family optima do not bound W from above.\n\nThe complete prior drafts dd4045b39aa0c257, ee71972baf834490 and\n83357ebfcaa1356b were read. They are provenance, not accepted proof premises.\nEvery prior coefficient identity needed below is reproduced and derived\nwithin this joint draft. Numerical findings ad24079f80cd6a3a and\n242f8d453ac26183 are not mathematical inputs.\n\nAll notation in this document is local. In particular, A in Section 5 is\na child STEP SUM; A_j in Section 7 is one plus a child sum. The physical\nbridge d in Section 5 is distinguished from the bridge EXCESS d_j in\nSection 7. The letters a_i below are multipliers, whereas unindexed a above\nis the fixed physical hierarchy parameter.\n\n## 2. Exact same-word Huber price, before choosing any policy\n\nLet Hub(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and |x|-1/2 otherwise. It is convex,\ndifferentiable and 1-smooth, with unique minimizer0. Start at x_0=D.\nEvery actual query is x_i=D-p_i>=S+1>1, so\n\n    g_i=1, f_i=D-p_i-1/2, 2f_n=D.\n\nFor an empty word S=0 the same calculation uses the boundary point1;\nour screened words have S>0. Direct substitution in the ORIGINAL rows gives\n\n    Q_ij=0 for finite i!=j,\n    Q_(i,star)=0,\n    Q_(star,i)=-(2S-p_i).                                 (3)\n\nWrite b_i=lambda_(star,i), a_i=lambda_(i,star), and q=zeta/D with\neta=zeta-1. On this exact Huber transcript,\n\n    M(q)=D eta-sum_(i=0)^n b_i(2S-p_i).                    (4)\n\nThus a necessary condition for PSD is\n\n    sum_i b_i(2S-p_i)<=D eta,\n    eta>=0, sum_i b_i<=D eta/S<=3eta when S>1.             (5)\n\nThe last inequality is strict when eta>0; at eta=0 all b_i are zero.\n\nThe row price varies from 2S to S, including the terminal price S. No\nnonzero optimum-outgoing row is free on this transcript. Function values\nand the specified-minimizer radius are unchanged.\n\nThe price is exact as an evaluation, but it is not a sufficient PSD test.\nThere is a second useful restriction that keeps the x_0 mixed terms. Let\nK_ij=lambda_ij on finite pairs and let B=sum b_i. The complete finite flow is\n\n    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+a_i-b_i=2*1_(i=n),\n    sum_i a_i=2+B.                                        (6)\n\nOn the entire two-dimensional original subspace g_i=v for every finite i,\nx_0=x, flow gives\n\n    M(q)=q x^2-(2+B)xv+[D+sum_i b_i(1+p_i)]v^2.            (7)\n\nIndeed finite position terms sum to\nsum_i p_i(out_i^K-in_i^K)=2S-sum_i a_i p_i+sum_i b_i p_i;\nfinite gradient differences vanish, and the optimum terms supply the\nremaining a_i p_i and (a_i+b_i)/2. Therefore PSD also requires\n\n    zeta >= D(1+B/2)^2/[D+sum_i b_i(1+p_i)].              (8)\n\nFor example, spending the ENTIRE budget (4) on b_n=D eta/S, eta>0,\nwould give M(D,1,...,1)=0, but the x_0 derivative there equals\n2eta-b_n=-eta/S!=0. A PSD quadratic vanishing at a vector annihilates\nthat vector, as follows by evaluating it on that vector plus t times an\narbitrary direction for both signs of t. Such saturated terminal spending\ntherefore cannot be PSD without changing the budget allocation. This is a\nbudget warning, not a fourth screened policy.\n\n## 3. Full value flow and full correction in original coordinates\n\nPut z_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, so x_i=x_0+z_i. Under (6), (1) is exactly\n\n    q||x_0||^2-sum_i a_i<x_0,g_i>\n     +sum_i a_i[-<g_i,z_i>+||g_i||^2/2]\n     +sum_i b_i||g_i||^2/2\n     +sum_(i!=j finite)K_ij[<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].   (9)\n\nIn particular its FULL matrix is\n\n    [[q,-a^T/2],[-a/2,C]],                                (10)\n\non the original n+2 coordinates. Define h_n=0 and out_i=a_i+sum_j K_ij.\nEvery entry of the gradient block C is\n\n    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(j>i)K_ij,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                         +h_i[a_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j.  (11)\n\nAll sums in (11) are over finite labels. Half the total mass incident to i\nis out_i-1_(i=n) by (6), including BOTH optimum directions. A forward\nposition row based at i subtracts h_i K_ij from the diagonal. For i<j,\ndifference squares contribute -(K_ij+K_ji) to the cross coefficient;\nposition rows based at i contribute -h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv, and rows based at j\ncontribute h_i[a_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv]. This proves (11) without omitting b.\n\nFor q>0 the exact full square completion is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0-sum_i a_i g_i/(2q)||^2\n                +sum_(i,j)[C_ij-a_i a_j/(4q)]<g_i,g_j>.   (12)\n\nThe terminal, every mixed term, and every original gradient remain.\n\nMore generally start with any value-flow base (K^0,a^0,b^0=0) at 1/D.\nPermit a signed finite modification deltaK with final K^0+deltaK>=0.\nSet r_i=sum_j deltaK_ij-sum_j deltaK_ji. Exact final flow forces\n\n    a_i=a_i^0+b_i-r_i>=0, b_i>=0.                         (13)\n\nThe complete value-free correction to the base is\n\n    eta||x_0||^2/D-sum_i(b_i-r_i)<x_0,g_i>\n     +sum_i(b_i-r_i)sum_(t<i)h_t<g_i,g_t>\n     +sum_i(b_i-r_i/2)||g_i||^2\n     +sum_(i!=j)deltaK_ij[<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].   (14)\n\nThis follows by subtracting (9) for the base. It allows reductions of old\nfinite multipliers, provided their FINAL signs hold. The three policies\nbelow use particular nonnegative additions and do not exhaust (13)-(14).\n\nA balanced optimum cycle of weight rho_i adds rho_i to both a_i and b_i,\nkeeps K unchanged, and adds exactly\n\n    rho_i C_i^opt,\n    C_i^opt=Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i)\n           =||g_i||^2-<x_0,g_i>+sum_(t<i)h_t<g_i,g_t>.    (15)\n\nFor the sum of such cycles, deltaC has diagonal rho_i and entries\n2deltaC_ti=h_t rho_i for t<i. Its x_0-to-gradient entries are -rho_i/2.\nThese are not just positive diagonal corrections. For example a lone\ncycle at i>0, with x_0=0, g_i=1 and an earlier g_t=-2/h_t, other gradients0,\nhas value -rho_i when h_t>0. Adding eta||x_0||^2/D does not change that\nnegative value. This illustrates why (12), rather than diagonal signs,\nis the required test.\n\nFor any distinct finite i,j, a directed optimum triangle of weight t_ij\nadds t_ij to the three ORIGINAL rows Q_(star,i), Q_(i,j), Q_(j,star).\nIts flow is zero and its complete polynomial is\n\n    t_ij T_ij^opt,\n    T_ij^opt=||g_i||^2+||g_j||^2-<g_i,g_j>\n                  +<g_i,z_j-z_i>-<g_j,x_0+z_j>.         (16)\n\nOnly a_j increases. Its new x_0 cross term is -t_ij<x_0,g_j>, and\nits Huber price is t_ij(2S-p_i). Both facts matter below.\n\n## 4. The three explicit policies and their actual budgets\n\nDefine positive density v_i=h_i for i<n and v_n=S+1, so sum_i v_i=D.\nTake eta>=0 and q=(1+eta)/D.\n\nPolicy I: on the asymmetric array of Section 5, add balanced optimum cycles\n\n    rho_i=eta v_i/D.                                     (17)\n\nPolicy II: use exactly the same cycle law (17), on the source-calibrated\nfinite array and its original objective completion in Section 7.\n\nPolicy III: on the asymmetric array, use\n\n    rho_i=eta v_i/(2D),\n    t_(n,L_k)=eta/2, all other added triangles zero.      (18)\n\nHere L_k is the original physical root query of the last join. Thus this\npolicy changes a finite row too: it adds eta/2 to Q_(n,L_k), as well as\nto Q_(star,n) and Q_(L_k,star).\n\nEvery addition in (17)-(18) is nonnegative and has zero value flow. For\nPolicies I and II, (3) gives their EXACT new Huber expenditure\n\n    P_H=eta/D * sum_i v_i(2S-p_i).                        (19)\n\nFor Policy III it is\n\n    P_H=eta/(2D)*sum_i v_i(2S-p_i)+eta S/2.               (20)\n\nFor eta>0, (19)<=2S eta<D eta. Similarly (20)<=3S eta/2<D eta.\nThus these are feasible budget allocations with genuine paid costs and\npositive Huber remainder, not laws rejected merely for spending too much.\nAt eta=0 the costs vanish. Exact sums (19)-(20), not only their upper\nbounds, specify the necessary cost of each policy.\n\nThe tests below grant STRICTLY LARGER classes: arbitrary nonnegative\nbalanced weights, and for Policy III arbitrary nonnegative triangles\nstar->R_i->L_l->star, with any original left destination. Their failure\ntherefore includes the explicit policies without fitting a weight profile.\n\n## 5. Complete asymmetric base, with unrestricted left completion\n\nConsider the final actual parent (H_j,d,H_j), j=m-1, k=2^j-1. In THIS\nsection d=1+a c^j is the physical bridge, A=sum H_j, t=A+d,\nS=2A+d, D=1+2S. The left queries L_l=l, 0<=l<=k, include bridge L_k;\nright queries R_i=k+1+i, 0<=i<=k, include actual terminal n=R_k.\nLet p_i^c be the child chronological masses, with p_0^c=0,p_k^c=A.\n\nTake any nonnegative finite reference array K^c and nonnegative mu_i with\n\n    sum_v K^c_iv-sum_v K^c_vi+mu_i=2*1_(i=k).              (21)\n\nIn particular sum mu_i=2. Reference child PSD is NOT assumed. Set\n\n    theta=(3A+d)/(2S), s=1-theta=t/(2S),\n    kappa=0 or s/(2d), E=sum_i mu_i(A-p_i^c), 0<=E<=2A.\n\nThe prescribed original rows of the base are\n\n    K_(R_i,R_v)=K^c_iv, a_(R_i)=theta mu_i,\n    K_(R_i,L_0)=mu_i[s-kappa d(A-p_i^c)/A],\n    K_(R_i,L_k)=mu_i kappa t(A-p_i^c)/A,\n    K_(R_i,L_l)=0 for 0<l<k,\n    sum_l K_(L_l,R_i)=kappa mu_i(A-p_i^c).               (22)\n\nAll b_i are initially zero. All left finite rows and optimum attachments\nare otherwise arbitrary nonnegative values satisfying full original flow.\nNo PSD condition on the left child is presumed. Thus these screens grant\narbitrary position-dependent left corrections already present in the base.\n\nAll prescribed signs hold: s>0,theta>0, and at repaired kappa,\ns-kappa d(A-p_i^c)/A=s(A+p_i^c)/(2A)>=s/2. Put\nB_i=sum_l K_(R_i,L_l)=s mu_i+kappa mu_i(A-p_i^c), and\nF_i=sum_l K_(L_l,R_i)=kappa mu_i(A-p_i^c). Right flow is (21) plus\ntheta mu_i+B_i-F_i, hence is 2*1_(i=k).\n\nFor a completely explicit base completion, send every F_i from L_k, set\nall left finite rows0, and take\n\n    a_(L_0)=2s-kappa dE/A, a_(L_k)=kappa dE/A,\n    a_(L_l)=0 for 0<l<k.                                (23)\n\nThe first attachment is at least s; all are nonnegative. Incoming-minus-\noutgoing cross flow at L_0,L_k is precisely (23); other left flows vanish.\nThus (21)-(23) give a complete value-flow array for j>=1. For the empty\nword use a_0=2 separately; for H_1 use the actual one-step array\nlambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1, and apply (21)-(23)\nat subsequent levels. For h=(b) that one-step base has\n\n    M(1/(1+2b))=||x_0-(1+2b)(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/(1+2b)\n                              +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4.\n\nThe actual b=1+a is below sqrt(2)<3/2. This checks its starting calibration;\nno parent PSD is inferred. Subsequent definitions are coefficient formulas,\nnever executed recurrences. The proof next allows all larger left completions,\nnot just (23).\n\n## 6. New full left-block test: Policies I and III fail even with free weights\n\nOn the ORIGINAL formal space choose\n\n    g_(L_l)=v for EVERY left query, including L_k,\n    g_(R_i)=0 for EVERY right query, including terminal,\n    x_0=x.                                                (24)\n\nThe original positions are x_(L_l)=x-p_l^c v and x_(R_i)=x-tv.\nThis is a test subspace of the whole quadratic, not a quotient or a claim\nthat the formal assignment is interpolation-feasible.\n\nLet B_l be total backward inflow into L_l and F_l total forward outflow.\nLeft finite flow is -a_(L_l)-F_l+B_l. Multiplying it by p_l^c and summing\nshows that all left finite position terms contribute\nsum_l p_l^c[-a_(L_l)-F_l+B_l]v^2. The optimum rows contribute\n-a_L xv+sum_l a_(L_l)(p_l^c+1/2)v^2, where a_L=sum_l a_(L_l)=2s.\nBackward rows contribute (sum_i B_i)v^2/2. Forward rows contribute\nsum_l F_l[-t+p_l^c+1/2]v^2. All p_l^c a_(L_l) and p_l^c F_l terms cancel.\nConsequently the full base (9), including every arbitrary left coefficient,\nrestricts to\n\n    M_base(q)=q x^2-2s xv+Gamma_L v^2,\n    Gamma_L=sum_i B_i+sum_l p_l^c B_l-t sum_l F_l\n           =2s+kappa E.                                 (25)\n\nThe final equality uses sum_i B_i=2s+kappa E,\nsum_l p_l^c B_l=kappa tE, and sum_l F_l=kappa E, directly from (22).\nThis exact cancellation is the restrictive cross-block MOMENT of the base.\nIt is not a general identity for asymmetric certificates.\n\nA balanced cycle on a left label contributes\nrho_(L_l)[(1+p_l^c)v^2-xv] on (24); a cycle on a right label is zero.\nA backward optimum triangle star->R_i->L_l->star contributes precisely\nt_(R_i,L_l)[(1+p_l^c)v^2-xv] by (16). These statements include their\nx_0 mixed terms. Let U be the total weight of these left balanced cycles\nand backward triangles, and let J be their sum weighted by (1+p_l^c).\nThen 0<=J<=(A+1)U, and the complete modified restriction is\n\n    M(q)=q x^2-(2s+U)xv+(Gamma_L+J)v^2.                 (26)\n\nThe right balanced weights may be arbitrary; they vanish on this test.\nThe triangle weights and destinations may be arbitrary as specified;\nno Huber budget restriction is needed for the following obstruction.\n\nSuppose A>=1024 and d/A>2/5. Since E<=2A and kappa<=s/(2d),\n\n    Gamma_L<=s(2+A/d)<(9/2)s<(A+1)s.                    (27)\n\nFor q<=0, (26) cannot be PSD since its mixed coefficient is nonzero.\nFor q>0, set v=1 and x=(s+U/2)/q. Full PSD would require\n\n    q >= (s+U/2)^2/(Gamma_L+J)\n      > (s+U/2)^2/[(A+1)(s+U)] >= s/(A+1).             (28)\n\nThe last inequality follows by subtracting s(s+U) from (s+U/2)^2;\nthe difference is U^2/4>=0. This explicitly pays the mixed term: merely\nraising the positive gradient diagonal would miss its simultaneous change.\n\nAlso Ds>t=A+d, so\n\n    qD>Ds/(A+1)>(A+d)/(A+1)\n                     >(7/5)A/(A+1)>4/3.               (29)\n\nThe final inequality follows from A>20. Thus the exact full-space assignment\njust specified is negative at ANY proposed qD<=4/3, regardless of how the\nbalanced and backward-triangle weights are chosen. This proves the failure\nof both explicit Policies I and III and their larger stated classes.\n\n### The actual enclosing-m words satisfy the elementary conditions\n\nFor j=m-1, direct substitution in the physical sum identity gives\n\n    A=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2), d=1+a c^j.                (30)\n\nThe identity follows from S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j and S_0=0 by symbolic\ninduction. For m>=1536, 12/5<c<5/2 and a>3/10. For the upper bound on c,\nuse lambda<17/7 and epsilon<1/256 to get c<4369/1792<5/2.\nLet z=(2/c)^j. Since (5/6)^4<1/2 and j>=24, z<1/64. Then\n\n    A/d<1/(c-2)+z/a<17/7+5/96<5/2.\n\nThis proves d/A>2/5. Every child step exceeds1, so\nA>2^j-1>1024. Therefore (29) holds at EVERY m>=1536, on the actual\nunchanged word, not a newly parameterized child.\n\nThe proof is a short full-matrix screen. It does not spend the round on\nan impossibility theorem for general duals, or assert that a feasible level\nexists above this necessary lower restriction.\n\n## 7. The full source-calibrated array for Policy II\n\nIn this section set S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, d_j=a c^j, so the physical\nbridge at level j is 1+d_j and A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j. For any word H_j with\nterminal index z define the ORIGINAL primitive history polynomial\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<z)h_i(f_i-f_z)\n              +sum_(i<z)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-A_j(A_j-1)||g_z||^2.\n\nStart with empty finite array Lambda^0=0. At each join let p be the\nphysical bridge query and z the parent terminal; i ranges over nonterminal\nright-child queries. Use the following exact nonnegative weights:\n\n    u_j=2lambda,\n    v_j^w=[u_j d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,\n    k_j=1+(u_j+v_j^w)/2,\n    z_j^w=2A_j,\n    p_j^w=4A_j+2d_j-u_j(A_j-1)\n                         =2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda).     (31)\n\nEmbed the entire left finite array with weight1, the entire right finite\narray with weight k_j, and add\n\n    u_j sum_i h_i Q_(i,p)+v_j^w sum_i h_i Q_(i,z)\n                             +p_j^w Q_(z,p)+z_j^w Q_(p,z).   (32)\n\nThere are no optimum rows in this finite recursion. Define P_j by\nV_j+sum Lambda^j_ij Q_ij=P_j. This defines a full quadratic, without\nassuming its positivity.\n\nFor a check of every coefficient, temporarily write A=A_j,d=d_j,\ns=A-1,T=2A+d,K=A(A-1),K_+=T(T-1), u=u_j,v=v_j^w,k=k_j,\np^w=p_j^w,z^w=z_j^w, and r=sum_i h_i g_i over the right\nnonterminal queries. For a nonempty right child, function coefficients\ncancel precisely when\n\n    u+v=2(k-1), us+p^w-z^w=2(A+d),                       (33)\n\nwhich (31) satisfies. For the empty right child these equations remain\nsufficient and are the chosen policy; no necessity is asserted there.\nThe four gradient parts of the unweighted terms\nin (32) are respectively\n\n    d<r,g_p>+||r||^2/2+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2+s||g_p||^2/2,\n    -||r||^2/2+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2+s||g_z||^2/2-<r,g_z>,\n    d<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2,\n    -(d+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r>-<g_p,g_z>.\n\nAfter adding the history differences, the correction R_j in\nP_(j+1)=P_j^left+k_j P_j^right+R_j has coefficients\n\n    R_j=(u-v)||r||^2/2+(ud-z^w)<r,g_p>+(p^w-v)<r,g_z>\n                  +(p^w d-z^w)<g_p,g_z>+C_p||g_p||^2+C_z||g_z||^2,\n    C_p=K-d-d^2+(us+p^w)/2-(d+1/2)z^w,\n    C_z=-K_++kK+vs/2+(p^w+z^w)/2.\n\nSubstitute (31), put t_j=d_j/A_j, t_*=1/lambda,\nY=r-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. The SAME complete correction is\n\n    R_j=(t_*-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>\n                           +A_j^2(2+t_*+t_j)||Z||^2].     (34)\n\nFor instance the coefficient of ||r||^2 equals\n(u-v)/2=(lambda+1)(t_*-t_j), and ud-z^w=-2lambda A_j(t_*-t_j);\nsubstitution of the displayed C_p,C_z supplies the remaining coefficients.\nThus (34) concerns all original gradients and includes every right aggregate,\nbridge and terminal cross term.\n\nThe bracket in (34) is positive definite in Y,Z. Its first diagonal is\nlambda+1, and its determinant divided by A_j^2 is\n(lambda+1)(2+t_*+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0. It vanishes on common gradients\nbecause Y=Z=0. Consequently P_j e_j=0 at every depth, where e_j is the\nall-ones gradient vector. This is an exact matrix-kernel identity obtained\nby induction from (34), without claiming PSD.\n\n### Signs and the first three levels\n\nLet v_h=a/(c-2). Since c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta_word,\n0<v_h<1. Symbolic induction gives\n\n    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,\n    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                       (35)\n\nFor our m>=1536, c<5/2<3, hence d_j<A_j. The ratios t_j increase strictly.\nThe first values are\n\n    A_0=1,d_0=a; A_1=2+a,d_1=beta_word;\n    A_2=4+a(2+c),d_2=beta_word c.\n\nHere t_0<t_*, and\nlambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.\nAt level2 the exact factorization is\n\n    c(lambda d_2-A_2)\n       =epsilon[(lambda+4)-(4lambda+2)epsilon-(5lambda+2)epsilon^2].  (36)\n\nFor 2<lambda<3 and epsilon<=1/12, the bracket exceeds\n6-14/12-17/144>4>c. Thus lambda d_2-A_2>epsilon, t_2>t_*,\nand t_j>t_* for all j>=2. It follows that R_0,R_1 are PSD but R_j is\nnegative semidefinite for j>=2. This is a statement about the signed join\ncorrections; the full matrices continue to be the sums in (34).\n\nThe weights u_j,v_j^w,z_j^w,k_j are positive. At j=0, p_j^w=4+2a>0.\nAt j=1, A_1<lambda implies p_j^w>2lambda-2>0. At j>=2,\np_j^w>2lambda. Thus every original finite multiplier produced by (32)\nis nonnegative. At epsilon=0 all t_j=t_* and (31)-(32) reduce exactly to\nthe accepted original-Silver primitive gluing weights u=v=p^w=2lambda,\nz^w=2A,k=lambda^2. At positive epsilon the full first two levels also\npass PSD because R_0,R_1>=0. The obstruction below is not a failure of\nthese starting calibrations.\n\nComplete the finite array to the ORIGINAL objective by setting\n\n    K^0_ij=Lambda^m_ij/D, a_i^0=2h_i/D for i<n,\n    a_n^0=2(S+1)/D, b_i^0=0.                             (37)\n\nIts entire quadratic is\n\n    M^0(1/D)=[||x_0-sum_i v_i g_i||^2+P_m(g)]/D.         (38)\n\nTo check (38) directly, expand x_n=x_0-sum_(i<n)h_i g_i in\n\n    ||x_0||^2-2D f_n+sum Lambda^m Q\n                 +2sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,star)+2(S+1)Q_(n,star).\n\nAfter using the definition of V_m, its value coefficients cancel and it\nis P_m+||x_n-(S+1)g_n||^2. These are exactly the square and weights in\n(38). This is also the objective completion in accepted fact042, with no\nnew primitive inequality assumed. Policy II adds (17) to this full array.\n\n## 8. A rightmost original direction defeats ALL balanced-cycle repairs\n\nLet w be the original gradient vector that equals1 on the first four\nqueries of the RIGHTMOST H_3 subword and0 elsewhere. Its support is\n{n-7,n-6,n-5,n-4}; every one of these is an original physical query.\nIn that H_3, all left-child gradients including its bridge are1 and all\nright-child gradients including its terminal are0. Therefore the two\nembedded P_2 values vanish and (34) gives\n\n    P_3(w)=A_2^2-2A_2 d_2-d_2^2=-E_2,\n    E_2=(lambda d_2-A_2)(A_2+d_2/lambda)>epsilon.        (39)\n\nAt every enclosing join j>=3, the support stays in its right child;\nits bridge and terminal gradients are0 and its left block is0. Since\nR_j<=0, repeated substitution in the exact identity (34) gives\n\n    P_m(w)<=-E_2 product_(j=3)^(m-1) k_j.                (40)\n\nThis is a symbolic inequality, not an executed recurrence. For j>=2,\n(31) gives k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j>lambda^2>5.\nAlso (35) gives D=2A_m-1<2c^m<2(5/2)^m. Consequently\n\n    P_m(w)/D < -2^m/(1000m).                            (41)\n\nIndeed E_2>epsilon=1/(4m), and\n5^(m-3)/[2(5/2)^m]=2^m/250. All inequalities retain the fixed enclosing m.\n\nLet T=sum_i v_i w_i=A_2+d_2, since the support contains a complete H_2\nand its next physical bridge 1+d_2. Because a<1/2,c<5/2,\nA_2<25/4,d_2<5/2, so 0<T<9. Put theta_w=T/D and use the FULL original\nassignment\n\n    x_0=0, g=w-theta_w e_m.                              (42)\n\nHere 0<theta_w<1 since D>15. The square in (38) vanishes:\nsum_i v_i g_i=T-theta_w D=0. Moreover P_m e_m=0, so P_m(g)=P_m(w).\nIncreasing only q adds zero on this direction because x_0=0.\n\nIt remains to price EVERY balanced cycle, not just the previously displayed\nnegative direction's diagonal. Its terminal gradient is an\ninterpolation variable, not an extra update. The zero-perturbation calibration\nmeans epsilon=0, a=1/lambda, c=lambda, with the same recursive physical word;\nit is explicitly distinguished from the assigned positive epsilon.\n\nAccepted inputs read in this round are:\n\n* fact1252b8823bb5a290: the full original interpolation convention,\n  objective certificate, terminal reduction and full-coordinate scope;\n* fact04219554e41d9750: the unconditional original benchmark and the full\n  primitive/history and objective identities, Sections10--11;\n* fact5fd396eba75651e7: ONLY its scalar benchmark comparison on these words,\n  Section10. Data242f8d453ac26183\nmotivate considering changed finite blocks, but no floating-point equality,\ncoefficient, zero pattern, sign, rank or feasibility premise is taken from them.\n\nUse normalized smoothness1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. (2)\n\nIts original matrix is [[q,-nu^T/2],[-nu/2,C]]. Every entry is\n\n    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(j>i)K_ij,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                             +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j, (3)\n    out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij.\n\nTo check (2)--(3), each optimum-incoming row contributes its displayed\ninitial-point mixed term and its gradient position term. Difference squares and the two\npossible position-row source indices give exactly the off-diagonal in (3).\nNo original coordinate or interpolation pair is removed.\n\nWhen q>0, square completion gives\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0-sum_i nu_i g_i/(2q)||^2\n                       +sum_(i,j)[C_ij-nu_i nu_j/(4q)]<g_i,g_j>. The equation\n2Ce=D nu is therefore exactly (6). Equations (1),(6) are algebraic contact\nconditions, not a positivity assertion.\n\n## 2. Exact common-gradient compression with arbitrary finite redistribution\n\nConsider ANY last join (H_j,d,H_j), with k=2^j-1 and physical bridge\nd=1+a*c^j. This proves (7)--(9) for arbitrary final finite arrays,\nincluding signed changes to their predecessors.\n\nFor xi=0, q=1/D, full PSD would force (D,1,1) into the kernel of (9).\nIt follows that\n\n    pi_B-tF-pi_F=2S nu_L-2t. This is only a necessary two-block statement. The\nexplicit lift in the next section shows why it is not sufficient.\n\n## 3. Inserting (12) gives zero left flow.\nRight flow is (2*1_(i=k)-mu_i)+theta mu_i+s mu_i=2*1_(i=k).\nThis proves (1) on every original label, including the terminal.\n\nThis law does not keep the old initial-left-sample anchor or its old right\nattachment scale. Its backward moment is pi_B=2s A, not the frozen moment\nin the prior additive-cycle screens. Its compressed entries are\n\n    nu_L=2s, nu_R=2theta, F=0,\n    Gamma_L=2s(A+1), Gamma_R=D_c,\n    kappa= D_c-D theta^2.                                (13)\n\nThey satisfy (10). The entire coarse matrix has the explicit squares\n\n    ||x-D(su+theta v)||^2/D + kappa||u-v||^2.             (14)\n\nThe positive coarse curvature is a real change, not a renamed Schur cone.\nFor the actual final joins, kappa>0: multiplication by V^2 gives\n\n    V^2 kappa=2A^3-4Ad^2-2d^3+5A^2+8Ad+3d^2.           (15)\n\nWhen 0<d/A<1/2, the cubic part is\n2A^3[1-2(d/A)^2-(d/A)^3]>0, and the quadratic part is positive.\nThe actual final words satisfy this ratio at m>=1536, as proved in Section7.\n\n### The ACTUAL full lift, not only (14)\n\nLet P_j be the complete source gradient polynomial of Section4. Let C^c\nbe the full child gradient block from (3), and define the algebraic child\nremainder\n\n    R^c(g_R)=sum_(i,v)[C^c_iv-D_c mu_i mu_v/4]<g_(R_i),g_(R_v)>,\n    r_mu=sum_i mu_i g_(R_i), p=L_k, x_p=x_0-sum_(l<k)h_l g_(L_l).\n\nR^c is a quadratic DEFINITION; its PSD is not assumed. Direct substitution\nof ALL rows (12) into (2) gives the FULL original identity\n\n    M_I(1/D)=P_j(g_L)/D+R^c(g_R)\n       +||x_p+(A-Ds)g_p-(D theta/2)r_mu||^2/D\n       +(kappa/4)||r_mu-2g_p||^2.                        (16)\n\nFor a detailed coefficient check, the left source identity and its canonical\noptimum completion produce P_j/D and the square\n||x_p-(A+1)g_p||^2/D. Replace the child's right optimum rows by\ntheta Q_(R_i,star)+s Q_(R_i,p). Their difference from Q_(R_i,star)\nis s[-f_p+<g_(R_i),x_p-g_p>+||g_p||^2/2]. The child right starting\nposition is x_p-d g_p. After value cancellation and after its initial-point\nsquare is subtracted, the remaining three-variable quadratic is\n\n    ||x_p||^2/D+2(A/D-s)<x_p,g_p>+(A^2/D+2s)||g_p||^2\n       -theta<x_p,r_mu>+(d-s)<g_p,r_mu>+D_c||r_mu||^2/4. (17)\n\nCompleting the x_p square yields (16): the remaining g_p diagonal equals\nkappa, since sV=t, and its g_p,r_mu coefficient is -kappa. The r_mu\ndiagonal is kappa/4. This verifies every term, including the original\ninitial-point mixed terms, both terminals and every within-block direction.\n\nEquation (16) also proves recursive algebraic contact: P_j annihilates\ncommon gradients by Section4, a contacting child has R^c e=0, and both\ndisplayed squared linear forms vanish at the full Huber vector. Thus the\nparent annihilates that vector without any PSD assumption. Starting from\nthe empty child, (12) defines an actual flow/contact array at every depth.\n\n### Feasible original-Silver calibration\n\nAt epsilon=0, Section4 gives P_j=0 identically. Also\nd=1+(A+1)/lambda. Put u=1/lambda, so u^2+2u=1 and1/4<u<1/2.\nSubstituting d=uA+1+u into (15) gives\n\n    V^2 kappa=2(1-u)A^3+(6-8u)A^2\n                     +(1+u)(8u-2)A+(1+u)^2(1-2u)>0.   (18)\n\nEvery displayed coefficient is positive, including at A=0. Starting from\nthe empty full certificate ||x_0-g_0||^2, (16) inductively gives full PSD\nat every original-Silver depth: Schur completion of a PSD child at positive\n1/D_c makes R^c PSD; all other terms are positive squares. This is a joint\ncalibration proof, not a claimed fact for the perturbed words.\n\nFor every actual enclosing m, the one-step base is also fully PSD:\n\n    M_1(1/(1+2b))=||x_0-(1+2b)(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/(1+2b)\n                                      +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4,\n    b=1+a<sqrt(2)<3/2.                                 (19)\n\nNevertheless (16) has an unpaid full left source polynomial. Section4 gives\nan explicit actual original direction where every term except P_j/D\nvanishes and P_j<0. Thus (12) fails despite (14), without relying on any\nunproved recursive child feasibility.\n\n## 4. Complete source array and a zero-boundary H4 direction\n\nIn this section use capacity notation\n\n    A_j=1+sum H_j, d_j=a*c^j,\n    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, physical level-j bridge=1+d_j.\n\nHere d_j is the bridge EXCESS, different from d in Section3. For terminal z\ndefine the original history polynomial\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<z)h_i(f_i-f_z)\n              +sum_(i<z)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-A_j(A_j-1)||g_z||^2.\n\nFrom Lambda^0=0, at each join with bridge p and terminal z, embed the\nentire left array with weight1 and right array with weight k_j, and add\n\n    u_j sum_(i right, i<z)h_i Q_(i,p)\n      +v_j sum_(i right, i<z)h_i Q_(i,z)\n      +w_j Q_(z,p)+z_j Q_(p,z),                         (20)\n    u_j=2lambda,\n    v_j=[u_j d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,\n    k_j=1+(u_j+v_j)/2,\n    z_j=2A_j,\n    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-u_j(A_j-1)\n                         =2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda).\n\nAll repetitions in (20) are aggregated onto their actual directed row. This\ndefines Lambda^j, not an executed recurrence. Define P_j by\n\n    V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv=P_j(g).                    (21)\n\nThe values cancel: writing A=A_j,d=d_j,s_c=A-1, the two required\nidentities are u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and u_j s_c+w_j-z_j=2(A+d).\nThese imply source flow out-in=-2h_i at nonterminals and2(A-1) at\nthe terminal. No optimum row is in this finite source array.\n\nFor a full quadratic audit, write r=sum of actual right steps times their\nnonterminal gradients, T=2A+d, K=A(A-1), K_plus=T(T-1). The four\ngradient polynomials in the order of (20), BEFORE multiplication, are\n\n    d<r,g_p>+||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2+s_c||g_p||^2/2;\n    -||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2+s_c||g_z||^2/2-<r,g_z>;\n    d<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;\n    -(d+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r>-<g_p,g_z>.\n\nAdding the history differences gives, with t_j=d_j/A_j, t_star=1/lambda,\nY=r-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z, the exact full identity\n\n    P_(j+1)=P_j^left+k_j P_j^right+E_j,\n    E_j=(t_star-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>\n                                    +A_j^2(2+t_star+t_j)||Z||^2]. (22)\n\nFor example the ||r||^2 coefficient is (u_j-v_j)/2, the r,g_p\ncoefficient is u_j d_j-z_j, and the r,g_z coefficient is w_j-v_j.\nThey respectively equal (lambda+1)(t_star-t_j),\n-2lambda A_j(t_star-t_j), and the coefficient obtained by expanding Y,Z\nin (22). The bridge diagonal before substitution is\nK-d-d^2+(u_j s_c+w_j)/2-(d+1/2)z_j, and the terminal diagonal is\n-K_plus+k_j K+v_j s_c/2+(w_j+z_j)/2. Substitution supplies precisely\nthe two diagonals of (22). This checks all coefficients.\n\nThe bracket in (22) is positive definite in Y,Z. Its determinant divided by\nA_j^2 is (lambda+1)(2+t_star+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0; its first\ndiagonal is positive. Every common-gradient vector is annihilated by P_j,\nby (22) and the empty base, because Y=Z=0 for those vectors.\n\nTo establish the actual ratios and every sign without using a previous\nunverified obstruction, put v_h=a/(c-2). Since c(c-2)>1>beta,\n0<v_h<1, and symbolic substitution into the physical sum equation gives\n\n    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,\n    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                     (23)\n\nThus t_j increases strictly. Its first three levels obey\n\n    A_0=1,d_0=a; A_1=2+a,d_1=beta;\n    A_2=4+a(2+c),d_2=beta c,\n    t_0<t_star, t_1<t_star, t_2>t_star.                  (24)\n\nFor the middle sign,\nlambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.\nFor the last, elementary expansion gives\n\n    c(lambda d_2-A_2)=epsilon[(lambda+4)-(4lambda+2)epsilon\n                                            -(5lambda+2)epsilon^2]>0. (25)\n\nFor 2<lambda<3 and epsilon<=1/12 its bracket exceeds\n6-14/12-17/144>0. Consequently t_j>t_star for every j>=2.\nThe weights u_j,v_j,z_j,k_j are positive. At j=0, w_j=4+2a>0;\nat j=1, A_1<lambda makes w_j>2lambda-2>0;\nat j>=2, w_j>2lambda. Every final source multiplier is nonnegative.\nAt epsilon=0, all t_j=t_star and (22) gives P_j=0 identically,\nin agreement with fact042's original primitive identity.\n\nNow take an ORIGINAL H3 gradient vector w with entries1 at0,1,2,3\nand0 at4,5,6,7. Both children in (22) have zero P_2 value: the left is\ncommon-gradient, the right is zero. Its full value is\n\n    P_3(w)=A_2^2-2A_2d_2-d_2^2=-E,\n    E=(lambda d_2-A_2)(A_2+d_2/lambda)>0.                (26)\n\nThe physical displacement on this support is T_w=A_2+d_2. In H4 set\n\n    v_i=1 at i=0,1,2,3;\n    v_i=-1 at i=8,9,10,11;\n    v_i=0 at EVERY other index0,...,15.                 (27)\n\nThese are actual query indices. The bridge7 and terminal15 are zero.\nThe two signed supports have the identical physical steps, so\nsum_(i<15)h_i v_i=0. At its H4 join, Y=-T_w,Z=0, so (22) gives\n\n    P_4(v)=-(1+k_3)E-(t_3-t_star)(lambda+1)T_w^2<0.      (28)\n\nFor any H_j with j>=4, place (27) in its FIRST actual H4 subword and set\nevery later gradient to zero. At each enclosing source join the root,\nterminal and right block are zero. Equation (22) then gives exactly\nP_j(v)=P_4(v), not a conjectured or numerical amplification.\n\nFinally at the top join of H_m, set x_0=0, give its left H_(m-1) these\ngradients, and set every right gradient, including the actual terminal, to\nzero. For every m>=5 the original left terminal is zero, its displacement\nis zero, and x_p=0. Hence EVERY term of (16) except its first vanishes:\n\n    M_I(q)=P_4(v)/D<0 for EVERY real objective level q.  (29)\n\nChanging q contributes only (q-1/D)||x_0||^2=0. This is the promised\nfull lift failure after a viable original-Silver baseline and a positive\nactual coarse block. It identifies a within-child direction invisible to\nits terminal, total displacement and the common-gradient compression.\n\n## 5. Screen II: explicit signed replacement by a backward bridge fan\n\nThe next law removes the left finite source rather than trying to pay it.\nRecursively use its own child objective array (K^c,mu) with sum mu=2,\nstarting from empty K=0,mu_0=2. At a join use the same A,d,V,s,theta as\nSection3, and specify\n\n    v_l=2h_l/(2S-p_l), l<k,\n    K_(L_k,L_l)=v_l, with ALL other left finite rows zero;\n    K_(R_i,R_v)=K^c_iv,\n    K_(R_i,L_k)=s mu_i, all other cross rows zero;\n    nu_(L_l)=v_l for l<k,\n    nu_(L_k)=2s-sum_(l<k)v_l,\n    nu_(R_i)=theta mu_i, xi=0.                          (30)\n\nRelative to an inherited left child, its signed finite change is\n-K^c plus the displayed fan. No original row is deleted from the model;\nevery FINAL multiplier has just been specified. This replacement explicitly\nacts on the failing inherited H4 directions of Screen I.\n\nEvery denominator is at least V=2S-A>0. Therefore\nsum v_l<=2A/V and nu_(L_k)>=2d/V>0. All other final signs hold.\nAt L_l< L_k, incoming fan weight v_l is canceled by its optimum attachment.\nAt the bridge, its outgoing fan plus its optimum attachment equals incoming\ncross mass2s. The right flow is the child flow as in Screen I. Thus all\noriginal value coefficients vanish.\n\nThis law is also algebraically Huber-contacting. At l<k equation (6) reads\n(2S-p_l)v_l=2h_l. On the right, subtract the child version of (6):\ntheta(3A+d-p_i)mu_i=(2A-p_i)mu_i+s(d+p_i)mu_i, since theta V=2A+d.\nThe initial-point derivative vanishes because total attachment mass is2.\nAt the Huber vector M(D,e)=0. Homogeneity then forces the only remaining\nbridge derivative to vanish. This proves (6) at the bridge also, without\nasserting PSD. For j=0 the law agrees exactly with (19), so the actual\none-step base passes.\n\nIt fails an elementary original-coordinate test before any long-depth proof.\nFor l<k, (3) gives EXACTLY C_(L_l,L_l)=nu_(L_l)=v_l: there is no\nfinite outgoing row at that index. For q>0 choose only this gradient to be1\nand choose x_0=v_l/(2q); every other original gradient is zero. Then\n\n    M_II(q)=v_l-v_l^2/(4q).                             (31)\n\nThus a necessary condition for full PSD is q>=v_l/4. At q=zeta/D it is\n\n    zeta>=D h_l/[2(2S-p_l)]>h_l/2.                     (32)\n\nNonpositive q cannot be PSD because the original mixed entry -v_l/2 is\nnonzero. The same one-gradient test is part of the whole matrix, not a\npostulated child or quotient.\n\nAlready at the original-Silver H3 calibration, the left child has a physical\nstep h_l=2 at p_l=sqrt(2). Its D=1+2S>2S-p_l, so (32) demands\nzeta>1. It therefore fails this required sharp baseline after its valid\none-step base; no long recursive PSD argument is undertaken for it.\n\nThe same exact test also directly excludes the desired actual target levels.\nAt the final assigned join choose the root of its left H_(m-1), namely\nl=2^(m-2)-1. Its actual step is h_l=1+a*c^(m-2), which grows without\nbound with m. Equation (32) excludes every bounded whole-word zeta in this\nlaw. More explicitly (23) gives S_m<c^m; at m>=1536, a>3/10 and c<5/2.\nTherefore\n\n    q>=v_l/4=h_l/[2(2S_m-p_l)]>h_l/(4S_m)\n                  >a/(4c^2)>3/250.                    (33)\n\nThe fixed-step original-coordinate test thus excludes any vanishing\ncertificate level through this fan. It is a restriction on THESE levels,\nnot on W. No feasibility at any higher level is asserted.\n\n## 6. Screen III: retain the objective block, correct moments, reroute right rows\n\nThe third law retains its own entire left OBJECTIVE finite block, rather than\nScreen I's primitive block or Screen II's deletion. Its base arrays have\nxi=0 and start from K=0,mu_0=2 at H_0. At each join set\n\n    K_(L_l,L_v)=K^c_lv + 2s mu_l*1_(v=k,l<k),\n    K_(R_i,R_v)=K^c_iv,\n    K_(R_i,L_k)=s mu_i, all other cross rows zero,\n    nu_(L_l)=(A/V)mu_l for l<k,\n    nu_(L_k)=(2d+A mu_k)/V,\n    nu_(R_i)=theta mu_i, xi=0.                          (34)\n\nThis changes every potentially deficient left source individually. To see\nwhere it comes from, retain the left finite block with scalar weight gamma\nand add u_l to L_l->L_k and v_l to L_k->L_l. Value flow gives\nnu_l=gamma mu_l-u_l+v_l. Its contact equation (6) requires\n\n    V u_l-(2S-p_l)v_l\n                   =2gamma t mu_l-2(1-gamma)h_l.        (35)\n\nThe two EXPLICIT choices tested in this draft are gamma=0 (30) and gamma=1\n(34), with respectively u_l=0,v_l=2h_l/(2S-p_l), and\nu_l=2s mu_l,v_l=0. Equation (35) is a diagnostic linking the laws, not\na proposed unsolved feasibility theorem for other gamma. There is no scan\nover gamma and no additional fourth law.\n\nAll signs in (34) hold, since A/V=1-2s>=0 and2d/V=6s-2>0.\nChild finite flow, the displayed additions and attachments give zero left\nflow at each nonterminal and at the bridge; right flow is unchanged as in\n(12). The total left optimum mass is2s, the right mass2theta, hence total2.\nEquation (35), the child contact equations, and the same remaining-bridge\nhomogeneity argument as for (30) prove algebraic contact at every depth.\nThe first physical step again agrees with the full PSD base (19).\n\nBefore its final paid rerouting, this law also has exactly the coarse squares\n(14), since its cross totals and left-destination moment equal those in\n(13). Thus the positive actual coarse assignment does not detect its\nisolated-gradient diagonal failure either.\n\nThe left forward correction has a position cost that the compression does\nnot see. For every l<k, the ENTIRE original diagonal in (3) is\n\n    C_parent_(L_l,L_l)=C^c_ll-2s h_l mu_l.              (36)\n\nIndeed the new optimum attachment decreases by2s mu_l while the finite\noutgoing total increases by exactly that amount, leaving out_l unchanged.\nThe additional forward row to L_k subtracts2s h_l mu_l from the position\ndiagonal. This calculation includes all original rows.\n\nAt the first nontrivial join H2, let b=1+a be the single child step and\nd=1+beta=2-epsilon the new physical bridge. Its child has mu_0=mu_1=1,\nC^c_00=1, so\n\n    C_parent_00=1-2b(b+d)/(3b+2d)<0.                   (37)\n\nFor the zero-perturbation source calibration, b=sqrt(2),d=2 and\ns=sqrt(2)-1; (37) equals2sqrt(2)-3<0 exactly.\nFor the actual enclosing m>=1536, epsilon<1/128 and lambda<17/7 give\na>889/2193>2/5. Thus7/5<b<sqrt(2)<3/2 and d>3/2. The numerator\nof 2b(b+d)/(3b+2d)-1 is\n\n    b(2b-3)+2d(b-1)>-3/10+6/5>0,\n\nwhich proves the actual strict sign in (37). Setting x_0=0,g_0=1 and\nEVERY other original gradient zero gives M(q)=C_00<0 for any real q.\nThis kills the law at H2 after its successful one-step base; no putative\nall-depth PSD proof is used.\n\nThe same diagonal failure persists at all actual larger depths. Original\nindex0 stays in the left child, and (36) subtracts a nonnegative quantity\nat each enclosing join. Hence C^m_00<=C^2_00<0 for every m>=2.\nThese are symbolic coefficient identities, never executed recurrences.\n\n### A fully specified signed, nonscalar right-terminal redistribution\n\nTo exercise the additional freedom that the right finite block need not be\nan exact copy, include this change ONLY at the final join, AFTER constructing\nthe xi=0 base arrays (34). Set eta=1/100 and q=(1+eta)/D. Let\n\n    T_out=sum_(i<k)K^c_(k,i),\n    delta_i=eta K^c_(k,i)/[2(1+T_out)], i<k.\n\nReduce K_(R_k,R_i) by delta_i, increase nu_(R_k) by sum delta_i,\nand set xi_(R_i)=delta_i. All other final rows remain those of (34).\nThe change on each route is the signed polynomial\n\n    delta_i[Q_(R_k,star)+Q_(star,R_i)-Q_(R_k,R_i)]\n                  =delta_i<g_(R_k),g_(R_i)-x_(R_i)>.    (38)\n\nEach final finite entry is nonnegative because\ndelta_i/K^c_(k,i)=eta/[2(1+T_out)]<1 when that entry is positive;\nzero entries have delta_i=0. Each new optimum entry is nonnegative.\nFlow cancels exactly: at R_k its lost finite outgoing mass is replaced by\noptimum-incoming mass; at R_i its lost finite incoming mass is replaced by\noptimum-outgoing mass. Optimum flow also balances. This rerouting is not an\nadditive balanced optimum cycle on a frozen finite base.\n\nThe right-terminal outgoing rows change while its other right finite rows\nstay fixed. For right children of depth>=2, there are positive changed\nterminal rows and positive unchanged nonterminal rows, so the finite block\nis NOT a scalar copy. All actual target depths have this property.\n\nThe new rows have the exact Huber expenditure\n\n    price=sum_(i<k)delta_i(2S-P_(R_i))\n          <=2S sum_i delta_i<S eta<D eta.               (39)\n\nThus this is a genuine budget-valid original optimum-row payment at a\nsufficiently small advertised whole-word loss. It nevertheless leaves the\noriginal x_0=0,g_0=1,all-other-gradients0 test unchanged: every right\ngradient is zero, including R_k, so (38) vanishes term by term. Its final\nfull value remains C^m_00<0, independently of q. This supplies an actual\nsigned finite change and terminal reweighting without hiding their price,\nand demonstrates precisely why changing only those right rows is insufficient\nfor this law's left defect. The mutation is final-only, so no xi!=0 child is\nsilently fed into the xi=0 recursion (34).\n\n## 7. Actual physical bounds and original benchmark scope\n\nThe physical sum formula in (23) implies, for j=m-1,\n\n    A=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2), d=1+a*c^j.\n\nAt m>=1536,12/5<c<49/20<5/2 and a>3/10. For the upper bound use\nlambda<17/7 and epsilon<1/256, giving c<4369/1792<49/20.\nLet z=(2/c)^j. Since (5/6)^4<1/2 and j>=24, z<1/64.\nDropping the positive term2^j-1 from A gives\n\n    A>(d-1)(1-z)/(c-2)>(35/16)(d-1)>2d,\n\nwhere d>12 follows from a>3/10,c>2,j>=6. This proves d/A<1/2\nand the strict coarse positivity used in (15). No child parameter was reset.\n\nFor the original all-split benchmark, fact042 defines\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nAll integer splits are retained. Fact5fd gives on these SAME tau1/4 words\n\n    B_(2^m-1)/D<237/250 for EVERY m>=1536.\n\nA valid full upper W<=zeta/D with uniform zeta<250/237 would therefore\ngive the fixed improvement1-237zeta/250 at true horizons2^m-1.\nFor example zeta=101/100 would give1-23937/25000=1063/25000.\nThis is only the target arithmetic, NOT an obtained upper. Laws I and III\nfail for EVERY q; Law II forces divergent relative loss and (33). None\nsupplies a certificate to which that benchmark comparison can be applied.\n\nNo local failure is turned into a W lower bound. No per-level loss is\nsubstituted for a whole-word constant, and no unverified numerical pilot or\nspecial witness-family optimum is an upper bound.\n\n## 8. Missing mechanism and bounded-round handoff\n\nThe exact compression exposes which moments must change. Screen I DOES\nchange them and has an explicit positive coarse assignment; its full lift\n(16) still contains a negative zero-boundary within-child direction (27).\nThat direction has zero terminal gradient and zero weighted displacement,\nso a correction depending only on those two child interfaces cannot see it.\n\nScreen II removes that finite block explicitly. Its replacement gives too\nlittle isolated-gradient diagonal compared with the original initial-point\nmixed coefficient, producing (31)--(33). Screen III retains the objective\nfinite block and enforces contact through actual rows, but those forward rows\nhave the negative position cost (36). Its concrete right-terminal signed\nrerouting has a valid paid budget and fails to act on that left direction.\n\nThus the missing lift must change actual within-child finite rows so that\nthe zero-boundary innovations AND isolated physical large-step directions\nhave paid curvature, without the negative early diagonal induced here. It\nmust do so jointly with the coarse moments and all final multiplier signs.\nThis is a substantive matrix construction. It is not provided by (9), by\nflow/contact, or by an assertion that an arbitrary Schur problem is feasible.\n\nExactly THREE finite-row laws were screened. It does not renew itself.\n\n## 1. What was screened, and what the conclusions mean\n\nThree explicit laws leave the old aligned affine elapsed-clock class.\n\n1. The original word, all directed rows, and an explicit data convention\n\nNormalize L=1 and take the optimum sample x_star=g_star=f_star=0.\nFor arbitrary finite h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) >=0 define\n\n    S=sum_(t<n)h_t, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, q_i=P_i/S.        (1)\n\nFor S>0, q_0=0, q_n=1, and q_(i+1)-q_i=h_i/S. Zero steps\nrepeat q and must repeat positions, gradients and values. All expressions\nbelow are written finite sums; none was executed by a mathematical program.\nFor S=0 one can use the separate static fallback F(x)=||x||^2/2,\nx_0=e0, with every iterate unchanged, unique minimum0 and payoff1.\nThat degenerate fallback repairs no failure on the positive-mass words.\n\nFor any displayed normalized gradient-vector law v_i and scale sigma>0,\nuse the complete original physical arrays\n\n    g_i=sigma*v_i,\n    D_i^nc=sum_(t<i)h_t*v_t,\n    x_i=e0-sigma*D_i^nc,                 0<=i<=n.     (2)\n\nThese satisfy x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i, including every zero step.\nThe original signed interpolation residual, with its original orientation,\nis\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.       (3)\n\nEVERY ordered distinct pair in {star,0,...,n} is retained. In particular,\nno incoming terminal or remote queried-prefix row is discarded.\n\nHere is an explicit common value convention for attempted laws1 and2:\n\n    C_i^val=sum_(t=i)^(n-1) (h_t/2)*<v_t+v_(t+1),v_t>,\n    f_i=tau_val+sigma^2*C_i^val, C_n^val=0.           (4)\n\nFor these values every finite ordered row is EXACTLY\n\n    Q_ij/sigma^2 = C_i^val-C_j^val\n                  +<v_i,D_i^nc-D_j^nc>+||v_i-v_j||^2/2. (5)\n\nBoth optimum directions are EXACTLY\n\n    Q_(i,star)=tau_val-sigma*<v_i,e0>\n        +sigma^2*(C_i^val+<v_i,D_i^nc>+||v_i||^2/2),\n    Q_(star,i)=-tau_val+sigma^2*(||v_i||^2/2-C_i^val). (6)\n\nThus the full optimum-value interval, not only the terminal upper row, is\n\n    lower_i=sigma^2*(||v_i||^2/2-C_i^val),\n    upper_i=sigma*<v_i,e0>\n         -sigma^2*(C_i^val+<v_i,D_i^nc>+||v_i||^2/2),\n    max_i lower_i <= tau_val <= min_i upper_i. For cycles involving star,\nuse(5),(6), where tau_val also telescopes. No unknown expectation enters.\n\nOnly if ALL those signs pass may the accepted interpolation construction\nbe invoked. Explicitly, with the zero jet included, put\n\n    K(v)=max{||v||^2/2,\n             max_(0<=i<=n)[<x_i,v>-f_i+||v-g_i||^2/2]},\n    F(x)=sup_v[<x,v>-K(v)]. The Gram is the Gram of the actual displayed Euclidean vectors.\nIf additionally tau_val>max_i lower_i, every nonzero-jet quadratic is\nstrictly below the zero-jet quadratic at v=0. Finitely many continuous\nquadratics then leave K(v)=||v||^2/2 in a neighborhood of0. The\nsubdifferential of K at0 is {0}, so argmin F={0}. Consequently the true\nclosest initial distance is ||e0||=1, and smoothness1 is sharp locally.\n\nLaws1 and2 require that strict condition for a claimed TRUE-radius1\nrealization. Thus changing(4) cannot repair the stated performance\nfailures. If a different realization has a closer minimum, the present\norigin-radius upper bound is not asserted for that different normalization.\n\nFor advertised L>0 use L*F and physical steps h_i/L. The normalized\npayoff is still 2f_n/dist(x0,argmin F)^2, exactly W=2R.\n\n## 3. Law1: a parabolic mean and a transverse sign reversal\n\nIn R^2, with orthonormal e0,e1, choose the fixed coefficients\n\n    delta_par=1/4, nu_par=27/8, b_par=9/10,\n    v_i^par=(1-delta_par*q_i^2)e0\n                         +sqrt(nu_par)*(q_i-b_par)e1. (9)\n\nUse(2) for all positions. Written componentwise these are\n\n    x_i=[1-sigma*P_i+sigma*delta_par*U_i]e0\n                       -sigma*sqrt(nu_par)*(J_i-b_par*P_i)e1,\n    U_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*q_t^2, J_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*q_t.     (10)\n\nUse(4)--(7) for one explicit value array and its ALL-row/cycle costs.\nThe full gradient Gram and the initial-gradient correlations are\n\n    <e0,g_i>=sigma*(1-q_i^2/4),\n    <g_i,g_j>=sigma^2*[(1-q_i^2/4)(1-q_j^2/4)\n                         +(27/8)(q_i-9/10)(q_j-9/10)]. (11)\n\nThis is a real PSD rank2 Gram, not independent directions. At i=0 the\ntransverse component is negative and nonzero. At i=n it is positive.\nThe longitudinal mean is quadratic in elapsed mass, so this is not\nanother scalar retuning of the affine-clock law. Initial gradient and\ninitial radius are not aligned.\n\n### 3.1 The original optimum-to-terminal path pays the gain\n\nConsider ANY common values which make these gradients and positions legal\nwith the origin optimum and x0=e0. The row Q_(0,star)<=0 gives\n\n    f_0<=sigma-sigma^2*||v_0^par||^2/2.\n\nFor every physical t, the actual original row Q_(t+1,t)<=0 gives\n\n    f_(t+1)<=f_t-h_t*sigma^2*<v_(t+1)^par,v_t^par>\n                              -sigma^2*||v_(t+1)^par-v_t^par||^2/2.\n\nSumming ALL these rows, including the terminal row at t=n-1, yields\n\n    2f_n<=2sigma-D_path*sigma^2,\n    D_path=||v_0^par||^2\n       +2sum_t h_t*<v_(t+1)^par,v_t^par>\n       +sum_t||v_(t+1)^par-v_t^par||^2.              (12)\n\nThis is the concrete replacement for the terminal-only screen. It uses\nthe initial optimum row, every directed physical predecessor row and\nthe full finite-amplitude vector law. No value re-selection avoids it.\n\n### 3.2 Exact cross quadrature, without a trajectory evaluation\n\nFor a written partition interval [q,q+d] with 0<=q<=q+d<=1, define\n\n    v(q)=(1-delta_par*q^2)e0+sqrt(nu_par)*(q-b_par)e1.\n\nPolynomial integration gives the EXACT identity\n\n    integral_q^(q+d)||v(t)||^2 dt -d*<v(q),v(q+d)>\n       =d^3*[(delta_par+nu_par)/3\n                      +delta_par^2*(q^2+q*d+d^2/5)]. (13)\n\nFor the transverse term, this is the equality\nintegral(q-b_par)^2-d(q-b_par)(q+d-b_par)=d^3/3.\nFor the longitudinal term, expanding 1-2delta_par*t^2+delta_par^2*t^4\ngives delta_par*d^3/3+delta_par^2*d^3*(q^2+qd+d^2/5).\nThus(13) retains the entire error, not a Taylor remainder.\n\nSince q+d<=1, q^2+qd+d^2/5<=1. Also\n\n    integral_0^1||v(t)||^2dt\n       =1-2delta_par/3+delta_par^2/5\n                        +nu_par*(1/3-b_par+b_par^2)\n       =4001/2400>5/3,\n    (delta_par+nu_par)/3+delta_par^2=61/48<4/3.       (14)\n\nWrite d_t=h_t/S and d_max=max_t d_t. Because sum_t d_t=1,\nsum_t d_t^3<=d_max^2. Summing(13) over the EXACT original partition,\nwith zero intervals contributing zero, proves\n\n    (1/S)sum_t h_t*<v_t^par,v_(t+1)^par>\n                    >5/3-(4/3)*d_max^2.             (15)\n\nIf max h_t<=S/2, the right side is at least4/3. Furthermore\n||v_0^par||^2=1+nu_par*b_par^2>1 and the last sum in(12) is nonnegative.\nConsequently\n\n    D_path>1+(8/3)S,\n    2f_n<=1/D_path<1/[1+(8/3)S]<1/(1+2S)=H_base(h). (16)\n\nThe middle inequality follows by completing the square in(12):\n2sigma-D_path*sigma^2=1/D_path-D_path*(sigma-1/D_path)^2.\nThe bound is over ALL scales and ALL legal common values. In particular\nit also holds for every successful strict-radius1 realization in(8).\n\n### 3.3 Exact original benchmark comparison, at the actual horizon\n\nKeep the assigned enclosing parameters, without substituting those of a child:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),\n    c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=beta_word/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_word*c^j,H_j),\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, m>=1536.                         (17)\n\nThe word is a palindrome and its largest entry is the central\n1+a_word*c^(m-1). It satisfies max h_t<S/2. To check the latter\nwithout any mass computation, c<3 and m>=3 give\n\n    2S_(m-1)>=2*(1+a_word*c^(m-2))\n                         +4*(1+a_word*c^(m-3)),\n    2S_(m-1)-(1+a_word*c^(m-1))\n       >=5+a_word*c^(m-3)*(2c+4-c^2)>0.             (18)\n\nHere 2c+4-c^2>0 on 1<c<3. Since S=2S_(m-1)+h_central,\nthe desired largest-step comparison follows.\n\nThe ORIGINAL benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n with ALL split indices retained.\nAccepted5fd396eba75651e7, Section10, gives at THIS actual n\n\n    B_n*H_base(H_m)<237/250,                         (19)\n\nso every legal law1 radius1 payoff V satisfies the exact comparison\n\n    V/U_n^ZJ=B_n*V\n        < [B_n*H_base(H_m)]*(1+2S)/[1+(8/3)S]\n        <237/250<1.                                 (20)\n\nLaw1 therefore cannot match the benchmark even on this original full\nword. This rejects its fixed nonlinear coefficients; it excludes no\narbitrary nonlinear mean law or terminal longitudinal reversal.\n\n## 4. Law2: accumulated real finite-field covariance with initial memory\n\n### 4.1 The incidence design is a real simplex, with its metric proved\n\nChoose a prime p_ff such that N_ff=p_ff^2+p_ff+1>=n+1, and put\nd_ff=N_ff-1. Such a prime exists: any prime divisor of (n+1)!+1 is\ngreater than n+1. This is an existence argument, not a performed\nfactorial evaluation or prime search.\n\nOver the field Z/p_ff Z, projective points are nonzero triples modulo\nnonzero scalar multiples. Each class has p_ff-1 representatives, giving\nN_ff=(p_ff^3-1)/(p_ff-1) points. A projective line is a nonzero linear\nform modulo nonzero scalar multiples, with incidence defined by the\nvanishing of that form. There are N_ff lines as well.\n\nThe kernel of one nonzero linear form has p_ff^2 elements. Removing zero\nand passing to scalar classes gives p_ff+1 points per line. Two distinct\nline forms are linearly independent; their common kernel has dimension1,\nso their lines share exactly one projective point. These counts follow\nby eliminating a coordinate with a nonzero coefficient, and eliminating\ntwo coordinates for the pair. No external finite-geometry theorem is\nbeing imported.\n\nIn REAL R^(N_ff), let 1_L be the incidence vector of line L and 1 the\nall-ones vector. Put k_ff=p_ff+1 and\n\n    u_L^ff=[1_L-(k_ff/N_ff)*1]\n                     /sqrt(k_ff-k_ff^2/N_ff).        (21)\n\nTheir real inner products, by the proved counts, are exactly\n\n    ||u_L^ff||^2=1,\n    <u_L^ff,u_M^ff>=-1/d_ff for L!=M.               (22)\n\nIndeed the distinct numerator inner product is\n1-k_ff^2/N_ff=-p_ff/N_ff, while the squared denominator is\n(p_ff+1)*p_ff^2/N_ff. Their quotient is -1/[p_ff(p_ff+1)].\nTheir Gram is therefore PSD because these are explicitly real vectors;\nthere is no conversion of a finite-field bilinear form into an assumed\nEuclidean metric. For arbitrary real coefficients z_L the exact norm is\n\n    ||sum_L z_L*u_L^ff||^2\n      =[N_ff*sum_L z_L^2-(sum_L z_L)^2]/d_ff.        (23)\n\nIt is nonnegative also directly by Cauchy--Schwarz, extending a selected\nsubset's coefficients by zero to the N_ff lines.\n\nChoose n distinct step lines and one further line, denoted u_t^ff\n(0<=t<n) and u_extra^ff. Add a unit e0 orthogonal to R^(N_ff).\n\n### 4.2 An explicit nonlinear all-history law\n\nChoose sigma>0, nu_inc>0, 0<delta_inc<=1/2 and an ARBITRARY real\ninitial offset b_inc. Define\n\n    z_i^inc=b_inc*u_extra^ff+(1/S)sum_(t<i)h_t*u_t^ff,\n    v_i^inc=(1-delta_inc*q_i^2)e0+sqrt(nu_inc)*z_i^inc. (24)\n\nUse ALL physical arrays(2) and the full common-value convention(4)--(7).\nIn coordinates the positions are\n\n    x_i=[1-sigma*P_i+sigma*delta_inc*U_i]e0\n                        -sigma*sqrt(nu_inc)*sum_(t<i)h_t*z_t^inc,\n    U_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*q_t^2.                          (25)\n\nZero steps add no innovation, so repeated queries have identical gradients.\nThe covariance is time dependent and retains every earlier innovation.\nIt is not an independent fresh-gradient construction or an AR nearest-\nneighbor assumption. The initial gradient has a transverse component\nunless b_inc=0. Opposite temporal inner products are allowed.\n\nWrite E_i=sum_(t<i)h_t^2, and r_min=min(i,j). The exact covariance is\n\n    <z_i^inc,z_j^inc>=b_inc^2\n      -b_inc*(q_i+q_j)/d_ff\n      +[N_ff*E_(r_min)/S^2-q_i*q_j]/d_ff.            (26)\n\nThe entire Gram of (x0,g0,...,gn), including all initial correlations,\nis determined by(24)--(26), with\n\n    <x0,g_i>=sigma*(1-delta_inc*q_i^2),\n    <g_i,g_j>=sigma^2*[(1-delta_inc*q_i^2)\n                       *(1-delta_inc*q_j^2)\n                              +nu_inc*<z_i^inc,z_j^inc>]. (27)\n\nThe initial displacement is EXACTLY e0; a successful strict realization\nof(8) has unique minimum0 and true radius1. No minimum is moved without\nchanging these law hypotheses. All pair/cycle costs are(5),(6) with\n(24), not an expected sign or an unspecified combinatorial feasibility\nclaim.\n\n### 4.3 Complete terminal charge, with every covariance term retained\n\nLet A_inc=1-delta_inc>0 and set\n\n    U=sum_t h_t*q_t^2<=S/3,\n    T=sum_t h_t*P_t=(S^2-E)/2, E=sum_t h_t^2,\n    eta=T/S, K_inc=1+S+eta, M=1+2S.\n\nThe bound on U is the left-endpoint inequality on each interval of the\nnondecreasing function(P/S)^2, whose integral from0 to S is S/3.\nThe formula for T is obtained by expanding the square of sum h.\n\nThe original row Q_(n,star)<=0, for ANY legal common values, yields\n\n    2f_n<=2sigma*A_inc-D_inc*sigma^2,\n    D_inc=M*A_inc^2+2delta_inc*A_inc*(S-U)\n                                   +nu_inc*C_inc(b_inc),\n    C_inc(b)=||z_n^inc||^2+2sum_t h_t*<z_n^inc,z_t^inc>.\n                                                            (28)\n\nDirect use of(26) gives the complete initial-offset polynomial\n\n    C_inc(b)=M*b^2-2K_inc*b/d_ff+C_inc(0),\n    C_inc(0)=[N_ff*(E+2J_inc)/S^2-1-2eta]/d_ff,\n    J_inc=sum_t h_t^2*sum_(r>t)h_r.                 (29)\n\nNo positive and negative cross term has been discarded. In particular\nallowing initial transverse correlation can make C_inc(b) negative;\nasserting nonnegativity for every b would be a wrong shortcut.\n\n### 4.4 A palindrome identity pays the zero-offset part\n\nFor a palindrome h, reflection of the exact physical indices gives\n\n    2J_inc=S*E-F3, F3=sum_t h_t^3.                 (30)\n\nProof: sum h_t^2 times prefix mass equals the suffix-mass sum by\nreflection. Adding those sums gives sum h_t^2(S-h_t)=S E-F3.\n\nPut p_t=h_t/S, e=sum p_t^2, f=sum p_t^3. For ANY nonnegative\np_t with sum p_t=1 the following exact identity holds:\n\n    (n+1)e-nf-1\n       =sum_(i<j)(p_i-p_j)^2*(1-p_i-p_j)>=0.       (31)\n\nTo verify it, sum(p_i-p_j)^2=n e-1 and\nsum(p_i-p_j)^2(p_i+p_j)=n f-e. Subtract these two equalities.\nEach last factor is nonnegative because all remaining p's are nonnegative.\nSince N_ff>=n+1, e-f>=0 and N_ff e>=1, it follows that\n\n    (N_ff+1)e-N_ff f-1>=0, N_ff E-S^2>=0.\n\nMultiplying the numerator of C_inc(0) in(29) by S^2 and using(30)\ngives exactly\n\n    N_ff*E-S^2\n      +S*[(N_ff+1)E-N_ff*F3/S-S^2]>=0.             (32)\n\nThus C_inc(0)>=0 for EVERY palindrome, including zero entries. This is\na proved coefficient inequality, not covariance domination by an\nunverified temporal kernel.\n\n### 4.5 The first original two-row cycle pays a negative initial-offset charge\n\nLet h_lead be the first positive physical step. All earlier zero steps\nleave q=0, z=b_inc*u_extra^ff and x=e0. Summing the TWO original rows\nat this actual step cancels their values and gives exactly\n\n    Q_(lead,lead+1)+Q_(lead+1,lead)\n      =sigma^2*(h_lead/S)^2\n          [delta_inc^2*(h_lead/S)^2\n                    +nu_inc*(1-b_inc*S/d_ff)-delta_inc*h_lead]. (33)\n\nThe innovation has length h_lead/S, and its inner product with the\ninitial transverse offset is -b_inc/d_ff. This proves(33) directly,\nincluding the initial correlation term instead of presuming it vanishes.\nLegal original rows require the square bracket to be nonpositive.\n\nAssume the explicit screen conditions\n\n    h is a positive-mass palindrome, S>=2,\n    0<h_lead<=2, d_ff>=4, S<=d_ff^2/4.              (34)\n\nIf C_inc(b_inc)>=0, (28) immediately gives D_inc>M*A_inc^2.\nIf C_inc(b_inc)<0, (29),(32) imply\n\n    0<b_inc<2K_inc/(d_ff*M)<=2/d_ff,\n\nbecause eta<=S/2 makes K_inc<=M. Consequently\n1-b_inc*S/d_ff>=1-2S/d_ff^2>=1/2. The necessary original cycle(33)\nthen forces\n\n    delta_inc*h_lead>=nu_inc/2,\n    delta_inc>=nu_inc/4.                            (35)\n\nMeanwhile completing the square in(29) gives\n\n    C_inc(b_inc)>=-K_inc^2/(d_ff^2*M)>=-M/d_ff^2.\n\nSince A_inc>=1/2, S-U>=2S/3 and S>=2, the COMPLETE correction in(28)\nis bounded below, using(35), by\n\n    2delta_inc*A_inc*(S-U)+nu_inc*C_inc(b_inc)\n      >=nu_inc*S/6-nu_inc*M/d_ff^2\n      >=nu_inc*S*(1/6-5/32)=nu_inc*S/96>0.          (36)\n\nHere M=1+2S<=(5/2)S and d_ff>=4. Thus BOTH covariance phases give\n\n    D_inc>M*A_inc^2,\n    2f_n<=A_inc^2/D_inc<1/M=H_base(h).              (37)\n\nCompletion of the square in(28) proves the second line for every sigma.\nThis is a uniform screen over b_inc in ALL of R, every nu_inc>0 and\n0<delta_inc<=1/2, and all legal common values. The coefficient payment\nis not an amplitude or value tuning inside the old affine clock.\n\n### 4.6 Conditions and exact benchmark on the original full hierarchy\n\nFor h=H_m in(17), h_lead=1+a_word<2 and the palindrome property holds.\nWe have a_word<=c-2: c>=lambda implies c(c-2)>=1>beta_word.\nInduction on j then gives\n\n    1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a_word*c^j<=c^(j+1),\n    S=S_m<c^m<3^m.                                 (38)\n\nAlso n=2^m-1>=2^(m-1), and for m>=10 the exact integer comparison\n3^10<4^8, followed by multiplying the left/right by3/4 at each\nincrement, gives 3^m<4^(m-2)<=n^2/4. Thus\nS<=n^2/4<=d_ff^2/4. The remaining conditions S>=2,d_ff>=4 hold\nalready for m>=3. In particular ALL of(34) hold for m>=1536.\n\nAt the actual n=2^m-1, use ONLY the accepted comparison(19):\n\n    V_inc(H_m)/U_n^ZJ=B_n*V_inc(H_m)\n                           <B_n*H_base(H_m)<237/250. (39)\n\nNo finite-field expectation, remote-cycle deletion or Gram-positivity\nguess is a premise. Successful realizations would be static by(8), with\ntrue radius1 under the declared strict condition; even such realizations\ncannot improve the benchmark here. This closes ONLY law(24), its stated\ncoefficient range and its origin-anchored radius convention. It does not\nexclude arbitrary signed incidence weights, nonlinear longitudinal\nprofiles, different initial anchors or terminal longitudinal reversals.\n\n## 5. Law3: a fully legal changing-gate function with a terminal sign change\n\n### 5.1 Static model, sharp smoothness and true radius\n\nFor delta_gate>0 define the symmetric Huber scalar\n\n    Hub_delta(t)=t^2/2                    if |t|<=delta_gate,\n                 delta_gate*|t|-delta_gate^2/2 otherwise,\n    clip_delta(t)=max(-delta_gate,min(delta_gate,t)).\n\nThe ONE static function on R^2 is\n\n    F_gate(x,y)=x^2/3+Hub_delta(x+y)/4.              (40)\n\nIts gradient is\n\n    grad F_gate(x,y)=(2x/3+clip_delta(x+y)/4,\n                                      clip_delta(x+y)/4). (41)\n\nThe function is finite, differentiable and convex. It vanishes exactly\nat x=0,x+y=0, so its unique minimum is(0,0). Start at\nx0_gate=(0,2delta_gate), whose TRUE closest radius is2delta_gate.\n\nLet e_x=(1,0), a_gate=(1,1), lambda_gate=2/3, kappa_gate=1/4.\nThe global Hessian upper matrix is\n\n    M_gate=lambda_gate*e_x*e_x^T+kappa_gate*a_gate*a_gate^T\n                       =[[11/12,1/4],[1/4,1/4]],\n    I-M_gate=[[1/12,-1/4],[-1/4,3/4]]>=0.           (42)\n\nThe last matrix has nonnegative diagonal entries and determinant0.\nFor two points the derivative difference is obtained by integrating\nmatrices lambda_gate e_x e_x^T+theta kappa_gate a_gate a_gate^T,\nwith theta in[0,1], along the connecting segment. Each such matrix is\nPSD and bounded by I, so grad F_gate is1-Lipschitz.\nNear0 the Hessian is exactly M_gate, whose eigenvalues are1 and1/6:\nits trace is7/6, its determinant1/6, and I-M_gate is singular.\nTherefore1 is the SHARP global smoothness. No loose normalization is\nused to create the finite gain.\n\n### 5.2 Arbitrary words, complete data and ALL original cycles\n\nFor arbitrary h>=0 the actual full physical trajectory is defined by\n\n    c_i=clip_delta(x_i+y_i),\n    x_(i+1)=(1-2h_i/3)*x_i-(h_i/4)*c_i,\n    y_(i+1)=y_i-(h_i/4)*c_i,\n    g_i=(2x_i/3+c_i/4,c_i/4),\n    f_i=x_i^2/3+Hub_delta(x_i+y_i)/4,               (43)\n\nincluding the actual terminal g_n,f_n. This is the exact law of ONE\nfixed function(40), not a different Hessian or oracle chosen at each\nstep. The equations are symbolic definitions, not executed recurrences.\nEvery zero step simply repeats the whole jet.\n\nThere is an exact ALL-pair cost formula. For any two samples i,j,\nincluding the optimum jet, put p_i=x_i+y_i, d_x=x_j-x_i,\nd_c=c_j-c_i, and\n\n    B_Hub(i,j)=Hub_delta(p_j)-Hub_delta(p_i)-c_i*(p_j-p_i).\n\nDirect substitution in the original orientation(3) gives\n\n    Q_ij=-(lambda_gate/2)*d_x^2-kappa_gate*B_Hub(i,j)\n             +||lambda_gate*d_x*e_x+kappa_gate*d_c*a_gate||^2/2. (44)\n\nThe scalar clip is nondecreasing1-Lipschitz. For p_j>p_i its Bregman\narea is integral_(p_i)^(p_j)(clip_delta(t)-clip_delta(p_i))dt.\nOn the final subinterval of length d_c, monotonicity and Lipschitz1\nbound that area below by the triangle area d_c^2/2. Reversing the\nordered endpoints uses the initial subinterval and gives the same bound.\nHence B_Hub(i,j)>=d_c^2/2 for every ordered pair.\n\nLet A_gate have columns sqrt(lambda_gate)e_x and sqrt(kappa_gate)a_gate.\nBecause A_gate*A_gate^T=M_gate<=I, its operator norm is at most1.\nApplying it to (sqrt(lambda_gate)d_x,sqrt(kappa_gate)d_c) gives\n\n    ||lambda_gate*d_x*e_x+kappa_gate*d_c*a_gate||^2\n                         <=lambda_gate*d_x^2+kappa_gate*d_c^2.\n\nThus(44) proves Q_ij<=0 for EVERY original directed pair and BOTH\noptimum directions, with no finite-row omission. Summing(44) gives the\nexact cost of any directed cycle and shows it is nonpositive.\nThe full Gram is automatically PSD from these actual R^2 vectors.\nThe true radius is already known from the unique minimum, not chosen\nafter examining a formal Gram.\n\n### 5.3 A finite bridge which genuinely leaves the old sign restriction\n\nTake h=(3/2,12,3/2), whose actual length is n=3. All coordinates and\ngradients in the following table are divided by delta_gate; values are\ndivided by delta_gate^2.\n\n| i | position (x_i,y_i)/delta_gate | (x_i+y_i)/delta_gate | g_i/delta_gate | f_i/delta_gate^2 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 0 | (0,2) | 2 | (1/4,1/4) | 3/8 |\n| 1 | (-3/8,13/8) | 5/4 | (0,1/4) | 15/64 |\n| 2 | (-3/8,-11/8) | -7/4 | (-1/2,-1/4) | 23/64 |\n| 3 | (3/8,-1) | -5/8 | (3/32,-5/32) | 49/512 |\n\nThe exact substitutions in(41),(43) verify every entry. Queries0,1\nare on the positive plateau, query2 on the negative plateau, and the\nterminal is inside the quadratic region. The background Hessian\ndiag(2/3,0) and M_gate do not commute: their off-diagonal products in\nthe two orders are different. Thus the active Hessian law changes.\n\nIn particular\n\n    <g_3,x0_gate>=-5delta_gate^2/16<0,\n    <g_0,g_3>=-delta_gate^2/64<0.                    (45)\n\nThe initial gradient is not aligned with x0_gate. The terminal component\nalong the initial-radius direction is NEGATIVE. This is outside the\npositive-terminal/aligned-gradient obstruction of round59.\n\nIts exact normalized true-radius payoff is\n\n    V_gate=2F_gate(x_3,y_3)/(4delta_gate^2)=49/1024,\n    H_base(h)=1/(1+2*(3/2+12+3/2))=1/31,\n    V_gate/H_base=1519/1024>1.                       (46)\n\nThis is finite nonzero amplitude, exact values and ALL original rows.\nNo expectation or formal negative matrix direction is being called a\nlower witness.\n\nFor the ORIGINAL actual-n benchmark, the exact ratio is\n\n    V_gate/U_3^ZJ=49B_3/1024.                        (47)\n\nIt is below1. Here is a symbolic bound using the original all-split\ndefinitions from acceptedb98bb807d514e80d, without executing a DP.\nLet Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2 and\nG(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2. Then C0=B0=1,\nC1=lambda<5/2, B1=4. Monotonicity gives\n\n    C2=Omega(1,lambda)<Omega(1,5/2)<9/2,\n    B2=max{G(1,4),G(lambda,1)}<8.\n\nThe C2 bound follows from sqrt(89)<11. The B2 bounds follow from\nsqrt(3)<2 and sqrt(21)<5. At horizon3 the THREE original split\nexpressions satisfy\n\n    G(1,B2)<G(1,8)=6+4sqrt(2)<12,\n    G(C1,B1)<G(5/2,4)=7+2sqrt(6)<12,\n    G(C2,1)<G(9/2,1)=19/2+sqrt(37)/2<13.\n\nTherefore B3<13 and the exact ratio(47) is <637/1024<1.\nThis is only a finite bridge beyond Huber. It is also below the accepted\nsame-word scalar single-cut witness: at the middle step P=T=3/2,\nthe scalar cut from factb98bb807d514e80d is121/25, which is larger\nthan49/1024. No superiority over the existing full witness portfolio\nis claimed.\n\n### 5.4 An exact arbitrary-horizon counterexample to this fixed law family\n\nThe legal word h_ann=(3/2,5,1,6) has actual length4. For EVERY\ndelta_gate>0 the SAME function family(40) and initialization yield\nthe following exact trajectory, divided by delta_gate:\n\n| i | position | p_i | gradient |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 0 | (0,2) | 2 | (1/4,1/4) |\n| 1 | (-3/8,13/8) | 5/4 | (0,1/4) |\n| 2 | (-3/8,3/8) | 0 | (-1/4,0) |\n| 3 | (-1/8,3/8) | 1/4 | (-1/48,1/16) |\n| 4 | (0,0) | 0 | (0,0) |\n\nThe first two steps bring the trajectory into the core. The remaining\nsteps1 and6 annihilate the two eigenmodes of M_gate; the displayed\nintermediate point remains in the core, so this eigenmode explanation\nis backed by the actual nonlinear gate checks, not just a matrix product.\n\nThe values at i=2,3,4, divided by delta_gate^2, are respectively\n3/64,5/384,0. All original rows hold by(44). The true radius is still\n2delta_gate and the terminal payoff is EXACTLY0.\n\nFor every n>=4 append n-4 zero steps. The actual iterate remains the\nminimum, so the whole fixed scale family has\n\n    sup_(delta_gate>0) V_gate(h_ann,0,...,0)=0,\n    [sup_delta V_gate]/U_n^ZJ=0.                    (48)\n\nThe denominator is the original positive U_n^ZJ at THAT n. This is an\nall-horizon family counterexample, not a computed finite trend.\nIt refutes using(40) with scale selection alone as the required all-word\nadversary. It does not say W_n(h_ann,0,...,0)=0: other legal functions,\nincluding the accepted scalar witnesses, have positive error there.\nVarying the geometry, Hessian spectrum or initialization with h is\noutside this fixed law family and remains open.\n\n## 6. Original target interface: what remains unpaid\n\nFor every sufficiently large actual horizon n and EVERY predetermined\nh>=0, the original affirmative target still requires a SINGLE legal\nstatic function, finite dimension and attained minimum giving\n\n    2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x0-x_star||^2)\n                            >=(1-epsilon)/(2S_bullet(n)+1),\n\nwith a threshold in n independent of h. No statement above establishes\nthis. A negative answer still requires an actual full-class objective\nUPPER bound with a fixed relative benchmark gap on unbounded horizons;\nnone is asserted here either.\n\nThe next decisive interface cannot merely be a covariance matrix whose\nterminal row has the right sign. Laws1 and2 show exact original-row\npayments that survive changes of scale and common values. Law3 shows\nthat genuine queried gate changes and terminal reversals are feasible,\nbut its two spectral modes are killed by a four-step word. A new law\nwould need word-dependent nonlinear memory/geometry which pays those\npayments and avoids an annihilator, together with a uniform original\nbenchmark comparison. That final all-word comparison is an unpaid\nTHEOREM-LEVEL gap, not a technical lemma or a solved reduction.\n\nNo universal Huber optimality, covariance impossibility, unrestricted\nW upper bound, resolution of(P), project finalization, revocation,\npublication, shutdown or Goal completion is claimed.\n\n## 7.
