All prior computational and formal allocations remain closed.\n\nThe full public evidence2df3df199e391be9, bfed68e4c77df24e,\nd5345c6e59f22ce3, and84a4435a88a9f487 was read. Their mathematical assertions\nare not accepted premises. The geometry, scales, endpoint estimates and\nidentities used here are derived jointly below. The sole accepted input is\nthe original benchmark/ray conclusion of fact04219554e41d9750.\n\nThere is a new cap-payment estimate in Section7: during an actual persistent\npositive horizontal excursion, a weighted downstream deficit absorbs the\ncircular cap loss, with a uniform positive loss rate and both neighbors\nretained. Its exact entry costs remain unpaid. Sections5 and6 supply,\nrespectively, an exact terminal-gradient identity retaining cancellation and\nthe complete initialized incoming interface for every interior component.\nNone of the three screens gives a uniform bound on the actual middle\nterminal value below 1-T_infty. The failures are failures of the stated\ncomparisons, not a disproof of terminal absorption on the actual trajectory.\n\n## 1. Exact static function, body and physical word\n\nFor each integer m>=16 put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,\n    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,\n    d=c-2,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,\n    S_j=sum H_j, n=2^m-1.\n\nEvery child uses this SAME enclosing m and these same parameters. Select\nthe first occurrences of levels J,...,m-1. Checkpoint i has level\nl_i=J+i-1, physical step index 2^l_i-1 and value b_l_i.\nThe ordinary block masses are s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k,\nand the last mass is S=S_(m-1). Every entry of the word is executed.\n\nFor 0<=q<=1 define\n\n    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),\n    C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), e=C-beta=sqrt(1+2beta),\n    K=conv({0} union {(p(q),-q):0<=q<=1}),\n    kappa=1+beta^2.\n\nThe complete horizontal sections of this full body are\n\n    0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p<=p(q).                              (1)\n\nIt has the two radial segments to (C,0) and (beta,-1), and the whole\ncircular arc between them. No finite-plane replacement is made.\n\nFor a compact convex body B containing0 put\n\n    E_B(w)=max_(g in B){<g,w>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nWrite Hub_L for the one-sided Huber: zero for r<=0, r^2/2 for\n0<=r<=L, and L*r-L^2/2 for r>=L.\n\nThe local data of every ordinary block are defined using its actual order.\nIf its physical entries are a_1,...,a_N, set alpha_j=a_j/2.\nStart (u_N,-y_N)=(beta,-1), q_N=1, and define backwards\n\n    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)\n       =max{beta/e, (y_j+(1-alpha_j)*beta)/\n                              (u_j-(1-alpha_j)*beta)},\n    (u_(j-1),-y_(j-1))=(u_j,-y_j)+alpha_j*(p(q_(j-1)),-q_(j-1)).\n                                                               (2)\n\nThe left side increases continuously from beta/e to infinity. Its right\ndenominator is positive since u_j>=beta and alpha_j>0. The starting\ncoordinates for block i are denoted u_i,y_i; set M_i=u_i+y_i/beta.\nThese are finite symbolic definitions, not a recurrence executed this round.\n\nFix ell_1=0,D_1=1 and define, before the run,\n\n    T_i=b_l_i/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,\n    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,\n    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,\n    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)),\n    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S),\n    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))\n                      +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)),\n    x_0=e_1 in R^(k+1).                                    (3)\n\nWe use the range in which every T_i>0; this holds for all sufficiently\nlarge m. This is exactly the frozen circular half-sum.\n\n## 2. Geometry, true radius, and every neighboring gradient\n\nCompleting the square in E_B identifies its unique maximizer as Pi_B(w),\nthe Euclidean projection. The maximizing inequalities at w and w' imply\n\n    ||Pi_B(w)-Pi_B(w')||^2\n                <=<Pi_B(w)-Pi_B(w'),w-w'>.\n\nThe maximizing formula and this inequality give differentiability with\ngradient Pi_B and a 1-Lipschitz gradient. Thus E_B is convex, nonnegative\nand1-smooth. Its zero set is the polar cone of B: if some support pairing\nis positive, taking a sufficiently small positive multiple of that\nsupport vector gives a positive envelope value.\n\nThe cone of K has extreme rays (C,0),(beta,-1). Consequently\n\n    Phi_i=0 iff x_i<=A_i and\n                      x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i).            (4)\n\nThe disk with center(beta,beta), radius1+beta supports the whole arc.\nIts normal and the horizontal endpoint normal prove, in (2),\n\n    Pi_K(u_j,-y_j)=(p(q_j),-q_j),\n    u_i=beta+sum alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),\n    y_i=1+sum alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_i>=beta*y_i.                (5)\n\nFor the uncapped points the normal ratio is (y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta).\nThe defining predecessor ratio reduces its numerator by alpha_j*beta\nand increases its denominator by the same amount, so q_(j-1)<=q_j.\nAt the capped endpoint the inequality y<=beta*(u-C)/e is preserved\nby every further backward capped step. These facts check the normals\nalso at the transition and endpoints.\n\nAt any global query, define ALL gradients by\n\n    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)), i<=k,\n    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.\n\nEvery physical step is exactly\n\n    x_j^+=x_j-(h/2)(P_j-Q_(j-1)), 1<=j<=k+1.               (6)\n\nIn particular earlier components are never reset or deleted.\n\nIf x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1), replacing (P,-Q) by (P,0) improves its\nmaximization by Q*(x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))+Q^2/2 when Q>0.\nThus Q_i=0 in that region. If x_i<=ell_i and its output is\nnonnegative, (4) gives Phi_i=0.\n\nThese properties prove the active-pair chronology inductively. At block i\nthe active local states are (A_i+sigma_i*u_j,ell_(i+1)-sigma_i*y_j);\nits input is >=ell_i, so Q_(i-1)=0 at the ACTIVE pair's queries.\nIts next component vanishes, as do all still-future components.\nEarlier components retain (6). The selected step produces, exactly,\n\n    x_i(t_i)=A_i-beta*D_(i+1),\n    x_(i+1)(t_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1),\n    t_i=2^l_i.                                             (7)\n\nEquation(7) specifies only that pair at its OWN checkpoint time.\nIt is not a simultaneous snapshot of the later retired chain.\n\nThe map stacking all adjacent pairs and the last scalar counts each\ncoordinate at most twice. Its squared norm is <=2. Thus the factor1/2\nmakes F convex1-smooth. F>=0 and F(0)=0. Its entire minimum set is\n\n    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i,\n                x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;\n                                      x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.\n\nThe point (A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) belongs to it. From (5),\n\n    1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1,\n    A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).\n\nFor x_1<0 the squared distance from e_1 is at least1.\nFor 0<=x_1<=A_1 it is at least (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2,\ndecreasing on this interval and attained by that feasible point. Therefore\n\n    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2\n          =(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2>0.        (8)\n\nAll physical samples, terminal samples and minimizers obey every ordered\nsmooth-convex interpolation inequality. To check this directly, apply\nthe 1-smooth descent inequality to F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, minimized at y;\nit gives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.\nThe Gram matrices are actual Euclidean Gram matrices. Scaling F by an\nadvertised L>0 and using steps h/L preserves (6) and the normalized payoff.\n\n## 3. Uniform scales and the strictly scoped accepted input\n\nHere are the bounds needed for the normalizations, derived jointly so no\nearlier unverified estimate is used as a premise.\n\nIn a backward block set w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta). Expansion of (2),\nusing the circular normal at uncapped points, gives\n\n    w_(j-1)/w_j\n       =1+[p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j)]/\n                         [beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1))].       (9)\n\nAt a transition to q_(j-1)=0 this follows from y_(j-1)=y_j;\nif both q's are zero it is equality. The numerator is the integral\nof p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t). After division by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1)\nthe integrand is <=max(1,-p'(t)). Since w_N=1, multiplication gives\n\n    y_i<=Y_k:=2 exp(2k),\n    integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.\n\nFor the integral split at t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta.\nAt uncapped points u<=beta+2k*(Y_k+beta). The uncapped backward steps\nare contiguous and have p>=beta; their combined deficit from C times\nhalf their mass is <=4k^2*(Y_k+beta). The remaining p's equal C.\nPaying also y_i/beta and beta gives\n\n    |M_i-C*s_i/2|<=K_k:=20k^2 exp(2k).                     (10)\n\nThe concave function p(q)+q/beta has smaller endpoint C. Using (5),\n\n    M_i>=beta+1/beta+C*s_i/2>=C*(1+s_i/2).                 (11)\n\nLet v_mass=a_word/d. Since c*(c-2)>1>eta_word, 0<v_mass<1.\nSubstitution in the exact mass relation gives\n\n    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,\n    b_j=d*S_j+c-1-d*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                       (12)\n\nUniformly for J<=j<=m-1 the relative leading-mass error is\nO(c^(-J)+(2/c)^J). Also k*K_k/S_J->0: its numerator has logarithm\nO(k+log k) and the denominator has logarithm J*log(lambda)+O(1).\nUsing 0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 with x=1/(4m) yields\n\n    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),\n    S_(m-r)/lambda^(m-r)->exp(1/4) for every fixed r>=1.\n\nThe first amplitude ratio T_1/M_1 is (d/C)*(1+o(1)); the other amplitude\nratios are (c*d/C)*(1+o(1)). The individual relative errors are uniformly\nO(K_k/S_J+c^(-J)+(2/c)^J), and k times this bound tends to zero.\nTaking logarithms in the product therefore proves\n\n    D_(k+1)=(d/C)*(c*d/C)^(k-1)*(1+o(1))\n                 ->(lambda-2)*exp(-2),\n    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C*S_J),\n    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2               (13)\n\nuniformly for i<k. Here c*d=1+O(1/m) and log(C)/beta->2,\nso C^k->exp(2). Eventually every adjacent sigma ratio is in [1/3,1/2],\nand sum sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2.\n\nThe ORIGINAL ALL-SPLIT benchmark is C_0^bench=B_0=1,\n\n    C_n^bench=max_(0<=r<n)Omega(C_r^bench,C_(n-r-1)^bench),\n    B_n=max_(0<=r<n)G(C_r^bench,B_(n-r-1)),\n    Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,\n    G(a,b)=2a+b/2+sqrt(b^2+8ab)/2, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nThe circle constant C in this report is distinct from C_n^bench.\nOnly the unconditional ray conclusion of accepted fact04219554e41d9750\nis used:\n\n    0<Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m) B_(2^m-1)<infinity,\n    Z_1<5.                                                (14)\n\nThe latter follows from its b=1,j=0 upper enclosure, B_1=4, and\n(2+3/100)^2<5. No seed hypothesis is assumed.\n\nPut q_root=t_k=2^(m-1), N=2^(m-1)-1. The final suffix is the actual\nordered H_(m-1), indexed t=0,...,N. During this suffix the last excess\nis D_(k+1)-delta times half the mass already read, and stays >=delta.\nOutput isolation gives Q_k=0, proving this claim by induction.\nThus its terminal Huber numerator is exactly D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S)].\n\nThe retired function and its exact updates are\n\n    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k)Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))\n                         +1/2 Hub_(C*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k),\n    d_i=P_i-Q_(i-1), x^+=x-(h/2)d, Q_0=Q_k=0,\n    V_m=2G_ret(x(N))=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x(N)).\n\nPhi_k denotes the original last circular value, exactly equal to this\nhorizontal Huber at these actual states. Hence\n\n    B_n*2F(x_n)/R_m^2=T_m+B_n*V_m/R_m^2,\n    T_m=B_n*D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S)*R_m^2]\n                  ->T_infty=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda)<5/64. (15)\n\nThe useful finite-error normalizing scale is\n\n    (B_n/R_m^2)*sigma_1^2*(1+K_k+2^m)->0.                  (16)\n\nIndeed B_n*sigma_1^2=O(lambda^(-m+2k)); multiplying by K_k\nstill decays exponentially in m. The remaining term is\nO((2/lambda)^m*lambda^(2k)), also tending to zero. No numerical\nevaluation, finite benchmark replay or m>=1536 inference is used.\n\n## 4. Both endpoint estimates, included in this new joint attempt\n\nFor i<k define\n\n    r_i=x_i-A_i, z_i=beta*(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1),\n    U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i>=0; U_k=P_k, r_k=x_k-A_k.\n\nChanging cone coordinates in (1) gives the exact formula\n\n    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta*Q)^2+Q^2]/2:\n         0<=Q<=sigma_i,\n         0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta*Q}.               (17)\n\nFor r_i<=0 it reduces to\n\n    U_i=0, Q_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i),\n    Phi_i=Hub_(kappa*sigma_i)(z_i)/kappa.                  (18)\n\nThere are the state-dependent implications\n\n    U_i>0 => x_i>A_i>=ell_i => Q_(i-1)=0,\n    Q_i>0 => x_(i+1)<ell_(i+1) => U_(i+1)=0.              (19)\n\nThe full coupled residual law is\n\n    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta*Q_i-Q_(i-1)),\n    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)(kappa*Q_i-beta*Q_(i-1)-beta*Q_(i+1)\n                                         +beta*U_i-U_(i+1)). (20)\n\nAt the first checkpoint r_1=-beta*D_2. Since its coordinate subsequently\ncannot increase under (6), this inequality persists and U_1=0.\nIf z_1>kappa*sigma_1, (18) gives Q_1=sigma_1 and\n\n    x_2=beta*x_1-z_1<ell_2-beta^2*D_2-kappa*sigma_1.\n\nThen U_2=0 and P_2<=beta*sigma_2. Write\ngamma_1=kappa*sigma_1-beta*sigma_2. In this region z_1 loses\nat least h*gamma_1/2; an entry step can add at most h*C*sigma_2/2.\n\nEvery occurrence of b_j in the suffix is immediately followed by a\ncomplete H_j. This follows inductively from its physical ruler definition.\nTake the LAST entry into z_1>kappa*sigma_1 if the terminal residual\nexceeds that threshold. The following subtree and (12) give\n\n    (z_1(N))_+<=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C*sigma_2/2              (21)\n\nprovided gamma_1>0 and d*C*sigma_2<=gamma_1.\nFor the case above-threshold since the start, retain all prehistory.\nWith S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1), (6) gives\n\n    z_1(0)<=beta+C*sigma_2*S_pre/2.\n\nThat branch cannot end above the threshold when\n2beta+C*sigma_2*S_pre<=gamma_1*S.\nAll these finite conditions hold eventually by (13),\nS_pre/S->lambda-1, and (lambda-2)*(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1.\nEventually the right side of (21) is <=2sigma_1. Therefore\n\n    Phi_1(x(N))<=2sigma_1^2.                               (22)\n\nFor the last component write L_k=C*sigma_k. Its own checkpoint, at the\nstart of this suffix, gives r_k(0)=-beta*D_(k+1)<0.\nWhenever r_k>L_k, Q_(k-1)=0 and P_k=L_k. An entry step adds at most\nh*sigma_(k-1)/2. The last entry and its following H_j imply\n\n    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(1/2) max_(0<=j<=m-2)\n                                   [b_j*sigma_(k-1)-L_k*S_j]_+.\n\nSet s=S_(m-2), T=b_(m-2)/2-1, Delta=d-C*T/M_k.\nSince sigma_k/sigma_(k-1)=T/M_k exactly, (12) bounds this by\n\n    L_k+(sigma_(k-1)/2)[c-1+(Delta)_+*s].\n\nPay the defect before taking asymptotics:\n\n    d*M_k-C*T\n       =d*(M_k-C*s/2)+C*(3-c)/2\n                         +(C*d/2)(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).\n\nEquations(10),(11) give\n\n    (Delta)_+*s <=(2d/C)K_k+3+d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).\n    A_err=c-1+(2d/C)K_k+3+d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2),\n    Phi_k(x(N))<=L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err.            (23)\n\nThis is a finite bound, with the entire 2^(m-2) correction retained.\nBy (16) the benchmark-normalized sum of (22),(23) tends to zero.\n\nThese are jointly derived endpoint bounds in an UNVERIFIED report.\nThey are not accepted facts and cannot be used as accepted predecessors.\n\n## 5. Schema A: exact terminal charge retaining later dissipation\n\nFor a differentiable convex E define its Bregman increment\n\n    B_E(w^+,w)=E(w^+)-E(w)-<grad E(w),w^+-w>.\n\nFor E_B and g=Pi_B(w), g^+=Pi_B(w^+), expansion gives the equality\n\n    B_E(w^+,w)\n       =1/2||g^+-g||^2+<w^+-g^+,g^+-g>.                   (24)\n\nThe last pairing is nonnegative by the new projection's normal\ninequality with the old g as a feasible comparison point.\nFormula(24) also covers the last scalar Huber using B=[0,L_k].\n\nFor each actual suffix step use (24) at every centered pair, or at r_k.\nWrite B_i(t) for these increments. Summing first-order expansions gives\n\n    V_m=2G_ret(x(0))-(1/2)sum_t h_t||d(t)||^2\n                                           +sum_t sum_i B_i(t). (25)\n\nThis is equality for the actual coupled trajectory. In particular,\nactivity erased after a positive source is not discarded.\n\nA second exact identity uses only ACTUAL TERMINAL projections.\nLet g_i^N=(P_i(N),-Q_i(N)) for i<k and g_k^N=P_k(N).\nPut N_N=sum_i P_i(N)^2+sum_(i<k)Q_i(N)^2. Since each terminal\ngradient is fixed during the following algebra,\n\n    V_m=sum_(i<k)[U_i(N)*r_i(0)+Q_i(N)*z_i(0)]\n               +P_k(N)*r_k(0)-N_N/2\n                      -(1/2)sum_t h_t<d(N),d(t)>.          (26)\n\nBoth neighbors enter the pairing. Explicitly,\n\n    <d(N),d(t)>\n       =sum_i P_i(N)P_i(t)+sum_(i<k)Q_i(N)Q_i(t)\n        -sum_(i<k)[P_(i+1)(N)Q_i(t)+Q_i(N)P_(i+1)(t)].     (27)\n\nIf a component's terminal projection is zero it contributes no frozen\nterminal charge. This is the useful distinction from all-time positive X.\n\nAttempt A1 tried to dominate the Bregman terms in (25) by gradient\ndissipation. The exact distance identity exhibits the obstruction.\nFor a fixed retired minimizer z_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0), put\n\n    N_grad=sum_i P_i^2+sum_(i<k)Q_i^2,\n    C_cross=sum_(i<k)P_(i+1)Q_i,\n    slack=-sum_(i<k)<(P_i,-Q_i),(z_*i-A_i,z_*(i+1)-ell_(i+1))>\n                                      -P_k*(z_*k-A_k)>=0.\n\nExpansion of (6) gives, with the ORIGINAL signs,\n\n    ||x-z_*||^2-||x^+-z_*||^2\n       =2h G_ret(x)+h(2-h)N_grad/4+h^2 C_cross/2+h*slack.   (28)\n\nFor h>2 the displayed gradient-square coefficient is negative.\nNo estimate in this screen pays it uniformly across all physical bridges.\nFor a scalar horizontal core r in (0,L_k), a large step sends\nr^+=(1-h/2)r<0; its Bregman increment is (h/2-1/2)r^2.\nDropping these terms would destroy the exact cancellation that gives\nterminal value zero in that elementary example.\n\nAttempt A2 tried to make the cross-time pairing in (27) nonnegative.\nThat universal statement is false for the SAME frozen function.\nHere is an explicit alternative-state test, not initialized reachability.\n\nChoose an interior pair i,i+1, set x_i=A_i,\nx_(i+1)=ell_(i+1)-kappa*sigma_i,\nand x_(i+2)=ell_(i+2). Choose earlier coordinates sufficiently negative\nthat their components are zero at both samples, retaining the permanent\nfirst-coordinate bound. Choose later coordinates zero, except for the\nusual isolated terminal scalar. Initially g_i=(beta*sigma_i,-sigma_i),\ng_(i+1)=0, and all other retired gradients vanish. A physical suffix\nstep h=b_(m-2) satisfying\n\n    h>2kappa+2ell_(i+2)/(beta*sigma_i)+2C*sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i\n\nsends component i into its zero cone and puts i+1 in its horizontal cap.\nThe next retired gradient is supported only at i+1 with value\nC*sigma_(i+1). Thus\n\n    <d_after,d_before>=-sigma_i*C*sigma_(i+1)<0.            (29)\n\nThe condition holds eventually because the threshold involving ell is\nO(k exp(2k)), while b_(m-2) has exponential order lambda^m.\n\nThis refutes universal cross-time positivity. It does NOT refute a\ntrajectory-specific integrated inequality for (26).\nThe exact initialized pairing sum in (26), or an equally sharp signed\nBregman comparison in (25), is unpaid. No claim that actual X is small\nor large is needed, and no actual terminal lower bound follows.\n\n## 6. Schema B: own-checkpoint initialization and reachable retirement\n\nThis screen starts each interior i at its actual OWN checkpoint t_i;\nit does not rename i as the first component. From (7),\n\n    r_i(t_i)=-beta*D_(i+1),\n    z_i(t_i)=-kappa*D_(i+1).                               (30)\n\nFor i<k the complete remaining physical word is\n\n    (H_l_i,b_(l_i+1),H_(l_i+1),...,\n                                  b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).        (31)\n\nFor i=k it is H_(m-1). The prefix ending at q_root includes all\nintervening checkpoints and their ordinary blocks. Their parameters\nare still the same enclosing m. Earlier coordinates in this prefix\nfollow (6); they are not placed back at their own checkpoint states.\n\nIn particular the incoming neighboring derivative at t_i is exactly\n\n    Q_(i-1)(t_i)\n      =-[Pi_(sigma_(i-1)K)(\n          x_(i-1)(t_i)-A_(i-1),\n                         ell_(i+1)/beta-beta*D_(i+1))]_2,\n    x_(i-1)(t_i)=1_(i-1=1)\n             -(1/2)sum_(u<t_i)h_u[P_(i-1)(u)-Q_(i-2)(u)]. (32)\n\nThis retains its full earlier physical history, including the incoming\nderivative whenever it is nonzero. Nothing in (7) forces its vanishing;\nno first-component isolation is imposed at retirement. The test below\nexhibits a saturated nonzero value under the weaker checkpoint-margin\nhypotheses, without asserting that alternative state is actually reached.\nAt the final-suffix start its value is the corresponding projection at\nthe actual coordinates given next. A clip formula for that projection\nwould require its OWN input bound; only the first permanent bound has\nbeen proved in Section4.\n\nFor T>=t_i define exact finite integrals over (31)'s already executed\nprefix,\n\n    H_j^U(i,T)=(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(T-1)h_u U_j(u),\n    H_j^Q(i,T)=(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(T-1)h_u Q_j(u).\n\nBEFORE q_root the full Q_k is retained and U_k=P_k-beta*Q_k.\nPut U_(k+1)=P_(k+1), Q_(k+1)=Q_0=0. Summing the full residual law,\n\n    r_i(T)=-beta*D_(i+1)-H_i^U-beta*H_i^Q+H_(i-1)^Q,\n    z_i(T)=-kappa*D_(i+1)-kappa*H_i^Q\n              +beta*H_(i-1)^Q+beta*H_(i+1)^Q\n                                   -beta*H_i^U+H_(i+1)^U. (33)\n\nAll H's on a line have the same (i,T). Thus (33) explicitly derives\nthe initialized incoming r_i(0),z_i(0) of the final suffix by T=q_root.\nIt also gives the ACTUAL terminal residuals by T=n. It retains both\nneighbors and all later dissipation, rather than just its positive terms.\n\nThere is also a componentwise terminal-charge identity starting at this\nOWN checkpoint, with no unknown replacement initial state. For i<k put\np_N=P_i(n), q_N=Q_i(n), u_N=p_N-beta*q_N, and use the FULL global\nd_j(u)=P_j(u)-Q_(j-1)(u) even before q_root. Substitution of the\nexact cumulative updates into the terminal maximizing formula gives\n\n    Phi_i(x_n)=-beta*D_(i+1)*u_N-kappa*D_(i+1)*q_N\n                -(p_N^2+q_N^2)/2\n       -(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(n-1)h_u\n                                  [p_N*d_i(u)-q_N*d_(i+1)(u)]. (33a)\n\nThe initial negative charge is explicit. The last bracket includes\np_N*P_i-p_N*Q_(i-1)-q_N*P_(i+1)+q_N*Q_i, with the ACTUAL\nold and future gradients. It may have either sign; it is not replaced\nby its positive terms. If p_N=q_N=0 the entire charge vanishes.\nPaying these signed whole-future pairings from the initialized margins\nis still open. The identity does not infer nonzero terminal value from\nnonzero past activity.\n\nAn absorbing retired region DOES exist:\n\n    C_ret={x: r_i<=0 for i<=k, z_i<=0 for i<k}.             (34)\n\nIf the actual suffix enters it, every retired projection is zero.\nThe only remaining scalar gradient acts on x_(k+1), and its excess\nstays >=delta, so output isolation continues and (34) is preserved.\nThis proves conditional no-reactivation AFTER entrance, not entrance.\n\nAttempt B1 sought entrance using the own-checkpoint margins (30).\nThose margins, even supplemented by earlier negative checkpoint margins,\ndo not constitute an invariant for all states satisfying them.\nThe following exact test fixes that scope.\n\nTake r_(i-1)=-beta*D_i, the own pair in (7), and still-future coordinates\nzero. This is an ALTERNATIVE simultaneous state, not an assertion about\ntheir later actual values. For i=2 it respects the permanent first bound.\nThen\n\n    z_(i-1)=beta*D_(i+1)-beta^2*D_i-ell_(i+1)/beta.\n\nIf this is >=kappa*sigma_(i-1), (18) gives the explicitly NONZERO\nincoming Q_(i-1)=sigma_(i-1). These conditions hold eventually:\nD_(i+1)/D_i is bounded away from0, beta=1/k, and\nell_(i+1)/beta and sigma_(i-1) are exponentially smaller than beta*D_i,\nby (10)--(13). The own component has P_i=Q_i=0.\nIts next component starts its next local block and has\nP_(i+1)=C*sigma_(i+1), Q_(i+1)=0 eventually, since the large local\nu_(i+1) satisfies the capped endpoint inequality.\n\nUse the physical step h=b_(J+i-2), which occurs in the next complete\nH_l_i. Since h*sigma_(i-1)/2=D_i+sigma_(i-1),\n\n    r_i^+=-beta*D_(i+1)+D_i+sigma_(i-1)>0                 (35)\n\nwhenever beta*T_i/M_i<1. Also h*C<2M_(i+1) ensures its output\nremains above ell_(i+1), so its new value is a positive horizontal Huber.\nBoth finite conditions hold eventually by (10)--(13).\n\nTherefore the checkpoint-margin-only invariant fails under an available\nphysical step. The test does NOT refute retirement of the ACTUAL\ninitialized trajectory: the state at this later occurrence of h must be\nproved reachable, and no such assertion is made. Its specific lesson is\nthat the actual incoming Q_(i-1), including its timing in (31), cannot\nbe discarded.\n\nAttempt B2 used telescoping tail-coordinate weights. Define\n\n    Z_i(x)=sum_(j=i)^(k+1) beta^(j-i)*x_j.\n\nThe full update, including Q_k before the root and the terminal scalar,\ngives exactly\n\n    Z_i^+=Z_i-(h/2)[sum_(j=i)^(k+1)beta^(j-i)U_j-Q_(i-1)].\n    Z_i(t_i)=A_i+beta*ell_(i+1).                           (36)\n\nThe two D_(i+1) terms cancel in this INITIAL value, not by a reset.\nFor i=1 this weighted sum is nonincreasing. For every middle i it\nretains the actual incoming source in (36), including all nonzero values.\nMoreover a bound on Z_i alone does not bound positive r_i or z_i:\nnegative downstream coordinates can offset them. The cancelling adjacent\nweight ratio is beta, whereas the scale ratios sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i\ntend to lambda-2. A comparison between them loses factors of order\n((lambda-2)/beta)^O(k) and does not pay the benchmark normalization.\n\nThe scoped unpaid initialized interface is now explicit: the signed\nintegrals in (33) up to q_root, their continuation through (31), and\nentrance into (34). Substituting x_N=x_0-sum h d/2 in each maximizing formula yields\nV_N=sum_(i<k)[U_i(N)r_i(0)+Q_i(N)z_i(0)]+P_k(N)r_k(0)-N_N/2-sum_t(h_t/2)<d_N,d_t>.\nThe pairing is sum_i P_i(N)P_i(t)+sum_i Q_i(N)Q_i(t)-sum_(i<k)[P_(i+1)(N)Q_i(t)+Q_i(N)P_(i+1)(t)]. This equality retains both neighbors, later dissipation and every large step; a terminal zero projection contributes no charge.\nAttempt A1: a positive Bregman domination cannot be assumed. The exact distance expansion is 2h G_ret+h(2-h)N_grad/4+h^2 C_cross/2+h slack, with its original negative gradient-square coefficient for h>2. In a horizontal quadratic core r^+=(1-h/2)r<0, B_E=(h/2-1/2)r^2 and the actual terminal value is zero; deleting it breaks cancellation.\nAttempt A2: cross-time positivity fails for an alternative state of the SAME frozen function. Set x_i=A_i,x_(i+1)=ell_(i+1)-(1+beta^2)sigma_i,x_(i+2)=ell_(i+2), earlier coordinates sufficiently negative to be in their zero cones before and after, later coordinates zero with the isolated last scalar. At the first sample d=(beta sigma_i,-sigma_i) on these coordinates. At the physical h=b_(m-2)>2(1+beta^2)+2ell_(i+2)/(beta sigma_i)+2C sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i, the first component is zero and the next horizontal projection is C sigma_(i+1), so <d_after,d_before>=-C sigma_i sigma_(i+1)<0. The condition holds eventually from the joint scale derivation. This does not assert initialized reachability or refute an integrated initialized inequality.\nStatus: partial identities, stuck uniform bound. {"id": "d4dabd06cd49316e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T03:22:23.648998+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max3 round63 schema B PARTIAL/STUCK, joint unverified: own-checkpoint residuals, full ordered remaining words and exact incoming Q_(i-1) histories are explicit. An absorbing retired cone is invariant AFTER actual entrance; entrance is unpaid. Checkpoint margins alone and beta-tail coordinate telescoping do not establish initialized retirement.", "evidence": "Indexed schema B of the same complete JOINT UNVERIFIED candidate 8dfaa8cef518215c. The following includes its static construction and chronology derivation, followed by the full initialized-interface attempt. No unverified finding is used as an accepted premise.\n\n## 1. Exact static function, body and physical word\n\nFor each integer m>=16 put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,\n    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,\n    d=c-2,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,\n    S_j=sum H_j, n=2^m-1.\n\nEvery child uses this SAME enclosing m and these same parameters. Select\nthe first occurrences of levels J,...,m-1. Checkpoint i has level\nl_i=J+i-1, physical step index 2^l_i-1 and value b_l_i.\nThe ordinary block masses are s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k,\nand the last mass is S=S_(m-1). Every entry of the word is executed.\n\nFor 0<=q<=1 define\n\n    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),\n    C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), e=C-beta=sqrt(1+2beta),\n    K=conv({0} union {(p(q),-q):0<=q<=1}),\n    kappa=1+beta^2.\n\nThe complete horizontal sections of this full body are\n\n    0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p<=p(q).                              (1)\n\nIt has the two radial segments to (C,0) and (beta,-1), and the whole\ncircular arc between them. No finite-plane replacement is made.\n\nFor a compact convex body B containing0 put\n\n    E_B(w)=max_(g in B){<g,w>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nWrite Hub_L for the one-sided Huber: zero for r<=0, r^2/2 for\n0<=r<=L, and L*r-L^2/2 for r>=L.\n\nThe local data of every ordinary block are defined using its actual order.\nIf its physical entries are a_1,...,a_N, set alpha_j=a_j/2.\nStart (u_N,-y_N)=(beta,-1), q_N=1, and define backwards\n\n    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)\n       =max{beta/e, (y_j+(1-alpha_j)*beta)/\n                              (u_j-(1-alpha_j)*beta)},\n    (u_(j-1),-y_(j-1))=(u_j,-y_j)+alpha_j*(p(q_(j-1)),-q_(j-1)).\n                                                               (2)\n\nThe left side increases continuously from beta/e to infinity. Its right\ndenominator is positive since u_j>=beta and alpha_j>0. The starting\ncoordinates for block i are denoted u_i,y_i; set M_i=u_i+y_i/beta.\nThese are finite symbolic definitions, not a recurrence executed this round.\n\nFix ell_1=0,D_1=1 and define, before the run,\n\n    T_i=b_l_i/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,\n    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,\n    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,\n    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)),\n    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S),\n    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))\n                      +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)),\n    x_0=e_1 in R^(k+1).                                    (3)\n\nWe use the range in which every T_i>0; this holds for all sufficiently\nlarge m. This is exactly the frozen circular half-sum.\n\n## 2. Geometry, true radius, and every neighboring gradient\n\nCompleting the square in E_B identifies its unique maximizer as Pi_B(w),\nthe Euclidean projection. The maximizing inequalities at w and w' imply\n\n    ||Pi_B(w)-Pi_B(w')||^2\n                <=<Pi_B(w)-Pi_B(w'),w-w'>.\n\nThe maximizing formula and this inequality give differentiability with\ngradient Pi_B and a 1-Lipschitz gradient. Thus E_B is convex, nonnegative\nand1-smooth. Its zero set is the polar cone of B: if some support pairing\nis positive, taking a sufficiently small positive multiple of that\nsupport vector gives a positive envelope value.\n\nThe cone of K has extreme rays (C,0),(beta,-1). Consequently\n\n    Phi_i=0 iff x_i<=A_i and\n                      x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i).            (4)\n\nThe disk with center(beta,beta), radius1+beta supports the whole arc.\nIts normal and the horizontal endpoint normal prove, in (2),\n\n    Pi_K(u_j,-y_j)=(p(q_j),-q_j),\n    u_i=beta+sum alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),\n    y_i=1+sum alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_i>=beta*y_i.                (5)\n\nFor the uncapped points the normal ratio is (y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta).\nThe defining predecessor ratio reduces its numerator by alpha_j*beta\nand increases its denominator by the same amount, so q_(j-1)<=q_j.\nAt the capped endpoint the inequality y<=beta*(u-C)/e is preserved\nby every further backward capped step. These facts check the normals\nalso at the transition and endpoints.\n\nAt any global query, define ALL gradients by\n\n    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)), i<=k,\n    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.\n\nEvery physical step is exactly\n\n    x_j^+=x_j-(h/2)(P_j-Q_(j-1)), 1<=j<=k+1.               (6)\n\nIn particular earlier components are never reset or deleted.\n\nIf x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1), replacing (P,-Q) by (P,0) improves its\nmaximization by Q*(x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))+Q^2/2 when Q>0.\nThus Q_i=0 in that region. If x_i<=ell_i and its output is\nnonnegative, (4) gives Phi_i=0.\n\nThese properties prove the active-pair chronology inductively. At block i\nthe active local states are (A_i+sigma_i*u_j,ell_(i+1)-sigma_i*y_j);\nits input is >=ell_i, so Q_(i-1)=0 at the ACTIVE pair's queries.\nIts next component vanishes, as do all still-future components.\nEarlier components retain (6). The selected step produces, exactly,\n\n    x_i(t_i)=A_i-beta*D_(i+1),\n    x_(i+1)(t_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1),\n    t_i=2^l_i.                                             (7)\n\nEquation(7) specifies only that pair at its OWN checkpoint time.\nIt is not a simultaneous snapshot of the later retired chain.\n\nThe map stacking all adjacent pairs and the last scalar counts each\ncoordinate at most twice. Its squared norm is <=2. Thus the factor1/2\nmakes F convex1-smooth. F>=0 and F(0)=0. Its entire minimum set is\n\n    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i,\n                x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;\n                                      x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.\n\nThe point (A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) belongs to it. From (5),\n\n    1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1,\n    A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).\n\nFor x_1<0 the squared distance from e_1 is at least1.\nFor 0<=x_1<=A_1 it is at least (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2,\ndecreasing on this interval and attained by that feasible point. Therefore\n\n    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2\n          =(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2>0.        (8)\n\nAll physical samples, terminal samples and minimizers obey every ordered\nsmooth-convex interpolation inequality. To check this directly, apply\nthe 1-smooth descent inequality to F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, minimized at y;\nit gives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.\nThe Gram matrices are actual Euclidean Gram matrices. Scaling F by an\nadvertised L>0 and using steps h/L preserves (6) and the normalized payoff.\n\n\n\n## 6. Schema B: own-checkpoint initialization and reachable retirement\n\nThis screen starts each interior i at its actual OWN checkpoint t_i;\nit does not rename i as the first component. From (7),\n\n    r_i(t_i)=-beta*D_(i+1),\n    z_i(t_i)=-kappa*D_(i+1).                               (30)\n\nFor i<k the complete remaining physical word is\n\n    (H_l_i,b_(l_i+1),H_(l_i+1),...,\n                                  b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).        (31)\n\nFor i=k it is H_(m-1). The prefix ending at q_root includes all\nintervening checkpoints and their ordinary blocks. Their parameters\nare still the same enclosing m. Earlier coordinates in this prefix\nfollow (6); they are not placed back at their own checkpoint states.\n\nIn particular the incoming neighboring derivative at t_i is exactly\n\n    Q_(i-1)(t_i)\n      =-[Pi_(sigma_(i-1)K)(\n          x_(i-1)(t_i)-A_(i-1),\n                         ell_(i+1)/beta-beta*D_(i+1))]_2,\n    x_(i-1)(t_i)=1_(i-1=1)\n             -(1/2)sum_(u<t_i)h_u[P_(i-1)(u)-Q_(i-2)(u)]. (32)\n\nThis retains its full earlier physical history, including the incoming\nderivative whenever it is nonzero. Nothing in (7) forces its vanishing;\nno first-component isolation is imposed at retirement. The test below\nexhibits a saturated nonzero value under the weaker checkpoint-margin\nhypotheses, without asserting that alternative state is actually reached.\nAt the final-suffix start its value is the corresponding projection at\nthe actual coordinates given next. A clip formula for that projection\nwould require its OWN input bound; only the first permanent bound has\nbeen proved in Section4.\n\nFor T>=t_i define exact finite integrals over (31)'s already executed\nprefix,\n\n    H_j^U(i,T)=(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(T-1)h_u U_j(u),\n    H_j^Q(i,T)=(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(T-1)h_u Q_j(u).\n\nBEFORE q_root the full Q_k is retained and U_k=P_k-beta*Q_k.\nPut U_(k+1)=P_(k+1), Q_(k+1)=Q_0=0. Summing the full residual law,\n\n    r_i(T)=-beta*D_(i+1)-H_i^U-beta*H_i^Q+H_(i-1)^Q,\n    z_i(T)=-kappa*D_(i+1)-kappa*H_i^Q\n              +beta*H_(i-1)^Q+beta*H_(i+1)^Q\n                                   -beta*H_i^U+H_(i+1)^U. (33)\n\nAll H's on a line have the same (i,T). Thus (33) explicitly derives\nthe initialized incoming r_i(0),z_i(0) of the final suffix by T=q_root.\nIt also gives the ACTUAL terminal residuals by T=n. It retains both\nneighbors and all later dissipation, rather than just its positive terms.\n\nThere is also a componentwise terminal-charge identity starting at this\nOWN checkpoint, with no unknown replacement initial state. For i<k put\np_N=P_i(n), q_N=Q_i(n), u_N=p_N-beta*q_N, and use the FULL global\nd_j(u)=P_j(u)-Q_(j-1)(u) even before q_root. Substitution of the\nexact cumulative updates into the terminal maximizing formula gives\n\n    Phi_i(x_n)=-beta*D_(i+1)*u_N-kappa*D_(i+1)*q_N\n                -(p_N^2+q_N^2)/2\n       -(1/2)sum_(u=t_i)^(n-1)h_u\n                                  [p_N*d_i(u)-q_N*d_(i+1)(u)]. (33a)\n\nThe initial negative charge is explicit. The last bracket includes\np_N*P_i-p_N*Q_(i-1)-q_N*P_(i+1)+q_N*Q_i, with the ACTUAL\nold and future gradients. It may have either sign; it is not replaced\nby its positive terms. If p_N=q_N=0 the entire charge vanishes.\nPaying these signed whole-future pairings from the initialized margins\nis still open. The identity does not infer nonzero terminal value from\nnonzero past activity.\n\nAn absorbing retired region DOES exist:\n\n    C_ret={x: r_i<=0 for i<=k, z_i<=0 for i<k}.             (34)\n\nIf the actual suffix enters it, every retired projection is zero.\nThe only remaining scalar gradient acts on x_(k+1), and its excess\nstays >=delta, so output isolation continues and (34) is preserved.\nThis proves conditional no-reactivation AFTER entrance, not entrance.\n\nAttempt B1 sought entrance using the own-checkpoint margins (30).\nThose margins, even supplemented by earlier negative checkpoint margins,\ndo not constitute an invariant for all states satisfying them.\nThe following exact test fixes that scope.\n\nTake r_(i-1)=-beta*D_i, the own pair in (7), and still-future coordinates\nzero. This is an ALTERNATIVE simultaneous state, not an assertion about\ntheir later actual values. For i=2 it respects the permanent first bound.\nThen\n\n    z_(i-1)=beta*D_(i+1)-beta^2*D_i-ell_(i+1)/beta.\n\nIf this is >=kappa*sigma_(i-1), (18) gives the explicitly NONZERO\nincoming Q_(i-1)=sigma_(i-1). These conditions hold eventually:\nD_(i+1)/D_i is bounded away from0, beta=1/k, and\nell_(i+1)/beta and sigma_(i-1) are exponentially smaller than beta*D_i,\nby (10)--(13). The own component has P_i=Q_i=0.\nIts next component starts its next local block and has\nP_(i+1)=C*sigma_(i+1), Q_(i+1)=0 eventually, since the large local\nu_(i+1) satisfies the capped endpoint inequality.\n\nUse the physical step h=b_(J+i-2), which occurs in the next complete\nH_l_i. Since h*sigma_(i-1)/2=D_i+sigma_(i-1),\n\n    r_i^+=-beta*D_(i+1)+D_i+sigma_(i-1)>0                 (35)\n\nwhenever beta*T_i/M_i<1. Also h*C<2M_(i+1) ensures its output\nremains above ell_(i+1), so its new value is a positive horizontal Huber.\nBoth finite conditions hold eventually by (10)--(13).\n\nTherefore the checkpoint-margin-only invariant fails under an available\nphysical step. The test does NOT refute retirement of the ACTUAL\ninitialized trajectory: the state at this later occurrence of h must be\nproved reachable, and no such assertion is made. Its specific lesson is\nthat the actual incoming Q_(i-1), including its timing in (31), cannot\nbe discarded.\n\nAttempt B2 used telescoping tail-coordinate weights. Define\n\n    Z_i(x)=sum_(j=i)^(k+1) beta^(j-i)*x_j.\n\nThe full update, including Q_k before the root and the terminal scalar,\ngives exactly\n\n    Z_i^+=Z_i-(h/2)[sum_(j=i)^(k+1)beta^(j-i)U_j-Q_(i-1)].\n    Z_i(t_i)=A_i+beta*ell_(i+1).                           (36)\n\nThe two D_(i+1) terms cancel in this INITIAL value, not by a reset.\nFor i=1 this weighted sum is nonincreasing. For every middle i it\nretains the actual incoming source in (36), including all nonzero values.\nMoreover a bound on Z_i alone does not bound positive r_i or z_i:\nnegative downstream coordinates can offset them. The cancelling adjacent\nweight ratio is beta, whereas the scale ratios sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i\ntend to lambda-2. A comparison between them loses factors of order\n((lambda-2)/beta)^O(k) and does not pay the benchmark normalization.\n\nThe scoped unpaid initialized interface is now explicit: the signed\nintegrals in (33) up to q_root, their continuation through (31), and\nentrance into (34). Accepted 5fd396eba75651e7 supplies, on the current\nenclosing-m word at tau=1/4 and every m>=1536,\n\n    B_(2^m-1)*H_base(H_(m,1/4))<237/250.                 (1)\n\nOnly this scoped Huber comparison is used from the spiral fact. No primitive\nhistory, negative minimax resolution, seed existence or discarded-prefix\nclaim is assumed.\n\nThe dispatch-specified shared drafts 687befe50820df21, 6e098f91c4a13444,\n22347ffeb25f8830, d4ae45931cfc72a3, 97f52e264069f3ac,\n8c09ef072ab9d6f9 and 5f198236d1ff200e were recalled from global memory.\nThey are UNVERIFIED awareness, not correctness premises. The first normal\nand Jordan obstructions use c=13/5 and a=c^-17 or c^-18 with NO added1\nin their bridge steps. The later near-unit Newton draft uses its own\ntau=1 enclosing family. Those are not the current tau1/4 word.\n\nThe new controlling object is a SINGLE static convex C1 function with a\nfinite polyhedral partition. Its actual gradient changes according to\ninequalities evaluated at the actual current point. Regional Hessians can\nfail to commute, and the GD updates form an ordered operator word whose\nletters are generated by these inequalities. No Hessian is assigned freely.\nThe constructions below all have explicit feedback or a reverse-side\nreaction; none transports mass outward through disjoint tree generations.\n\nFor comparison, shared 89154918c70588a0/62e1d3a5b912ecbb studied a fixed\ntriangle-plane identity decomposition. Shared49c229ff9ff6eb2c/\n0458dd4dceadebde studied outward64-ary Huber transport with pure-generation\ndilution. Here there is no triangle incidence identity and no generation\nsplitting. The two oblique normals can return activity to an earlier side;\nthe third family uses coupled metric projection. Their failures are derived\nagain below, without using those unverified claims as lemmas.\n\n## 2. Uniform notation, diagnostic word, and normalization\n\nAll spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces. An operator norm\nis the induced Euclidean norm. Positive part is z_+=max(z,0), and\nnegative part is z_-=max(-z,0). The superscript or subscript66 identifies\nnew local notation, distinct from the registered benchmark symbols.\n\nFix an integer m>=3 and 0<theta_66<=1/4. Put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_66=1-theta_66/m,\n    c_66=lambda*(1+theta_66/m), a_66=beta_66/c_66,\n    H^66_0=(), H^66_(j+1)=(H^66_j,b^66_j,H^66_j),\n    b^66_j=1+a_66*c_66^j, n_m=2^m-1,\n    S^66_m=sum H^66_m.\n\nEvery child uses this SAME enclosing (m,theta_66). Thus H^66_m is precisely\nthe current H_(m,tau) with tau=theta_66. No recursive child resets m.\nThe parameter theta_66 is unrelated to the project's Huber threshold tau.\nWrite A_66=b^66_0, B_66=b^66_1, C_66=b^66_2. The first four physical\nsteps of H^66_m are exactly (A_66,B_66,A_66,C_66).\n\nThe following rational bounds will be used, without numerical evaluation:\n\n    4/3<A_66<sqrt(2)<17/12<3/2,\n    23/12<=B_66<2, 3<C_66<4.                          (2)\n\nIndeed beta_66>=11/12, c_66<=13lambda/12, and lambda<5/2 imply\na_66>=11/(13lambda)>22/65>1/3. Also\na_66<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1. Further B_66=1+beta_66, and\nC_66=1+lambda*(1-theta_66^2/m^2). Since lambda>12/5 and\ntheta_66^2/m^2<=1/144, its added term is at least143/60>2 and is\nless than lambda<5/2. The square of17/12 exceeds2, giving its bound\non sqrt(2). This establishes (2).\n\nFor a function G with minimum0 and a specified initial point outside its\nminimum set define\n\n    E_66(G,h)=2G(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin G)^2,\n    x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t*grad G(x_t).                       (3)\n\nThe advertised smoothness is1. For every L>0 use f=L*G and the original\nphysical steps h_t/L. The iterates, true radius and normalized payoff are\nunchanged. Every function below is static before this run; its dependence\non the known word is permitted by the original minimax quantifiers.\n\n## 3. A complete scalar-to-field identity paying ALL directed rows\n\nFor 0<=eta<=1 define the single scalar C1 piecewise quadratic\n\n    chi_eta(z)=(z_+^2+eta*z_-^2)/2,\n    ell_eta(z)=chi_eta'(z)=z if z>=0, eta*z if z<=0.\n\nThese two definitions agree at0. The derivative is nondecreasing and\n1-Lipschitz, so chi_eta is convex and C1. For any real s,t define\n\n    J_eta(s,t)=chi_eta(s)-chi_eta(t)-ell_eta(t)*(s-t)\n                         -(ell_eta(s)-ell_eta(t))^2/2.\n\nHere is the exact exhaustive sign calculation:\n\n    s,t>=0: J_eta=0;\n    s,t<=0: J_eta=eta*(1-eta)*(s-t)^2/2;\n    s>=0>=t: J_eta=eta*(1-eta)*t^2/2;\n    s<=0<=t: J_eta=(1-eta)*(eta*s^2/2-s*t).             (4)\n\nAll four quantities are nonnegative. Endpoints satisfy adjacent formulas.\nNo scalar interpolation theorem is being imported.\n\nLet T_66 be any real matrix with ||T_66||<=1, let d_66^aff be any fixed\noffset vector, and define G(x)=sum_l chi_eta((T_66*x-d_66^aff)_l).\nPut z_x=T_66*x-d_66^aff and v_x=(ell_eta((z_x)_l))_l. Then\ngrad G(x)=T_66^T*v_x, and\n\n    ||grad G(x)-grad G(y)||<=||v_x-v_y||\n                         <=||T_66*(x-y)||<=||x-y||.\n\nThis proves global1-smoothness, including all partition boundaries. On an\nopen sign cell the Hessian is T_66^T*D*T_66, where the diagonal entries\nof D are1 or eta. Consequently0<=Hessian<=I. Convexity also follows\ndirectly from convex chi_eta composed with affine forms.\n\nFor ANY x,y, in the original directed orientation of1252b8823bb5a290,\n\n    Q^66_xy=G(x)-G(y)+<grad G(x),y-x>\n                          +||grad G(x)-grad G(y)||^2/2\n     =-sum_l J_eta((z_y)_l,(z_x)_l)\n       -(||v_y-v_x||^2-||T_66^T*(v_y-v_x)||^2)/2<=0.   (5)\n\nThis identity pays every ordered pair on the SAME function. In particular\nit pays pairs of remote queries, pairs in either orientation at a zero\nstep, every unqueried terminal pair, and every pair with ANY minimizer.\nThe Gram matrix of any finite actual sample collection is PSD because its\nvectors are actual Euclidean vectors. There is no expected-only or\ncomponentwise replacement for joint legality.\n\nFor the two fields below take, in R^2,\n\n    u_66=(1,0), v_66=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), r_66=1/2,\n    w_66=2/3=1/(1+r_66),\n    p_66(x)=<u_66,x>, q_66(x)=<v_66,x>,\n    U_66=u_66*u_66^T, V_66=v_66*v_66^T,\n    T_66*x=sqrt(w_66)*(p_66(x),q_66(x)).\n\nThe Gram eigenvalues of the two unit normals are1-r_66 and1+r_66;\ntherefore ||T_66||^2=w_66*(1+r_66)=1. Set d_66^aff=0. The resulting\nstatic field is\n\n    F^66_eta(x)=w_66/2*[p_66(x)_+^2+q_66(x)_+^2\n                         +eta*p_66(x)_-^2+eta*q_66(x)_-^2]. (6)\n\nThis is a finite sum of FOUR fixed hinge squares when eta>0 and of TWO\nwhen eta=0. All normals, offsets0 and nonnegative weights are explicit.\nBecause chi_eta is homogeneous of degree2, (6) equals the G in (5).\nThe gradient is w_66[ell_eta(p_66)*u_66+ell_eta(q_66)*v_66].\nIts open-cell Hessians are\n\n    A^66_++=w_66*(U_66+V_66),\n    A^66_+-=w_66*(U_66+eta*V_66),\n    A^66_-+=w_66*(eta*U_66+V_66),\n    A^66_--=eta*w_66*(U_66+V_66).                     (7)\n\nThe actual region is determined by the current signs of p_66,q_66.\nAll first two fields' updates are EXACTLY x_+=(I-h*A^66_sign(x))*x.\nSince [U_66,V_66]=-r_66*(u_66*v_66^T-v_66*u_66^T)!=0,\n\n    [A^66_+-,A^66_-+]=w_66^2*(1-eta^2)*[U_66,V_66]!=0\n\nwhen eta<1. The two regions (++ and --) are nonempty; for (++), use\nu_66+v_66. On this open region A^66_++ has eigenvalue1. Thus the\nleast global Lipschitz gradient constant of each (6) is exactly1,\nnot a loose trace normalization. For eta>0 the Hessians are also at\nleast (eta/3)*I, although this lower bound is not used for the payoff.\n\n## 4. Screen1: obtuse two-hinge activation feedback, then exact absorption\n\nFreeze eta=0 in (6) and start from x_0=u_66. The complete minimum set is\n\n    K_66={x:p_66(x)<=0, q_66(x)<=0}, min F^66_0=0.     (8)\n\nIt contains0. For z in K_66, <u_66,z><=0, so\n||u_66-z||^2=1+||z||^2-2<u_66,z>>=1. Equality holds only atz=0.\nThus the closest minimizer is0 and the true initial radius is exactly1.\n\nPut d_66=1-2A_66/3, e_66=2B_66/3-1, t_66=d_66*e_66. By (2),\n\n    d_66>1/18, e_66>=5/18, t_66>5/324.               (9)\n\nThe first four initialized updates, derived from their actual signs, are\n\n    x_1=d_66*u_66,\n    x_2=-t_66*u_66,\n    x_3=-t_66*(u_66+(A_66/3)*v_66),\n    x_4=-t_66*(u_66+((A_66+C_66*d_66)/3)*v_66).       (10)\n\nIndeed at x_0,x_1 one has p_66>0,q_66<0, so the gradient is\n(2/3)*p_66*u_66. At x_2, p_66=-t_66<0 and q_66=t_66/2>0,\nso the gradient is (t_66/3)*v_66. At x_3,\n\n    p_66=-t_66*(1-A_66/6)<0,\n    q_66=t_66*d_66/2>0,\n\ngiving the fourth line of (10). At its end,\n\n    p_66(x_4)=-t_66*[1-(A_66+C_66*d_66)/6]<0,\n    q_66(x_4)=t_66*d_66*(1-2C_66/3)/2<0.            (11)\n\nFor the first sign, A_66<3/2, C_66<4 and0<d_66<1 give\nA_66+C_66*d_66<11/2<6. For the second, C_66>3. This proves\nthe complete reached sequence of open regions\n\n    (+,-), (+,-), (-,+), (-,+), (-,-).\n\nThe ordered product through x_4 is\n\n    (I-C_66*w_66*V_66)*(I-A_66*w_66*V_66)\n          *(I-B_66*w_66*U_66)*(I-A_66*w_66*U_66).    (12)\n\nThe U/V factors do not commute. These letters came from (10)--(11),\nnot from a postulated matrix word. This is real backward activity:\novershooting the u side activates the opposing oblique v side.\nBut x_4 is in the INTERIOR of the complete minimum cone. Every subsequent\ngradient is exactly0, so EVERY remaining original step is executed at\nthe same point. The terminal objective is exactly0 for every m>=3.\n\nThe all-row identity (5), including (8)'s minimizers and terminalx_4,\nis explicit. This is a legal, sharply1-smooth, actually noncommuting field,\nbut an exact failed screen: E_66(F^66_0,H^66_m)=0. This conclusion applies\nto THIS initialization and THESE normals/weights/offsets, not to all\none-sided hinges or every initial point in this field.\n\n## 5. Screen2: weak reverse-side curvature prevents zero payoff but confines it\n\nFreeze, before the run, the explicit word-dependent number\n\n    eta_66=1/[100000*(1+S^66_m)]                       (13)\n\nin (6), and keep x_0=u_66. This permits the previously inactive side to\nreact, so it is a concrete change to the pure two-hinge law. Every term is\nnonnegative, eta_66>0, and u_66,v_66 spanR^2. Thus the complete minimum\nset is {0}, its attained value is0, and the true initial radius is1.\nEquations (4)--(7) prove sharp global1-smoothness and ALL original rows.\n\nThe following initialization argument proves the switches rather than\nassuming them persist under a perturbation. For either field (6), the\npiecewise-affine continuous gradient along a line segment is the integral\nof symmetric Hessians between0 andI. Segments lying in a boundary are\nhandled by splitting the finitely many affine scalar forms, whose slope\ncan be chosen on the agreeing boundary; the gradient remains continuous.\nConsequently the GD map has global Lipschitz constant at most\n\n    k_h=max(1,|1-h|) for h>=0,                         (14)\n\nbecause its difference at two points is (I-h*Abar)*(x-y), where\n0<=Abar<=I. Both maps fix0. Further\n\n    ||grad F^66_eta(x)-grad F^66_0(x)||<=eta*||x||.     (15)\n\nTo see (15), write the difference as eta*T_66^T times the negative\ncoordinates of T_66*x, retaining their signed values, and use ||T_66||=1.\n\nLet x_t^eta be the actual trajectory for (13) and x_t^0 the trajectory\n(10). These are two fixed functions, not a time-varying blend. Since\nA_66,B_66,A_66<2, (14) gives ||x_t^eta||<=1 for0<=t<=3.\nComparison using the F_0 map and (15) yields, successively,\n\n    ||x_1^eta-x_1^0||<(3/2)*eta_66,\n    ||x_2^eta-x_2^0||<(7/2)*eta_66,\n    ||x_3^eta-x_3^0||<5*eta_66,\n    ||x_4^eta-x_4^0||<19*eta_66, ||x_4^eta||<3.       (16)\n\nThe fourth estimate uses k_(C_66)<3, C_66<4 and\n||x_3^eta||<=1. No recurrence or trajectory was executed by a program.\n\nEvery absolute residual p_66 or q_66 at x_t^0,1<=t<=4, is greater\nthan1/2400. For t=1,2 this follows from (9) and residuals\n(d_66,-d_66/2), (-t_66,t_66/2). For t=3 the smaller relevant residual\nis q_66=t_66*d_66/2>5/11664>1/2400, and\n|p_66|>3t_66/4. For t=4, (11) gives\n|p_66|>t_66/12>5/3888 and\n|q_66|>t_66*d_66/2>5/11664. These are exact rational comparisons.\nBoth normals have norm1; (13),(16) and19/100000<1/2400 prove that\nthe actual perturbed signs at every one of these points agree with those\nin Screen1. In particular x_4^eta is in the interior of K_66.\n\nThus the ACTUAL first ordered product is, with the matrices from (7)\nevaluated at eta_66,\n\n    x_4^eta=(I-C_66*A^66_-+)*(I-A_66*A^66_-+)\n                 *(I-B_66*A^66_+-)*(I-A_66*A^66_+-)*u_66. (17)\n\nIt is genuinely noncommutative. The field has positive curvature and\ngradient in the (--)-region, so that region is no longer a minimum cone.\nIts continued chronology must still be proved. Inside K_66 the residual\nupdate under any remaining original step h is\n\n    (p_66(x_+),q_66(x_+))^T\n      =[[1-eta_66*w_66*h, eta_66*w_66*r_66*h],\n        [eta_66*w_66*r_66*h, 1-eta_66*w_66*h]]\n                                  *(p_66(x),q_66(x))^T. (18)\n\nEach h<=S^66_m and (13) gives eta_66*w_66*h<1. Every matrix entry\nin (18) is nonnegative, and its diagonal entries are strictly positive.\nHence BOTH residuals remain strictly negative forever. No positive-side\nhinge reactivates. Every remaining operator is exactly\nI-h*eta_66*A^66_++, and\n\n    x_(n_m)^eta=product_(t=4)^(n_m-1)(I-h_t*eta_66*A^66_++)*x_4^eta. (19)\n\nThis tail product commutes because its confinement was proved. Its\neigenvalues are1-h*eta_66 and1-h*eta_66/3, both in(0,1]; thus\n||x_(n_m)^eta||<3. The EXACT terminal value in this reached region is\n\n    2F^66_eta66(x_(n_m)^eta)\n          =eta_66*<x_(n_m)^eta,A^66_++*x_(n_m)^eta>.\n\nIt is strictly positive and at most9*eta_66, so\n\n    E_66(F^66_eta66,H^66_m)<9/[100000*(1+S^66_m)],\n    E_66/H_base(H^66_m)<18/100000=9/50000.             (20)\n\nFor theta_66=1/4 and EVERY m>=1536, (1) gives the original benchmark\ncomparison E_66/U_(n_m)^ZJ<(9/50000)*(237/250)<1/5000.\nThere is no omitted true-radius factor. This rejects the specific weak\nreverse-curvature law early and uniformly. It DOES NOT exclude larger\nreverse weights, other initializations, affine offsets, different normal\nangles or fields that reactivate their strong-curvature side.\n\n## 6. Screen3: a polyhedral squared-distance field with actual repeated feedback\n\nHere use new normals with a sharper metric coupling:\n\n    r_66^D=sqrt(15)/4, delta_66^D=1-(r_66^D)^2=1/16,\n    u_66^D=(1,0), v_66^D=(-r_66^D,1/4),\n    U_66^D=u_66^D*(u_66^D)^T, V_66^D=v_66^D*(v_66^D)^T,\n    p_66^D(x)=<u_66^D,x>, q_66^D(x)=<v_66^D,x>,\n    K_66^D={x:p_66^D(x)<=0,q_66^D(x)<=0},\n    D_66(x)=dist(x,K_66^D)^2/2.                       (21)\n\nThis is ONE static globally C1 convex piecewise-quadratic function, not\na sum of independently chosen face energies. To remove boundary ambiguity\nin the initialized Hessians, freeze epsilon_66^D=1/100, put\n\n    k_66^D=-epsilon_66^D*(v_66^D+r_66^D*u_66^D),\n    x_0^D=u_66^D+k_66^D.                              (22)\n\nThen k_66^D is in K_66^D, p_66^D(k_66^D)=0 and\nq_66^D(k_66^D)=-epsilon_66^D*delta_66^D<0. For z in K_66^D,\n<u_66^D,z-k_66^D><=0, giving\n||x_0^D-z||^2>=||u_66^D||^2=1, with equality atz=k_66^D.\nThe actual closest initial radius is therefore1, although ||x_0^D||>1.\nThe complete minimum set is K_66^D and its attained value is0.\n\n### 6.1 The complete static regions and update guards\n\nFor P_D=p_66^D(x), Q_D=q_66^D(x), r_D=r_66^D, the exhaustive region law is\n\n    K: P_D<=0,Q_D<=0                     => grad D_66=0, D_66=0;\n    U: P_D>=0,Q_D+r_D*P_D<=0             => grad D_66=P_D*u_66^D, D_66=P_D^2/2;\n    V: Q_D>=0,P_D+r_D*Q_D<=0             => grad D_66=Q_D*v_66^D, D_66=Q_D^2/2;\n    O: P_D+r_D*Q_D>=0,Q_D+r_D*P_D>=0     => grad D_66=x, D_66=||x||^2/2. (23)\n\nOn overlaps the value and gradient formulas agree. In regionU,\nx-P_D*u_66^D lies in K_66^D and P_D*u_66^D is an outward normal there;\nsimilarly for V. In regionO,\n\n    x=[(P_D+r_D*Q_D)/(1-r_D^2)]*u_66^D\n                     +[(Q_D+r_D*P_D)/(1-r_D^2)]*v_66^D\n\nis in the nonnegative normal cone at0, so its closest point is0.\nThese conditions partition the plane: if neither K nor a feasible\none-wall projection applies, both displayed coefficients are nonnegative.\nThus (23) is an exact projection law. The open-cell Hessians are respectively\n0,U_66^D,V_66^D,I. These have norm at most1, and the nonempty open O\nregion makes the least Lipschitz gradient constant exactly1.\nThe actual next iterate is (I-h*A^D_66(x))*x, with A chosen by (23).\nThis guard, evaluated at every actual state, specifies the entire later\nchronology; no later face sequence is being prescribed independently.\n\n### 6.2 Initialized U-to-V-to-U-to-V feedback is actually reached\n\nIn this paragraph use r_D=r_66^D, delta_D=delta_66^D,\nepsilon_D=epsilon_66^D, P_(D,t)=p_66^D(x_t^D), Q_(D,t)=q_66^D(x_t^D).\nPut alpha=A_66-1>1/3 and F_66^D=B_66*r_D^2-1>=51/64.\nAt x_0^D, P_(D,0)=1 and Q_(D,0)+r_D*P_(D,0)=-epsilon_D*delta_D<0,\nso it is in OPEN U.\nAt x_1^D=(I-A_66*U_66^D)*x_0^D,\n\n    P_(D,1)=-alpha, Q_(D,1)=r_D*alpha-epsilon_D*delta_D>0,\n    P_(D,1)+r_D*Q_(D,1)=-delta_D*(alpha+r_D*epsilon_D)<0.\n\nThe next query is therefore in OPEN V. The second update gives\n\n    P_(D,2)=alpha*F_66^D-B_66*r_D*epsilon_D*delta_D>0,\n    Q_(D,2)=(1-B_66)*Q_(D,1)<0,\n    Q_(D,2)+r_D*P_(D,2)\n       =-delta_D*[B_66*r_D*alpha+epsilon_D*(1-B_66*delta_D)]<0.\n\nFor its strict positive residual, P_(D,2)>17/64-2/1600>0. It is in OPEN U.\nThe third update gives\n\n    P_(D,3)=(1-A_66)*P_(D,2)<0,\n    Q_(D,3)=r_D*alpha*[A_66*F_66^D-(B_66-1)]\n          -epsilon_D*delta_D*[A_66*B_66*r_D^2-(B_66-1)]>0,\n    P_(D,3)+r_D*Q_(D,3)\n       =-delta_D*[alpha*(1+A_66*F_66^D)\n                      +r_D*epsilon_D*(1-A_66*B_66*delta_D)]<0. (24)\n\nTo check Q_(D,3)>0 completely, the expression\nA*(15B/16-1)-(B-1) increases in both A and B on (2)'s rectangle.\nAt A=4/3,B=23/12 its value is7/48. Since r_D>1/2 and alpha>1/3,\nthe first term in Q_(D,3) is greater than7/288. The subtracted term is\nless than3/1600, which is smaller than7/288. Also\nA_66*B_66*delta_D<3/16<1, proving the last sign in (24).\nThe query x_3^D is in OPEN V. Thus the actually reached first three\nupdate factors are\n\n    x_3^D=(I-A_66*U_66^D)*(I-B_66*V_66^D)\n                         *(I-A_66*U_66^D)*x_0^D.     (25)\n\nHere [U_66^D,V_66^D]=-r_D*(u_66^D*(v_66^D)^T-v_66^D*(u_66^D)^T)!=0.\nThis family really returns to an earlier active wall, instead of being\na relabeled outward graph. Later queries use all four guards (23).\nThe following bound applies to every possible guard-generated later\nhistory, so a complete list of its later faces is not needed to reject it.\n\n### 6.3 Global1-smoothness and exact ALL-directed legality, without an external premise\n\nThe argument works for ANY nonempty closed convex set K in any finite\nEuclidean dimension, with D(x)=dist(x,K)^2/2. Write P_x for its metric\nprojection and R_x=x-P_x. Existence follows by minimizing the continuous\ncoercive squared distance on the closed set; strict convexity along the\nsegment between two distinct candidates gives uniqueness. Differentiating\nthe squared distance along P_x+t*(z-P_x) at t=0 gives\n\n    <R_x,z-P_x><=0 for EVERY z in K.                  (26)\n\nThe two projection inequalities at x,y give\n<R_x-R_y,P_x-P_y>>=0. Since x-y=(P_x-P_y)+(R_x-R_y),\n\n    <R_x-R_y,x-y>>=||R_x-R_y||^2,\n    ||R_x-R_y||<=||x-y||.                             (27)\n\nConvex combinations of P_x,P_y are in K, and convexity of squared norm\nshows D is convex. Evaluating the squared distance to P_x at x+d gives\nD(x+d)<=D(x)+<R_x,d>+||d||^2/2. Direct expansion gives\n\n    D(y)-D(x)-<R_x,y-x>\n           =||R_y-R_x||^2/2-<R_x,P_y-P_x>>=0.        (28)\n\nThese lower and upper remainder bounds prove differentiability with\ngrad D(x)=R_x; (27) proves global1-Lipschitz gradient. The entire original\nordered interpolation residual is the EXACT expression\n\n    D(x)-D(y)+<R_x,y-x>+||R_x-R_y||^2/2\n                             =<R_x,P_y-P_x><=0.      (29)\n\nEquation (26) proves its sign for all x,y, including every remote query,\nthe actual terminal and any minimizer. The complete Gram is again actual\nEuclidean data. If y is any minimizer, P_y=y and R_y=0; the opposite\nminimizer-to-query residual is exactly0. Nothing is paid only in expectation.\n\n### 6.4 A dimension-free exact product bound rejects this WHOLE unscaled family\n\nFor any nonnegative step h and current pointx,\n\n    x_+=x-h*R_x=P_x+(1-h)*R_x,\n    dist(x_+,K)<=|1-h|*dist(x,K).                    (30)\n\nThe comparison point P_x is in K. This proves (30) even when the update\ncrosses arbitrarily many faces, and even for h>2 or h=0. (31)\n\nFor the last equality, levelj has multiplicity2^(m-j-1), the total\ncount is n_m, and the sum of j times these multiplicities is n_m-m.\nBoth sums follow inductively from the actual recursive word. Since\n|1-b_j^66|=a_66*c_66^j, their product is\na_66^n_m*c_66^(n_m-m)=beta_66^n_m*c_66^-m. These are symbolic\nidentities; the word and product were not executed by a program.\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3).\nSince n_m<2^m and0<beta_66<1, (31) gives uniformly\n\n    B_(n_m)*E_66(D,H^66_m)\n       <=(1+4*3^m)*c_66^(-2m)\n       <=5*(3/lambda^2)^m ->0,                      (32)\n\nbecause lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)>3. This bound is uniform in the dimension,\nthe nonempty closed convex set, its offsets and facial angles, the initial\npoint outside it, and0<theta_66<=1/4. In particular it covers every\nunscaled polyhedral squared-distance C1 field of the assigned type.\nIncreasing the number of faces, using overlapping real incidence geometry,\nor increasing actual Hessian noncommutation cannot defeat (30).\n\nEven the full SAME-word Huber reference dominates asymptotically. The exact\nsum identity is\n\n    S^66_m=n_m+a_66*(c_66^m-2^m)/(c_66-2)<2*c_66^m,\n\nbecause c_66>lambda>2 and\na_66/(c_66-2)<(1/lambda)/(lambda-2)=1. Thus\nE_66/H_base<5*beta_66^(2n_m)*c_66^-m<=5*c_66^-m->0.\n\nThe exclusion is for D=dist^2/2 at advertised smoothness1. A horizon-tuned\nsmaller scalar multiple, a Moreau envelope of a non-indicator function,\ngeneral sums of unequal-distance penalties or affine hinge feedback are\noutside (30)'s hypotheses. Physical words and zero-step repetitions\n\nNormalize L=1. The full given physical word is\n\n    h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0, S=sum_(t<n)h_t. Define\n\n    j(i)=number of positive physical steps strictly before i,\n                                             0<=i<=n. (2)\n\nEvery displayed compressed jet with index r is lifted to original\nsample i by r=j(i). This is an ACTUAL witness-array convention: zero\nsteps repeat the complete point, gradient and value. It imposes no\nequality between formal gradient coordinates in an upper certificate.\nAll original ordered pairs, including all repeated queries, are kept.\nThere is no deletion of a positive physical step or a terminal row.\n\nFor k<2 there is no paired memory.
